Mesa Housing — reviewed-with-selections

AI evaluation only. Not office-reviewed or agency-verified. Not a production import.

101 program proposals from 104 assessed leads.

Suggested starter set

Ten complementary usable starters spanning distinct pathways: statewide navigation (211), the single-adult and family coordinated-entry front doors, Mesa's shelter for families/women/seniors, Mesa's men's shelter, Mesa's rent/utility crisis aid, the rental-subsidy rehousing route, the East Valley DV shelter, the 12-17 youth crisis line, and the veterans' walk-in access point. The set deliberately mixes service types - navigation, entry/assessment, shelter, financial aid, rehousing, safety, youth, veterans - rather than several variants of one service, and each limitation is stated so missionaries can set expectations before calling. All needs-resolution records (Helaman House, SAFEDVS, the tracker, UMOM youth crisis/ILP, NREC, Dismas, the Resource Village, Mesa I-HELP) remain outside the starter set with administrator-focused reasons, and reserve usable resources (deposit help, the Mesa Resource Line, second DV shelters, Vista Colina, HUD-VASH, AHP/H2O, DDD lanes, additional shelter capacity) carry considerations written against this actual set. No tiers or reserve ranks are introduced.

211 Arizona Helpline (2-1-1) — Statewide one-number front door: live specialists 9 a.m.-7 p.m. daily, a Housing Crisis Hotline, and shelter/housing/rent/utility referrals with Spanish-language support. Referral-only - it provides no beds or funds and may not know same-day shelter availability.

Brian Garcia Welcome Center – single adult coordinated entry (Keys to Change) — The single-adult coordinated entry front door - 24/7 walk-in assessment, diversion and shelter/housing referrals for adults across Maricopa County. Central Phoenix walk-in; no placement guarantee; phone variants in older listings (use 602-883-4509 / 602-229-5155); Spanish-language service not confirmed.

Family Housing Hub — family coordinated entry and shelter referrals (UMOM New Day Centers) — The countywide family coordinated entry and shelter waitlist - the front door for families with children seeking shelter and housing programs. Long waits (200+ families, about three months reported) and inconsistent posted hours - call before visiting.

City of Mesa - Office of Homeless Solutions · Off the Streets emergency shelter (Windermere Hotel site) — Mesa's main emergency shelter for families, women, seniors and DV survivors - hotel-based, 65 rooms/111 beds, with meals, 24/7 staffing, case management and housing plans. Referral-only (no walk-ups) via 480-644-HOPE; about a six-week wait reported and the operator transition to Mercy House - confirm current status.

A New Leaf · East Valley Men's Center (EVMC) — Mesa's 104-bed men's emergency shelter with a defined intake path (online pre-screen plus the (877) 211-8661 placement hotline). No phone intakes; beds via waitlist and availability can change daily; children under 18 are not allowed on site.

A New Leaf · MesaCAN Rent and Utility Assistance — Mesa's rent/utility crisis window with a Spanish application path, documented criteria and a clear document list. Mesa residents only; first-come funding with narrow appointment windows (first-Thursday rent line, limited slots).

A New Leaf · Rapid ReHousing (rental subsidy and case management) — The rental-subsidy rehousing route (up to 12 months plus move-in help and case management) that shows clients what coordinated entry can lead to beyond shelter. No direct applications - entry only through coordinated entry (211); funding and timing vary.

A New Leaf · Autumn House Domestic Violence Shelter (East Valley/Mesa) — The East Valley's local DV shelter - 22 beds, any gender, pet-friendly, screened through A New Leaf's 24-hour hotline. Confidential location; placement not guaranteed; the May 2026 countywide DV hotline transition means confirming the current intake route.

Safe Place — Youth Crisis Entry (Ages 12–17), Maricopa County — The 12-17 crisis entry - call, text or walk into Safe Place sites (QuikTrip, Valley Metro, libraries, fire stations) for immediate youth help countywide. Overnight shelter not guaranteed; use the current 44357 text instructions (older guides show 69866).

VA Community Resource & Referral Center (CRRC) – Phoenix (veteran housing and coordinated entry) — The veterans' walk-in front door - housing assessments and referrals (transitional, permanent, eviction prevention), the HUD-VASH pathway, benefits and health care, free of charge. Weekdays 7:30 a.m.-4 p.m.; central Phoenix; referrals depend on capacity - arrive early for same-day referrals.

Gaps: No confirmed emergency shelter capacity for young adults 18-24 in Mesa/East Valley: the UMOM youth records are unconfirmed or historical (operator transferred to Child Crisis Arizona), Covenant House has no Arizona site, and only Safe Place (12-17) plus outreach/coordinated-entry points (NAC; UMOM youth outreach) are verified.; No general hotel/motel voucher program and no safe-parking pathway: Red Cross assistance is disaster-triggered, UMOM heat relief is seasonal and referral-based, and hotel-based shelters (Off the Streets, Sue's Espacio, Rio Fresco) are referral-only.; Reentry housing: no confirmed community-referral reentry beds in Phoenix (Dismas' Phoenix facility unconfirmed); H2O and HUD-VASH touch reentry but require health-plan or VA routes.; No open voucher waitlists captured: Mesa HCV, HAMC, Tempe and Phoenix lists are closed or referral-only - no immediate Section 8 application pathway.; Mesa I-HELP rotating shelter unresolved - no confirmed rotating-shelter intake for Mesa adults (operator largely resolved to LSS-SW serving adult women, current operation unconfirmed).; Eviction/tenant legal defense is not represented as a resource (AZEvictionHelp.org appears only as a referral mention).; East Valley single-women capacity: no confirmed Mesa-area beds for women without children; Halle Women's Center (Phoenix) and DV shelters are the closest options.; No confirmed women-veterans-specific beds in Maricopa County (MANA House described for male veterans; Ashley's Place within U.S.VETS noted but not separately verified).

211 Arizona Helpline (2-1-1)

b01-211-arizona · usable

Statewide information-and-referral helpline for Arizona (a program of Solari) that connects people in Mesa and Maricopa County with shelter and housing options, rent and utility assistance, food, and other basic-needs programs. Live specialists help callers plan their next steps, and 211 operates a Housing Crisis Hotline for people who are experiencing homelessness or are at risk of it.

**Services Offered**
Live help finding and connecting to shelter and housing options, rent and utility assistance, food programs, employment and education resources, veterans services, health coverage application help (COVER Arizona), and other basic-needs programs. A Housing Crisis Hotline supports people who are experiencing homelessness or at risk of becoming homeless, and call specialists route callers to shelter intake processes (including family shelter intake through the Family Housing Hub).

**Eligibility Requirements**
Open to anyone in Arizona who needs help finding services - no income test or referral is needed to call. The individual programs 211 refers to set their own eligibility rules (for example, shelter intake screenings).

**Population Served**
Statewide, including Mesa and Maricopa County; individuals and families. Live help is available in English and Spanish, with real-time interpreter services for other languages.

**How to Best Connect**
Dial 2-1-1 from any phone in Arizona, or 877-211-8661 from anywhere. For a Spanish-speaking representative, choose option 2 after dialing. Live operators are available 9:00 a.m. to 7:00 p.m., seven days a week. Search the online directory any time at search.211arizona.org, or email through 211arizona.org. Relay callers can dial 7-1-1 and ask for 877-211-8661.

**Important Information to Know**
211 is a free referral and navigation line, not a housing provider - it does not itself offer beds or pay rent, and it may not know same-day shelter bed status. Ask specifically about the Housing Crisis Hotline and about the programs you are trying to reach.

phone: 2-1-1 in Arizona; 877-211-8661 from anywhere; Relay users: 7-1-1 or 800-367-8939 and ask for 877-211-8661

website: https://211arizona.org/

hours: Live specialists 9:00 a.m. to 7:00 p.m., 7 days a week; online directory available anytime.

resolutionReason: Official statewide helpline; the current site confirms identity, housing and shelter referral service including a Housing Crisis Hotline, statewide reach with Mesa/Maricopa coverage, 9 a.m. to 7 p.m. daily hours, call/online/email access, and Spanish-language live support. It directly assists navigation, so it is retained as usable.

Sources: 211 Arizona - statewide information and referral service (Solari)

Consider this: Starter: Arizona's one-number front door for shelter, housing and rent/utility referrals, with Spanish-language live help.

Identity judgment: Statewide referral helpline; distinct from local routing lines and provider records.

A New Leaf · Autumn House Domestic Violence Shelter (East Valley/Mesa)

b01-anl-autumn-house · usable

A New Leaf's domestic-violence shelter and transitional housing in a confidential Mesa-area location - the East Valley's first DV shelter, operating since 1983. It has 22 beds for survivors fleeing abuse and serves survivors of any gender; the facility is pet-friendly. Placement is arranged by phone screening through A New Leaf's 24-hour DV hotline.

**Services Offered**
Safe shelter and transitional housing for people fleeing domestic abuse, with staff support from arrival; help stabilizing daily life through a workforce center (job and employment help), transportation assistance, and counseling; court advocacy for orders of protection; help planning the move into independent housing; and optional services for up to six months after leaving. The facility is pet-friendly.

**Eligibility Requirements**
For victims of domestic violence. All clients are screened prior to placement - call the hotline to be assessed. Bed availability varies and placement is not guaranteed. No fee for shelter is published; ask about costs during screening.

**Population Served**
Survivors of domestic violence fleeing abuse in the East Valley/Mesa area. A New Leaf states Autumn House serves anyone who is a victim of domestic violence, regardless of gender; program materials describe shelter for women, children, and men leaving abusive situations.

**How to Best Connect**
Call A New Leaf's 24-hour domestic-violence hotline at (480) 890-3039 or 1-844-SAFEDVS (844-723-3387) - the hotline handles screening and placement, and program staff should not be contacted directly. A New Leaf also lists the email DVSTOP@turnanewleaf.org for domestic-violence services, and its 2026 domestic-violence services fact sheet lists AutumnHouse@turnanewleaf.org for availability questions; the hotline remains the screening and placement route. The location is confidential, and intake is by phone screening rather than walk-in.

**Important Information to Know**
Placement is not guaranteed: clients are screened before admission, and the shelter has 22 beds. Countywide domestic-violence shelter access changed in May 2026 when the centralized screening line closed and shelters shifted to direct calls; A New Leaf's hotline remains the line it lists for placement into its own programs. Other routes: National Domestic Violence Hotline (800-799-7233) and the Arizona Sexual & Domestic Violence Hotline (602-279-2980; text 520-720-3383). In an emergency, call 911.

phone: 24-hour DV hotline: (480) 890-3039; 1-844-SAFEDVS (844-723-3387)

address: Mesa area (confidential location; address not disclosed to protect survivors)

website: https://turnanewleaf.org/services/sexual-and-domestic-violence-services/autumn-house/

hours: Hotline 24/7; staff on site around the clock; survivors accepted throughout the year.

resolutionReason: Current provider pages confirm the program (22 beds, serves any gender, pet-friendly, around-the-clock staff, hotline screening) and the East Valley/Mesa service area; the intake number matches A New Leaf's current 'for placement' listing, with the May 2026 countywide screening transition preserved as a caution. The provider's 2026 domestic-violence fact sheet additionally lists AutumnHouse@turnanewleaf.org for availability questions; added under How to Best Connect while the hotline remains the screening and placement route.

Sources: A New Leaf - Autumn House Domestic Violence Shelter · A New Leaf - Sexual & Domestic Violence Services (24-hour hotline) · A New Leaf - Domestic & Sexual Violence Services Department fact sheet (2026; Autumn House availability email) · 12News - Arizona domestic violence hotline discontinued (May 2026)

Consider this: Starter: the East Valley's local DV shelter - 22 beds, any gender, pet-friendly, reached through A New Leaf's 24-hour hotline.

Identity judgment: East Valley DV shelter; distinct from the SAFEDVS screening-line record and other DV shelters; the misattribution of Autumn House to Sojourner is corrected in the Sojourner record.

A New Leaf · East Valley Men's Center (EVMC)

b01-anl-evmc · usable

Emergency shelter in Mesa for single men experiencing homelessness - 104 beds, an immediate bed and storage, case management toward a permanent housing plan, and onsite workforce help. Intake begins with an online pre-screen; A New Leaf does not take intakes over the phone.

**Services Offered**
Emergency shelter for single men: a bed and storage for personal belongings for the length of stay; help from staff to build a permanent housing plan; an onsite workforce center for resumes, references, and job interviews; employment and transportation assistance; and referrals to substance abuse support groups.

**Eligibility Requirements**
Single men experiencing homelessness. Children under 18 are not allowed on site. Entry requires completing A New Leaf's online pre-screen - no phone intakes are taken - and admission depends on bed availability.

**Population Served**
Single men facing homelessness, especially in Mesa and the East Valley; A New Leaf describes single men as the largest homeless population in Arizona and the group that struggles most to find help.

**How to Best Connect**
First step: complete the online pre-screen linked on the EVMC page (https://forms.office.com/r/E1CyUV7sR4). For shelter placement, call the individuals' hotline (877) 211-8661 (also reachable through 2-1-1). Do not call program staff for services. Intake hours are not published - confirm current details when you call.

**Important Information to Know**
Beds are filled through intake and a waitlist, so availability can change day to day, and a pre-screen does not guarantee a bed. No children can stay on site. Staff ask that clients not contact them directly - use the hotline and the pre-screen. Older regional lists have also described EVMC as a Mesa coordinated-entry access point with weekday hours; confirm current assessment hours by phone if you need that service.

phone: Placement hotline (individuals): (877) 211-8661; program office: (480) 610-6722 (staff ask that services not be requested by direct call)

address: 2345 N Country Club Dr, Mesa, AZ 85201

website: https://turnanewleaf.org/services/housing-and-shelter/east-valley-mens-center/

hours: Intake hours not published; enter through the online pre-screen and call (877) 211-8661 for current intake information.

resolutionReason: Current program page confirms identity, the 104-bed men's emergency shelter, Mesa address, onsite services, and the updated intake route (online pre-screen plus the (877) 211-8661 placement hotline; no phone intakes); the staff-contact caution is preserved.

Sources: A New Leaf - East Valley Men's Center · A New Leaf - Housing & Shelter services and placement hotlines

Consider this: Starter: Mesa's men's emergency shelter with a defined intake path (online pre-screen plus placement hotline).

Identity judgment: Mesa single-men's shelter; distinct from A New Leaf's other programs.

A New Leaf · Helaman House (bridge/transitional housing, Mesa)

b01-anl-helaman-house · needs-resolution

Transitional 'Bridge to Success' housing step in Mesa's housing path for people moving on from the Off the Streets emergency shelter - named in City of Mesa materials as A New Leaf's Helaman House, with wraparound case management, help building work and credit history and savings, and connections to rental assistance and housing vouchers. Whether it currently operates, who can enter, and how referrals work are not confirmed.

**Services Offered**
Transitional (bridge) housing with built-in structure and continued case management for people moving on from Mesa's emergency shelter stabilization stage - per City of Mesa descriptions: building work and credit history, saving toward a home, practicing life skills, and connecting to rental assistance, housing vouchers, or other long-term affordable housing. A 2022 city agreement also described bridge-to-housing with emergency temporary housing and support services at this location.

**Eligibility Requirements**
Not confirmed. Historical 2022 program materials describe Mesa's single men who can transition with minimal support (for example, employed at least a month, drug-free, without significant unresolved legal issues), but those descriptions are dated - re-check before relying on them.

**Population Served**
Mesa participants in the city's homelessness response; historically described as single men exiting the Off the Streets stabilization stage.

**How to Best Connect**
No public intake line or current program page is published. To ask whether it is operating and how referrals work, contact the City of Mesa Office of Homeless Solutions' Homeless Resource Line at (480) 644-HOPE (4673) (weekday business hours - confirm), or A New Leaf's main line (480) 969-4024 / housing@turnanewleaf.org. Do not send anyone to the address without confirming the program is active there.

**Important Information to Know**
This is an unresolved lead: Mesa's current web pages still name Helaman House in the housing path, but A New Leaf's current public service listings do not include it, the city funding agreement ran through December 31, 2024, and no public contact route is published. An unrelated Mesa recovery home also uses the name 'Helaman House' at a different address (2613 S Power Rd) - do not confuse them. Verify current status before any referral.

address: 1311 E University Dr, Mesa, AZ 85201 (per the City of Mesa 2022 agreement; current site not confirmed)

resolutionReason: Current existence, eligibility, service scope and intake are unresolved: Mesa's pages still name Helaman House in the housing path while A New Leaf's live service list omits it; the city funding agreement ran through December 31, 2024; no public contact route is published; and an unrelated Mesa recovery home uses the same name at a different address. Targeted checks (city pages, municipal agreement, program metrics, provider site) could not establish a usable intake route, so it is kept for administrator verification rather than client referral.

Sources: City of Mesa - Off the Streets (Bridge to Success / Helaman House) · City of Mesa - Addressing Homelessness in Mesa · City of Mesa - 2022 subrecipient agreement for bridge housing at Helaman House, 1311 E University Dr

Consider this: Needs resolution: confirming whether this Mesa bridge-housing step still operates would clarify a transitional option in the city's own housing path.

A New Leaf · MesaCAN Rent and Utility Assistance

b01-anl-mesacan · usable

MesaCAN (Mesa Community Action Network), part of A New Leaf, provides emergency rent and utility assistance to low-income Mesa residents facing eviction or utility shut-off, plus access to broader services (financial coaching, workforce, tax help) at its Mesa office. Funding is limited and help is first-come, first-served.

**Services Offered**
Financial help with past-due rent or mortgage payments and utility bills to help Mesa households avoid eviction or utility shut-off; help applying for LIHEAP (state utility assistance); information on City of Mesa security- and utility-deposit programs; and access to other MesaCAN services at the same office, including financial coaching, workforce services, free tax preparation (VITA), savings-match programs, and economic development help.

**Eligibility Requirements**
Must be a Mesa resident (city residents only). Rent help is for households facing eviction or a rent crisis; utility help is for households with a disconnection notice, and documented criteria include a household member who is 60+, a household member with a disability, or a child 6 or younger (confirm current criteria). Required documents include proof of income for the last 30 days for all household members, IDs for adults, birth certificates and Social Security cards for household members, and the current lease or mortgage statement plus the utility bill or disconnection notice. Assistance is first-come, first-served and funding can run out; applicants 60+ are exempt from the utility orientation.

**Population Served**
Low-income Mesa households facing a rent or utility crisis; application forms are available in English and Spanish.

**How to Best Connect**
Rent help: call the appointment line at (480) 750-9228 on the first Thursday of the month between 2:00 and 3:00 p.m., or until appointments are gone - you must reach a live person to get instructions. Utility help: schedule an orientation by calling (480) 833-9200 or emailing mesacanclient@turnanewleaf.org; orientations are Monday, Tuesday, and Wednesday mornings at 8:00 a.m., and a completed application with all documents is required at the orientation. In person: 635 E Broadway Rd, Mesa, weekdays 8:00 a.m. to 4:30 p.m. Applications (English and Spanish) are on the program page or available at the office.

**Important Information to Know**
Assistance is not guaranteed: only a limited number of appointments are available, the phone system tells callers when funding is exhausted, and an appointment does not guarantee aid. Older referral lists show different call-in times (some said Thursdays 2:00-2:30 p.m.); the current program page says the first-Thursday 2:00-3:00 p.m. window - confirm when you call. Bring every required document; incomplete packets cannot be processed at orientation.

phone: (480) 833-9200 (office; utility orientations); (480) 750-9228 (rent-assistance appointment line)

address: 635 E Broadway Rd, Mesa, AZ 85204

website: https://turnanewleaf.org/services/financial-empowerment/mesacan/mesacan-rent-and-utility-assistance/

hours: Office: Monday to Friday, 8:00 a.m. to 4:30 p.m. Rent appointments: first Thursday of the month, 2:00 to 3:00 p.m. (until slots fill). Utility orientations: Monday, Tuesday, and Wednesday mornings from 8:00 a.m.

resolutionReason: Current program pages confirm location, hours, both intake lines, document requirements, and the limited-funding process; older-referral-list concerns are resolved, with the appointment-day discrepancy flagged in Important Information to Know.

Sources: A New Leaf - MesaCAN Rent and Utility Assistance · City of Mesa - Mesa Community Action Network (MesaCAN) fact sheet

Consider this: Starter: Mesa's main rent/utility crisis window with a Spanish application path and a clear document list.

Identity judgment: MesaCAN rent/utility aid; distinct from the state STCS program it helps administer; appointment-window conflict preserved.

A New Leaf · Rapid ReHousing (rental subsidy and case management)

b01-anl-rapid-rehousing · usable

A New Leaf's Rapid ReHousing program provides short-term rental subsidy (up to 12 months), move-in cost help, and case management for individuals and families experiencing homelessness in Maricopa County, including Mesa. Entry is through the region's coordinated entry system - call 211 - not a direct application.

**Services Offered**
Rental subsidy for up to 12 months depending on assessment, help with move-in costs including application fees, rent and utility payments based on standards tailored to the household, temporary case management, workforce assistance, and referrals to other services so participants can maintain housing long term.

**Eligibility Requirements**
Households currently experiencing homelessness or living in emergency shelter - both individuals and families. Must complete coordinated entry: single adults are assessed with the Vi-SPDAT through the single coordinated entry system, and families are assessed with the Vi-FSPDAT through the Family Housing Hub. The amount of assistance depends on case-management assessment and verification of household income and debt; funding availability varies.

**Population Served**
Individuals and families across the Valley (Maricopa County), including Mesa; the program has no single geographic focus and staff meet participants where they are.

**How to Best Connect**
There is no walk-in office. Call 2-1-1 (877-211-8661) to start coordinated entry; families may be routed through the Family Housing Hub (602-595-8700). Questions about A New Leaf's housing programs can go to housing@turnanewleaf.org, and the main line is (480) 969-4024.

**Important Information to Know**
A New Leaf cannot take direct applications - entry is only through coordinated entry, and neither timing nor funding is guaranteed. Assistance is evaluated month to month and typically steps down over time; it is meant to stabilize housing quickly rather than provide a permanent subsidy. If a longer-term subsidy is needed, ask about vouchers or long-term affordable housing options at the same time.

phone: 2-1-1 / (877) 211-8661 (coordinated entry); A New Leaf main line: (480) 969-4024

address: No public office; staff meet participants across the community

website: https://turnanewleaf.org/services/housing-and-shelter/rapid-rehousing/

hours: No public office hours; inquiries go through coordinated entry (call 211) or email.

resolutionReason: Current page confirms program design (up to 12 months, move-in help), eligibility route (Vi-SPDAT/Vi-FSPDAT through coordinated entry), Valley-wide service area, and the contact route (211; housing@turnanewleaf.org); funding and capacity limits are stated.

Sources: A New Leaf - Rapid ReHousing · A New Leaf - Housing & Shelter services and placement hotlines

Consider this: Starter: the rental-subsidy rehousing route (up to 12 months) that shows clients what coordinated entry can lead to beyond shelter.

Identity judgment: Distinct rental-subsidy program; coordinated-entry-only intake preserved.

A New Leaf · SAFEDVS 24-hour domestic violence hotline (former countywide screening line)

b01-anl-safedvs · needs-resolution

A New Leaf's 24-hour domestic-violence hotline - historically the SAFEDVS centralized screening line for Maricopa and Pinal counties that assessed callers and matched them to openings in domestic-violence shelters. The countywide centralized screening function reportedly closed on May 15, 2026, with shelters shifting to direct calls; A New Leaf's current website still lists this number for placement into its own DV services, but the line's current scope and staffing are not confirmed.

**Services Offered**
Crisis hotline support for domestic-violence survivors - risk assessment, safety planning, and connection to shelter and services. Historically this was the single countywide phone entry for Maricopa and Pinal county DV shelter beds; A New Leaf now describes the number as the line for placement into its own domestic-violence programs (including Autumn House and the DVSTOP overflow program).

**Eligibility Requirements**
Open to anyone experiencing abuse; all callers have historically been assisted according to their situation, although help was never guaranteed because resources are limited. Current screening scope is unconfirmed.

**Population Served**
Domestic-violence survivors in Maricopa and Pinal counties historically; current countywide reach is unconfirmed.

**How to Best Connect**
Call (480) 890-3039 or 1-844-SAFEDVS (844-723-3387); A New Leaf also lists DVSTOP@turnanewleaf.org for its domestic-violence services. Because the countywide line's status changed in 2026, confirm what the line can currently help with before counting on it. Other current routes: call individual shelters directly; Sojourner Center's 24/7 hotline (602-244-0089); the National Domestic Violence Hotline (800-799-7233); the Arizona Sexual & Domestic Violence Hotline (602-279-2980; text 520-720-3383). In an emergency, call 911.

**Important Information to Know**
Unresolved: 2026 reporting from 12News and Sojourner Center says the centralized Safe DVS hotline closed May 15, 2026 and that survivors should call shelters directly, while A New Leaf's own website (as of September 2026) still lists this number for placement into its DV programs. It is not confirmed whether the number is currently staffed for new callers, whether it mainly serves prior waitlist clients, or how far its reach extends beyond A New Leaf shelters. Do not present it as the former one-call-for-all-county-shelters service.

phone: (480) 890-3039; 1-844-SAFEDVS (844-723-3387)

website: https://turnanewleaf.org/services/sexual-and-domestic-violence-services/

hours: Listed as 24 hours a day, 7 days a week (A New Leaf); current staffing not confirmed.

resolutionReason: Consequential access conflict unresolved: the countywide centralized DV screening line reportedly closed May 15, 2026 (12News; Sojourner Center), while A New Leaf's current site still promotes (480) 890-3039 / 1-844-SAFEDVS for placement into its DV services; public sources cannot confirm whether the line is currently staffed or how far its reach extends. Retained for administrator verification, with confirmed fallback routes (direct shelter calls; state and national hotlines) noted.

Sources: A New Leaf - Sexual & Domestic Violence Services (hotline listed 'for placement') · 12News - Arizona domestic violence hotline discontinued (May 2026) · Sojourner Center - When Systems Shift, Safety Must Stay Strong (centralized referral line transition)

Consider this: Needs resolution: confirming this line's current scope would restore or rule out a direct DV-shelter routing number that A New Leaf still publishes.

A New Leaf · La Mesita Apartments (affordable housing and project-based vouchers, Mesa)

b01-anl-la-mesita-apartments · usable

A 110-unit affordable apartment community in Mesa (studios through four-bedroom homes) run by A New Leaf, with onsite services including a Head Start program for ages 0-5. Some units are income-restricted (at or below 60% or 30% of area median income), and units are available for people transitioning from homelessness through the City of Mesa Project-Based Voucher (PBV) program, where tenants pay about 30% of income.

**Services Offered**
Long-term affordable rental housing with studio through four-bedroom units and onsite support: a Head Start program for children ages 0-5, a computer lab, financial-wellness and parenting classes, and workforce assistance. Some units carry project-based rental assistance so that people transitioning from homelessness who need support services can pay about 30% of their income for rent.

**Eligibility Requirements**
General units are for low-income households within income limits (some units serve households at or below 60% of area median income; some at or below 30%). PBV units follow City of Mesa criteria: applicants must meet the local chronically homeless definition (at least two episodes of shelter living plus a substantiated need for long-term case management and supportive services) and be an elderly individual or family, a disabled individual or family, or a family needing supportive services; income must be at or below published limits (PBV materials list 30% AMI caps, for example $13,200 for one person); a background check and LIHTC tenancy approval are required. The City describes PBV as a referral-based program.

**Population Served**
Low-income Mesa households - families with children, individuals, and (for PBV units) elderly or disabled households and families needing supportive services, including people transitioning from homelessness.

**How to Best Connect**
For unit availability, call the leasing office at (480) 969-5233 or email assistant@lamesita123apartments.com (Kay Kay Realty handles leasing, and A New Leaf directs housing inquiries there). For PBV-subsidized units, current City of Mesa guidance routes adults without children through a coordinated-entry access point (Brian Garcia Welcome Center, 602-229-5155) and families with minor children through the Family Housing Hub (602-595-8700). For PBV questions, contact City of Mesa Housing & Community Development at (480) 644-3536 (20 E. Main St, Suite 250; Spanish-language information at the same number), or email housing@turnanewleaf.org for general housing support.

**Important Information to Know**
Status check: older City of Mesa PBV packets list the La Mesita waiting list as open, but the City's current PBV page describes La Mesita as 'Referral Only' - confirm the current process and any wait before promising anything. PBV rent is about 30% of income, and LIHTC income limits are very low. Some A New Leaf materials note a veteran preference for units serving people transitioning from homelessness - confirm current preference rules with Mesa Housing. A New Leaf also operates a separate La Mesita Family Shelter on the same campus for families in crisis - ask which program fits your situation.

Ability360 (formerly Arizona Bridge to Independent Living) - housing information & referral, home modifications, Mainstream voucher partnership

b02a-ability360-housing · usable

Ability360 is the Phoenix area's nonprofit Center for Independent Living (formerly Arizona Bridge to Independent Living). It answers housing questions for people with disabilities, helps with accessible and affordable housing searches, and runs a home-modification program - ramps, grab bars, widened doorways and similar accessibility work - that is free for qualifying low-to-moderate-income Maricopa County residents. It has partnered with area housing authorities on HUD Mainstream vouchers for people with disabilities; ask about current voucher availability. Call (602) 256-2245 or the information line (602) 296-0536.

**Services Offered**
- Housing information and referral for people with disabilities and their families - staff answer questions about housing options and connect people to accessible/affordable housing and other community resources.
- Home Modification program that improves accessibility and safety at home: ramps, widened door openings, grab bars, sink and toilet modifications, relocated electrical switches, and similar work. "Adapt your home, not yourself."
- Broader independent living supports: independent living skills training, peer mentoring, transition services (including moving out of nursing homes or assisted living), home care services, and advocacy.
- A 2020 public report describes Ability360 working with five area housing authorities on HUD Mainstream Voucher applications for non-elderly people with disabilities, with designated vouchers reported under its control; the current status of any such vouchers is not confirmed - ask when you call.

**Eligibility Requirements**
- Information and referral: no eligibility rules are published; it serves people with disabilities and their families.
- Home modifications: for people with disabilities and/or seniors who are low-to-moderate-income Maricopa County residents. Mercy Care ALTCS members need a referral from their case manager. Required documents vary - call to confirm.
- Housing vouchers: eligibility is determined by the housing authority under HUD rules; nothing is guaranteed.

**Population Served**
- People with disabilities of all ages in the Greater Phoenix metropolitan area, including Mesa (Ability360 is described as Arizona's largest Center for Independent Living).
- Seniors are also served - the home-modification program is for "people with disabilities and/or seniors," and home-care services serve elderly clients.
- Families and support people of the above through information and referral.

**How to Best Connect**

- Main office: 5025 E. Washington St., Ste. 200, Phoenix, AZ 85034 - open Mon-Fri 8 a.m.-5 p.m., closed weekends.
- Mesa/Gilbert office (Home Care Services): 205 E. Southern Ave., Mesa, AZ 85210, (480) 655-9750, Mon-Fri 8 a.m.-5 p.m. - ask which location handles your request.
- Home modifications: use the "Get Started" form on the Ability360 Home Modifications page or call.
- Remote access: phone, email and online forms; you do not need to visit in person to start.

**Important Information to Know**
- Ability360 is not a shelter and does not provide direct rent subsidies; its housing role is information/referral, home modifications, and the Mainstream voucher partnership described above.
- Home modifications are described as currently free to people who qualify; a separate community listing says "fees vary," so confirm cost and funding during intake (funded mainly by Community Development Block Grants, with ALTCS or other sources sometimes possible).
- Funding and capacity are limited; ask about current timing and availability.
- If you were told about a Mainstream voucher here, confirm current voucher availability directly - it is not published.

AffordableHousingHub.org (unofficial third-party) - waitlist & affordable-housing tracker for Mesa and Maricopa County

b02a-affordablehousinghub-tracker · needs-resolution

Affordable Housing Hub is an independent, third-party website that compiles affordable-housing listings (Section 8, public housing, low-income tax-credit properties) and waitlist-status information nationwide, including Mesa and Maricopa County. It is not a housing provider and does not process applications, and its waitlist statuses are unofficial - data is attributed to another third-party site (section8waitlist.org) and should never be treated as authoritative. Treat it only as a rough monitoring pointer and confirm every opening with the housing authority or the property; its Mesa pages currently list both Mesa public housing and Section 8 waitlists as closed.

**Services Offered**
- A free, searchable national directory of affordable-housing properties and waitlist statuses. Its Mesa pages include a City of Mesa Housing Authority profile (unit counts, waitlist summaries, HUD income limits and fair-market rents, and authority contact details) plus links to Mesa LIHTC properties such as Mesa Artspace Lofts and Residences at Center Street Station.
- Browsing and pointers only: no case management, no housing navigation, no intake, no applications processed, no vouchers issued.

**Eligibility Requirements**
- None to browse the website (it is public).
- Each listed housing program has its own rules (income limits, preferences, lotteries) set by the housing authority or property - not by Affordable Housing Hub.

**Population Served**
- Anyone researching affordable housing; no specific population is targeted. Mesa/Maricopa pages are relevant to local renters, including voucher seekers and people on or seeking waitlists.

**How to Best Connect**
- Website: https://affordablehousinghub.org/ - use the Mesa/Maricopa County pages to see what properties and programs exist.
- For anything actionable - status, applications, waitlist openings - contact the housing authority or the property directly, for example City of Mesa Housing Authority, (480) 644-3536, mesaaz.gov/housing. Nothing on this tracker should be passed to a client as fact without that confirmation.

**Important Information to Know**
- Unofficial and non-authoritative: waitlist data is supplied by a separate third-party site (section8waitlist.org); pages mix "re-check daily / synced" claims with last-verified dates months old, and the site is not a government or nonprofit service (it carries advertising).
- Do not rely on its status claims: an opening can be wrong or already closed. Confirm with the housing authority or property before advising anyone.
- Because its status information cannot be relied on and it offers no intake or application channel, it works at most as a monitoring pointer for staff watching for openings - not as a client referral.

American Red Cross - Disaster Services, Phoenix (emergency lodging/shelter for people displaced by home fires & other disasters)

b02a-red-cross-disaster-lodging · usable

American Red Cross disaster assistance can help people displaced from their homes by disasters - including house and apartment fires and floods - with emergency needs that can include a place to stay (shelter/emergency lodging), food and clothing, provided case-by-case around the clock. It is not a general shelter or hotel-voucher program for homelessness. After a home fire or other disaster, call (800) 733-2767; for financial assistance you will typically need an incident number from the responding fire department. Local office: 4747 N. 22nd St., Ste. 100, Phoenix, (602) 336-6660.

**Services Offered**
- Emergency assistance for people affected by local disasters (home/apartment fires, floods and similar events): can include shelter/emergency lodging, food, clothing, family reunification, and mental-health and health services; delivered 24 hours a day on a case-by-case basis.
- Recovery planning and referrals in the days and weeks after a disaster, plus preparedness information.
- Not offered: routine hotel/motel vouchers for people who are homeless, rent or deposit assistance, or ongoing housing programs.

**Eligibility Requirements**
- People impacted by a disaster (for example, displaced by a home fire).
- For financial assistance, the caller will typically need an incident number from the responding fire department; decisions are made case-by-case and are not guaranteed.

**Population Served**
- Residents affected by disasters in Maricopa, Gila and Pinal counties (the American Red Cross Central Arizona Chapter's service area), including Mesa - individuals and families of any age; no other population requirements.

**How to Best Connect**
- 24-hour emergency line: (800) 733-2767 (toll-free emergency voice/TDD). If a fire just happened, ask the responding fire department for the incident number before you call.
- Local office: 4747 N. 22nd St., Ste. 100, Phoenix, AZ 85016; (602) 336-6660; walk-in, phone, website or mail accepted. Office hours Mon-Fri 8 a.m.-4:30 p.m.; emergency services 24 hours.
- Online: redcross.org (see local Arizona pages).

**Important Information to Know**
- This is disaster relief, not a homelessness program: do not expect hotel vouchers for housing crises that are not disaster-related - for those needs, call 2-1-1 Arizona (dial 2-1-1) or local shelter services.
- Help is case-by-case and depends on verification (incident number); assistance can be limited in amount and scope and does not replace ongoing housing programs.
- Some older community lists show outdated Red Cross phone numbers (for example, (800) 842-7349); use the numbers above.

address: 4747 N. 22nd St., Ste. 100, Phoenix, AZ 85016

website: https://www.redcross.org/

Area Agency on Aging, Region One - HOPWA Housing Information Services (HIV Care Directions)

b02a-hopwa-care-directions · usable

The Area Agency on Aging, Region One runs the HOPWA "Housing Information Services" program for people living with HIV/AIDS in Maricopa and Pinal Counties. A housing information specialist provides housing referrals, places clients on affordable-housing waitlists, and connects them to emergency shelter resources; the agency's HIV Care Directions program also provides HIV medical case management and help finding and maintaining subsidized and affordable housing. Call (602) 264-2273.

**Services Offered**
- HOPWA Housing Information Services: housing referrals; placement on affordable-housing waitlists; emergency shelter resources; the Housing Information Specialist matches housing needs with appropriate housing providers in Maricopa and Pinal Counties.
- HIV Care Directions (same agency): comprehensive medical case management for people 18 and older with HIV; help enrolling in and maintaining Ryan White Part A services; bus tickets and ride services for medical-care access; help with health insurance premiums and HIV-related medical costs; and assistance finding and maintaining subsidized and affordable housing.
- This is navigation/intake help - not an emergency shelter bed line, and not a direct rent-subsidy program of its own.

**Eligibility Requirements**
- HIV or AIDS diagnosis - HOPWA requires at least one household member diagnosed with HIV/AIDS.
- Household income at or below 80% of Area Median Income.
- Residency in Maricopa or Pinal County; HIV Care Directions serves people 18 and older. Confirm details at intake.

**Population Served**
- Adults (18+) living with HIV/AIDS and their households, in Maricopa County (and Pinal County); Mesa residents are within the service area. A key housing-navigation route for medically vulnerable clients who need referrals rather than cash aid.

**How to Best Connect**

- Hours for this program are not published online - call to confirm before visiting; no walk-in intake process is published.

**Important Information to Know**
- The City of Phoenix is the local HOPWA funder and administers HOPWA activities for Maricopa and Pinal County residents; the Area Agency is the named access point for housing information, referrals and waitlist placement - not the funder or a shelter provider.
- Placement and timing depend on housing-provider availability and waitlists; no unit or voucher is guaranteed.
- Eligibility (HIV/AIDS diagnosis, income) is verified at intake; confirm current program scope by phone before sending someone.
- No fees are published for the information/referral services; housing obtained through HOPWA programs follows those programs' own rules.

AHCCCS Housing Program (AHP) — Statewide Permanent Supportive Housing (administrator: Arizona Behavioral Health Corporation with HOM, Inc.)

b02b-ahcccs-housing-program · usable

State-funded permanent supportive housing for AHCCCS members with behavioral health needs. The statewide Housing Administrator (ABC with HOM, Inc.) manages a single statewide waitlist and pays rent subsidies to landlords; members pay up to 30% of income, and referrals come from behavioral health providers.

**Services Offered**
- State-funded permanent supportive housing (PSH) for AHCCCS members with behavioral health needs: long-term rent subsidies in scattered-site units, dedicated site-based units (Community Living Program models), and project-based voucher units.
- Members pay up to 30% of their income toward rent; the subsidy is paid directly to the landlord by the statewide Housing Administrator (Arizona Behavioral Health Corporation with HOM, Inc.).
- As budget allows, limited extra help: housing deposits, move-in assistance, eviction prevention, and payments for member-caused damages.
- The Housing Administrator receives provider referrals, manages the single statewide waitlist, verifies eligibility, hosts housing briefings, verifies household income and rent share, conducts Housing Quality Standards inspections, pays rental subsidies, and handles lease renewals and recertifications.
- Supportive services (case management, housing search help, life-skills and budgeting support) come through the member's AHCCCS behavioral health provider network, not from the administrator.

**Eligibility Requirements**
- Must be an adult AHCCCS member (18+).
- Most funding is reserved for members with a Serious Mental Illness (SMI) designation; a limited number of units serve high-cost/high-need members with general mental health and/or substance use (GMHSU) needs, and some units are tied to specific services such as Assertive Community Treatment (ACT) teams.
- A qualified behavioral health provider must document the behavioral health need and the housing need (examples: homelessness, an institutional transition, or a documented medical necessity).
- Only approved behavioral health providers can submit the AHP application on a member's behalf — members cannot apply directly.

**Population Served**
- Adults enrolled in AHCCCS with a serious mental illness designation, plus a smaller number of high-need members with general mental health/substance use conditions. Statewide, including Mesa and Maricopa County.

**How to Best Connect**
- Members: contact your behavioral health provider, health home, or health plan care team and ask for a housing assessment and referral (in Maricopa County the Regional Behavioral Health Authority is Mercy Care). The provider submits the AHP application.
- Program, application, or waitlist questions: ABC at 602-712-9200 or AHPinfo@azabc.org (administrative office: 501 E. Thomas Rd., Phoenix, AZ 85012).
- Already housed through AHP: HOM at 602-265-4640 or find your Housing Specialist at hominc.com/find (HOM Central AZ: 5326 E Washington St, Phoenix, AZ 85034).
- General AHCCCS housing questions: Housing@azahcccs.gov.
- Referral-based only; there is no walk-in application site.

**Important Information to Know**
- Housing resources are limited — the state allocation covers roughly 2,700 members — and a waitlist is in use; a referral does not guarantee housing or a timeline.
- This is the state-funded AHCCCS Housing Program (AHP), separate from the AHCCCS H2O demonstration and from HUD Section 8 and Continuum of Care programs.
- ABC/HOM's contract as statewide Housing Administrator was renewed effective July 1, 2026.
- Licensed behavioral health residential facilities, group homes, and other licensed clinical residential settings are not eligible for AHP.
- Current waitlist length and unit availability are not published — ask the member's provider or ABC for status.

phone: 602-712-9200 (ABC); 602-265-4640 (HOM)

website: https://www.azahcccs.gov/AHCCCS/Initiatives/AHP/

resolutionReason: Retained as usable. Primary sources establish the program identity (state-funded AHCCCS Housing Program), the current statewide administrator (ABC with HOM; contract renewed effective 7/1/2026), the direct service (permanent supportive housing rent subsidies with up to 30% member rent share and budget-limited move-in/eviction help), eligibility (AHCCCS members with SMI or qualifying high-need GMHSU needs; provider-documented housing need), and a live intake route (behavioral health provider/health plan referral; ABC 602-712-9200, AHPinfo@azabc.org; HOM 602-265-4640). Remaining limits: current waitlist length and openings are not published; referral-only, no walk-in application.

Short-Term Crisis Services (STCS) — Arizona DES emergency housing help for families with children

b02b-stcs-family-crisis-assistance · usable

State emergency-assistance program for low-income families with children who are in a crisis. Help can include rent or mortgage payments to prevent homelessness, emergency shelter/temporary housing, utility help, and move-in costs; applications are made through the local Community Action Agency (MesaCAN in Mesa).

**Services Offered**
- Emergency financial assistance for eligible families during a crisis: rent or mortgage payments to prevent homelessness, emergency shelter/temporary housing, utility payments and deposits, rental deposits, and move-in assistance (first and last month's rent, security deposits, application fees).
- Help related to getting or keeping a job, such as transportation assistance and tools or equipment.
- Applications, decisions, and payments are handled by the local Community Action Agency (or another contracted partner); DES administers and funds the program.

**Eligibility Requirements**
- Arizona resident at the time of application.
- A dependent child under age 18 must live in the home and meet U.S. citizen or qualified legal resident criteria.
- Total gross household income at or below 125% of the Federal Poverty Guidelines — or 150% if the household includes someone age 60 or older or a person with a disability.
- Must be facing a temporary, unforeseen emergency crisis the household cannot meet with its own income or resources, and be able to explain and prove the emergency need (examples: family separation caused by domestic violence, loss of income, an unforeseen event, or a situation that endangers the family's health and safety).
- Help is limited to once in a 12-month period and is not guaranteed.

**Population Served**
- Low-income Arizona families with minor children in a housing or financial crisis, including families facing homelessness or eviction and families leaving unsafe homes. Mesa and Maricopa County residents apply through their local Community Action Agency.

**How to Best Connect**
- Apply through your local Community Action Agency — applications are handled at the local level.
- Mesa: Mesa Community Action Network (MesaCAN, a program of A New Leaf), 635 E. Broadway Rd., Mesa, AZ 85204; call (480) 833-9200; email mesacanclient@turnanewleaf.org; office hours Monday–Friday, 8:00 a.m.–4:30 p.m. Call to confirm current intake steps and what to bring — expect to explain the emergency and show proof (for example an eviction or utility notice and proof of income).
- Other Maricopa County areas: Maricopa County Human Services, 234 N. Central Ave., Suite 3000, Phoenix, AZ 85009, (602) 506-5911; Glendale: Glendale Community Action Programs, (623) 930-2000; Phoenix: City of Phoenix Human Services Department. The full statewide list is at des.az.gov/CommunityActionAgencies.
- Phone lines get very busy and demand is high — keep trying.

**Important Information to Know**
- Funding is limited; assistance can be unavailable even for eligible families, and agencies may take applications only while funds last.
- The program can help with emergency shelter/temporary housing, and DES policy defines temporary housing as possibly including a hotel/motel or other transient setting — but whether motel-type help is available for a given family depends on the local agency and current funding. Confirm by phone before relying on it.
- DES materials describe the income ceiling slightly differently (a community-services brochure lists 150% overall); the program standard is 125% generally, or 150% for households with someone 60+ or a person with a disability.
- DES program policy took effect July 1, 2026; eligibility and documents are verified by the local agency.

phone: 480-833-9200 (Mesa intake); 602-506-5911 (Maricopa County Human Services)

address: 635 E. Broadway Rd., Mesa, AZ 85204 (Mesa intake)

website: https://des.az.gov/stcs

hours: Mesa intake site (MesaCAN): Monday–Friday, 8:00 a.m.–4:30 p.m.; call to confirm intake steps and funding availability.

resolutionReason: Retained as usable. Although it was recorded as a routing source, STCS is a direct emergency-assistance program rather than a generic directory: DES sources establish the service types (rent/mortgage help, emergency shelter/temporary housing that may include hotel/motel, utilities, deposits, move-in costs), eligibility (dependent child under 18; 125%/150% FPG; once per 12 months), and the local intake route. The Mesa intake point was corrected/confirmed to Mesa Community Action Network (480-833-9200, 635 E. Broadway Rd.) rather than the county office number. Remaining limit: whether motel-type help is funded for a given family depends on the local agency and current funding — confirm by phone.

DDD Affordable Housing Program — Section 811 PRA, Coffelt-Lamoreaux and housing voucher referrals (DES Division of Developmental Disabilities)

b02b-ddd-affordable-housing · usable

DDD's affordable housing referral program for adults with developmental disabilities: Section 811 PRA units, the Coffelt-Lamoreaux community in Phoenix, and housing voucher referrals with public housing authorities. DDD Support Coordinators refer members directly; rent is 30% of household income.

**Services Offered**
- DDD Support Coordinator referrals into affordable housing reserved for people with developmental disabilities, instead of public lotteries:
  - HUD Section 811 Project Rental Assistance (811 PRA) — the leading source of DDD affordable-housing funding; units have been located in Phoenix, Tucson, Camp Verde, Bullhead City, Page, and Globe.
  - Coffelt-Lamoreaux Apartment Homes in Phoenix — 30 units reserved for DDD members within a 301-unit community (partnership with the Housing Authority of Maricopa County and the property manager).
  - Housing voucher referrals with the Housing Authority of Maricopa County and the City of Tucson (non-elderly adult members, ages 18–61) — the member chooses a property that accepts the voucher, is in the housing authority's service area, and passes inspection.
- Federally funded properties will consider reasonable modifications with documentation from a health care provider connecting the need to the disability.

**Eligibility Requirements**
- Must be a DDD member; the 811 PRA program (and, per DDD's quick guide, Coffelt-Lamoreaux referrals) requires eligibility for DDD and Arizona Long Term Care Services (ALTCS).
- Age 18–61 (non-elderly).
- Current Person-Centered Service Plan (PCSP) or planning document.
- Meet financial eligibility requirements and HUD requirements.
- For vouchers: be willing to live in the housing authority's area, apply at the property, pay the application fee, and be approved.
- Background checks apply to everyone who will live in the unit; all adults in the unit sign the lease.

**Population Served**
- Adults with developmental disabilities who are enrolled with DES/DDD and need affordable housing. Members can live alone, with family, or with roommates; income of live-in caregivers is not counted toward the household's combined income.

**How to Best Connect**
- Ask the member's DDD Support Coordinator for a referral — this program does not accept direct public applications.
- DDD Customer Service Center: 1-844-770-9500, option 1 (questions or help connecting with a Support Coordinator).
- Referrals come from DDD's own lists; timing depends on a vacancy matching the member's needs, so ask what is available now.

**Important Information to Know**
- Costs: monthly rent is 30% of the household's combined income; households generally must pay a deposit and the first month's rent, and may need to set up utilities before moving in.
- Availability is limited and there can be a wait between referral and move-in.
- Age wording varies slightly in DES materials (18–61 in the DDD quick guide and housing web page; one DES policy manual says 18–62) — confirm the exact limit with the Support Coordinator.
- Households must keep appointments, provide requested documents, follow property rules, and maintain HUD quality standards; properties are federally funded, drug-free, and smoke-free.
- A Spanish-language version of the DDD affordable housing quick guide is available (DDD-2313A-S).

phone: 1-844-770-9500 (option 1)

website: https://des.az.gov/services/disabilities/developmental-disabilities/individuals-and-families/supports-and-services/housing

resolutionReason: Retained as usable. DES/DDD sources establish the distinct lanes (811 PRA units; Coffelt-Lamoreaux in Phoenix; voucher referrals with the Housing Authority of Maricopa County and City of Tucson), the referral-only access through DDD Support Coordinators, eligibility (DDD and ALTCS for 811 PRA; ages 18–61; current PCSP; financial and HUD requirements), costs (30% of combined income; deposit and first month's rent are household responsibility), and the contact route (Support Coordinator; DDD Customer Service 1-844-770-9500, option 1). Remaining limits: current unit availability and wait times are not published, and DES documents vary between age 61 and 62 as the non-elderly upper limit.

Sources: Member Housing Opportunities — Arizona DES/DDD · Quick Reference Guide: DDD Affordable Housing (DDD-2313A, 06/25) · Guía de consulta rápida: Vivienda Asequible de DDD (DDD-2313A-S) · DES AdSS Medical Policy Manual 1710 — AHCCCS Housing Program (effective 09/17/2025)

Consider this: Adds the DDD referral lane into subsidized units and vouchers for adults with developmental disabilities - no disability-specific housing route exists in the starter set.

DDD Housing Specialists — referral route into the AHCCCS Housing Program and H2O

b02b-ddd-housing-specialists · usable

Housing Specialists connected to DDD coordinate referrals for eligible members into the AHCCCS Housing Program (state-funded permanent supportive housing) and the H2O housing demonstration. Access starts with the member's DDD Support Coordinator and planning team, not a public application.

**Services Offered**
- Housing Specialists working with DDD (staff at Administrative Services Subcontractors and provider agencies) coordinate and refer eligible members into:
  - the AHCCCS Housing Program — state-funded permanent supportive housing rent subsidies for AHCCCS members with behavioral health needs; and
  - H2O (Housing and Health Opportunities) — an AHCCCS demonstration for eligible members who are homeless or at risk of homelessness.
- They help identify and document housing needs, work with the member's planning team on independent-living goals, and coordinate with the statewide housing administrator for the AHCCCS Housing Program (ABC/HOM) and the H2O administrator (Solari).

**Eligibility Requirements**
- Must be enrolled with DES/DDD and have goals that include living independently in the community.
- The destination programs have their own rules: H2O, for example, serves members with a Serious Mental Illness designation who are homeless or at risk of homelessness and who also have an active chronic health condition or a recent/upcoming release from a correctional facility (within about 90 days).
- Referrals run through the member's planning team/Support Coordinator — there is no direct public application.

**Population Served**
- Adults with developmental disabilities who are DDD members and want to live independently, including members in Mesa and Maricopa County.

**How to Best Connect**
- Start with the member's DDD Support Coordinator: ask that housing be addressed in the person-centered service plan and request a connection to a Housing Specialist.
- DDD Customer Service Center: 1-844-770-9500, option 1.
- H2O questions: Solari (state H2O administrator) member support — 1-855-814-4673 (local 480-546-7135) or H2Omembersupport@solari-inc.org.
- For the AHCCCS Housing Program application itself, an approved behavioral health provider submits it on the member's behalf.

**Important Information to Know**
- This is an internal referral role rather than a walk-in service — there is no public Housing Specialist intake line; start with the Support Coordinator or the DDD Customer Service Center.
- Basis: DES Administrative Services Subcontractor policy (Medical Policy Manual 1710, effective September 17, 2025); confirm current local practice with the member's DDD team.
- Both destination programs are limited-capacity and may have waits; an eligible referral does not guarantee housing or timing.
- H2O is separate from the state-funded AHCCCS Housing Program and has different rules and an outside administrator (Solari).

phone: 1-844-770-9500 (option 1)

website: https://des.az.gov/services/disabilities/developmental-disabilities/individuals-and-families/supports-and-services/housing

resolutionReason: Retained as usable and kept separate from the 811 PRA/Coffelt-Lamoreaux lane and from the AHCCCS Housing Program administrator resource, because it is a distinct DDD referral function. DES Administrative Services Subcontractor Medical Policy Manual 1710 (effective 9/17/2025) directs AdSS and provider Housing Specialists to refer eligible members into the AHCCCS Housing Program and H2O; the practical entry point is the member's Support Coordinator (DDD Customer Service 1-844-770-9500, option 1), and the H2O member support contact (Solari) was verified. Remaining limits: local staffing/practice and referral timing are not published — confirm with the member's DDD team.

Sources: DES AdSS Medical Policy Manual 1710 — Housing Specialist referral duties for AHP and H2O (effective 09/17/2025) · Member Housing Opportunities — DES/DDD (housing options and H2O) · DDD Member Newsletter (Winter 2026) — H2O program and Solari member support contact

Consider this: Adds the internal DDD referral route into AHP and H2O - clarifies how DDD-connected clients access those programs, distinct from the programs themselves.

Identity judgment: DDD referral function into AHP/H2O; distinct from those programs.

Arizona Department of Housing (ADOH) — Section 811 PRA (DDD referral) + statewide affordable-housing/LIHTC information

b03a-adoh-811-lihtc · usable

Arizona's state housing agency. It administers the Section 811 Project Rental Assistance (811 PRA) rent subsidies for adults with developmental disabilities who are referred through DES/DDD, and it publishes statewide affordable-housing and Low-Income Housing Tax Credit (LIHTC) information. It is not an open waiting list for the general public.

**Services Offered**
Section 811 Project Rental Assistance (811 PRA): federal rent subsidies attached to units in participating affordable (LIHTC and other federally funded) properties, administered by ADOH as HUD's grantee. Tenants pay about 30% of household income toward rent, and the properties are expected to consider reasonable modifications for disability-related needs. ADOH also publishes LIHTC program materials, lists the online "Search for Affordable Housing in Arizona" route — HousingSearch.AZ.gov (free; market-rate, affordable, accessible and specialized-housing listings, with a call center for people without internet access) — and staffs public customer liaisons to answer housing questions; its site also lists Arizona's 24 housing authorities.

**Eligibility Requirements**
811 PRA is referral-only, not an open waiting list. For the DDD pathway, a member must be eligible for DDD and ALTCS, be 18–61 years old (some DES documents list 62 as the upper limit; confirm with DDD), have a current Person-Centered Service Plan, meet financial eligibility requirements, meet HUD requirements, and be referred by a DES/DDD Support Coordinator; the member must be willing to live where 811 units are available. The general affordable-housing information on ADOH's website is open to anyone; applying to a specific LIHTC property goes through that property's own tenant-selection process.

**Population Served**
811 PRA serves adults with developmental disabilities (DES/DDD members) ages 18–61 and their households. Statewide affordable-housing information is available to the general public, including Mesa and Maricopa County residents.

**How to Best Connect**
ADOH main line: 602-771-1000 (TTY: 7-1-1; older ADOH materials list 602-771-1001); customer liaisons answer public housing questions Monday–Friday, 8:00 a.m.–5:00 p.m. Address: 1110 W. Washington St., Phoenix, AZ 85007. Website: housing.az.gov. Affordable-housing search: housingsearch.az.gov or call 877-428-8844, Monday–Friday, 7:00 a.m.–6:00 p.m. MST (TTY 7-1-1), for a listing of subsidized and low-income properties. For 811 PRA, DDD members should contact their DES/DDD Support Coordinator, or the DDD Customer Service Center at 1-844-770-9500 (option 1); ADOH does not take public applications for 811 units. If ADOH's website does not load, call the main line for the current affordable-housing search link.

**Important Information to Know**
Wait times and unit availability vary. 811 units have been located in Phoenix and other Arizona cities (Phoenix, Tucson, Camp Verde, Bullhead City, Page, Globe), and DDD has reported Maricopa County units of its own; one DDD presentation reported an average of about 7 months from referral to move-in (2023 data — current times may differ). DES materials state that a deposit and first month's rent are the household's responsibility for Section 811 properties, with monthly rent at about 30% of combined household income. The program was documented as active in a November 2025 tenant-selection plan; confirm current details with ADOH or DDD.

phone: 602-771-1000

address: 1110 W. Washington St., Phoenix, AZ 85007

website: https://housing.az.gov/

hours: Public questions line: Monday–Friday, 8:00 a.m.–5:00 p.m.; affordable-housing search call center (877-428-8844): Monday–Friday, 7:00 a.m.–6:00 p.m. MST

AHCCCS Housing and Health Opportunities (H2O) Demonstration — Solari Program Administrator

b03a-h2o-solari · usable

Medicaid-funded housing program (H2O demonstration) for AHCCCS members who are homeless, have a Serious Mental Illness designation, and have a chronic health condition or a correctional release within 90 days. Solari is the statewide program administrator; enrollment runs through a data-driven referral process rather than a public application.

**Services Offered**
Outreach and education; transitional housing for up to 6 months (enhanced shelter settings and apartment/rental units with rental assistance); pre-tenancy and tenancy services (housing care plan, help finding and keeping housing); one-time transition and move-in costs; short-term rental assistance (up to 6 months, capped at 110% of fair market rent); and medically necessary home accessibility modifications. Program materials describe financial caps of up to $7,000 per federal fiscal year across utility arrears (up to 2 months, max $1,000), move-in assistance (max $3,000) and eviction prevention (up to 2 months of rent, max $3,000), with accessibility modifications up to $5,000 over the life of the demonstration. Rental assistance and move-in/eviction-prevention payments are administered by the program's Statewide Housing Administrator — the Arizona Behavioral Health Corporation (ABC), with HOM, Inc. handling housing functions and payments.

**Eligibility Requirements**
Must be an AHCCCS (Medicaid) member; must be experiencing homelessness or housing instability verified against the HUD definition (24 CFR 91.5); must have a Serious Mental Illness (SMI) designation; and must either have a chronic health condition or be in a correctional facility with release scheduled within 90 days, or released from a correctional facility within the last 90 days. Adults 18+.

**Population Served**
AHCCCS members with an SMI designation who are homeless, including those with chronic health conditions and people leaving incarceration; statewide, including Mesa and Maricopa County.

**How to Best Connect**
Solari H2O Support Line: toll-free 1-855-814-4673; local 480-546-7135; email H2Omembersupport@solari-inc.org. Hours: Monday–Friday, 8:00 a.m.–4:00 p.m. MST (voicemail after hours). Callers can ask about eligibility and referrals; Solari also publishes an H2O provider contact list (organizations' service areas and services) from its Member Services page. Clients should also tell their AHCCCS health plan, behavioral-health home/provider or case manager; AHCCCS asks health homes to keep charts current with housing-related Z codes, SMI designation and chronic-condition diagnosis so members appear in the referral file, and members can ask their plan for a Housing Liaison or Housing Specialist. Note: AHCCCS states external referrals are not being accepted at this time; enrollment starts from AHCCCS's data-driven referral list plus Solari's eligibility verification, so a client cannot self-enroll by applying.

**Important Information to Know**
No member copayments or fees are described — H2O is Medicaid-funded. Services are time-limited (transitional housing/rental assistance up to 6 months) and capacity is limited ("as resources are available"), so call Solari for current status. Grievances or appeals about H2O services can go to the Solari support line (same numbers), email H2Oappeals@solari-inc.org, or use the online appeal portal (h2oappealsubmission.solari-inc.org).

phone: 1-855-814-4673 (local 480-546-7135)

website: https://community.solari-inc.org/h2o/

hours: H2O Support Line: Monday–Friday, 8:00 a.m.–4:00 p.m. MST (voicemail after hours; closed federal/AHCCCS holidays)

resolutionReason: Retained as usable after a 2026-09-28 review pass: eligibility, service list, financial caps ($7,000/FFY; $1,000 utility arrears; $3,000 move-in; $5,000 accessibility; rental assistance up to 110% FMR) and the "external referrals are not being accepted" rule were re-confirmed on Solari and AHCCCS sources; the appeals route was corrected to H2Oappeals@solari-inc.org / the appeal portal, the Statewide Housing Administrator identified (ABC/HOM), and the FAQ source refreshed.

Sources: AHCCCS — Housing and Health Opportunities (H2O) Demonstration · Solari — H2O Frequently Asked Questions (current, Jan. 2026) · Solari — H2O Operation Manual V2 (rev. Sept. 1, 2025; eligibility, caps, SHA roles) · Solari — H2O Community Support Network (Program Administrator site) · Solari — H2O Member Services (support line, provider contact list, appeals) · AHCCCS — H2O services (FFS provider manual ch. 29; utility arrears $1,000 / move-in $3,000 / modifications $5,000) · AHCCCS — H2O services fee schedule / rate methodology (rental assistance up to 110% FMR)

Consider this: Adds H2O's housing and health supports for SMI members, including people leaving jail - a reentry-linked pathway absent from the starter set.
