Arizona Recovery Housing Association (AzRHA) — certified recovery residence search

b03a-azrha · usable

Statewide association of certified sober-living and halfway-house providers. Its website offers a search for certified recovery residences across Arizona, including the Phoenix metro area. It does not operate homes or take applications; each residence sets its own fees and rules.

**Services Offered**
Statewide certification and annual inspections of sober-living and halfway houses; a public "Search Homes" tool on its website for finding certified recovery residences; posted quality standards, code of ethics and a grievance process; and education, training and advocacy for recovery housing. The association works with the Arizona Department of Health Services, the Department of Corrections and local jurisdictions.

**Eligibility Requirements**
AzRHA has no client eligibility rules — it is an association, not a housing provider. Each residence screens its own applicants (for example, sobriety expectations, house rules, gender-specific homes) and sets its own fees, and homes are required by AzRHA standards to disclose all fees and rules in writing before move-in.

**Population Served**
Adults in recovery from substance use who are looking for structured sober-living or halfway-house housing. Certified homes are statewide, from Yuma to Flagstaff, with the largest concentrations in the Phoenix and Tucson metro areas.

**How to Best Connect**
Use the "Search Homes" page at https://www.myazrha.org/home-search. Contact AzRHA by email at membership@myazrha.org; corporate mailing address: 1940 W Chandler Blvd, Suite 2-215, Chandler, AZ 85224. No public phone number is listed on the current site. If the search page does not display results, email AzRHA to ask about currently certified homes; each home must then be contacted directly about openings and cost.

**Important Information to Know**
AzRHA certification is a private, voluntary credential separate from the Arizona Department of Health Services license that sober living homes must hold; a home's current certification can be checked through the site's search tool. Costs (weekly/monthly fees), bed availability, insurance or payment options, and admission rules are set by each residence and are not published by AzRHA — ask each home directly. Certification does not guarantee a bed or eligibility. AzRHA operates a grievance statement for concerns about certified homes.

address: 1940 W Chandler Blvd, Suite 2-215, Chandler, AZ 85224 (corporate mailing address)

website: https://www.myazrha.org/

resolutionReason: Retained as usable after a 2026-09-28 review pass: current name, site, certified-home search, annual inspections, standards, grievance channel, email and mailing address were re-confirmed (still no public phone), and the licensing-separation statement is corroborated by the ADHS sober-living fact sheet, now attached as a source. It remains a certification/search resource, not a housing provider; per-home fees and availability are correctly stated as unknown.

Sources: Arizona Recovery Housing Association (AzRHA) — About · AzRHA — Find Certified Recovery Housing / Search Homes · AzRHA — Quality & Operational Standards (certification, grievance, fee disclosure) · NARR — Arizona Recovery Housing Association affiliate listing · Arizona DHS — Sober Living Homes licensing fact sheet (AzRHA certification is a recognized certifying organization)

Consider this: Adds a certified recovery-residence search for clients needing sober housing - the starter set includes no recovery-housing pathway.

Identity judgment: Certification body and search tool; not a provider; per-home fees unknown.

AZCEND — East Valley shelter (I-HELP), rent & utility assistance, senior home sharing

b03a-azcend · usable

East Valley nonprofit (formerly the Chandler Christian Community Center, renamed AZCEND in 2017) that runs nightly emergency shelter (I-HELP) for adults, rent/utility/mortgage and eviction-prevention assistance, and a Senior Home Sharing program. Rent/utility help covers Chandler, Sun Lakes, Queen Creek and Gilbert; Senior Home Sharing includes Mesa, Tempe, Chandler and Gilbert.

**Services Offered**
I-HELP shelter: safe shelter, a hot meal and a shower every night for adults experiencing homelessness, with one-on-one case management that includes job placement, medical referrals, bus passes, SNAP and AHCCCS benefits help, and housing assistance. Community Action Program (CAP): rent, water/gas/electric, mortgage and eviction-prevention financial assistance, including the Maricopa County "HSRUA3" program (up to one month of past-due rent plus late fees). Gilbert Utility Discount Program: a $30/month water-bill discount for income-eligible Gilbert residents. East Valley Senior Home Sharing (a partnership between Aster Aging, AZCEND and Tempe Community Action Agency): matches adults 50+ who have a home with those looking for one so they can share living costs, serving Chandler, Gilbert, Mesa and Tempe. AZCEND also runs a food bank, heat relief, showers/laundry, a Family Resource Center and senior centers.

**Eligibility Requirements**
I-HELP: adults experiencing homelessness; an interview is required before entering, so call for bed availability. CAP rent/utility aid: residents of Chandler, Sun Lakes and Queen Creek (Chandler office) or Gilbert (Gilbert office) only; HSRUA3 prioritizes households at or below 200% of the federal poverty level, and applicants who already received Emergency Rental Assistance rent payments are not eligible (utility-only ERA recipients are eligible). Documents required include photo ID, current lease, proof of residence, 30 days of income records, and evidence of hardship or housing instability; the landlord must provide a Maricopa County vendor code on the Landlord Verification Form or complete a W-9. Senior Home Sharing: age 50+, able to care for oneself, able to cover basic living expenses, pass a background check, and provide proof of income. Gilbert utility discount: Gilbert water account holder with household income at or below 120% of area median income.

**Population Served**
Adults experiencing homelessness (I-HELP); households at risk of eviction or utility shut-off in Chandler, Sun Lakes, Queen Creek and Gilbert; and seniors 50+ across Chandler, Gilbert, Mesa and Tempe (home sharing). Mesa residents are not eligible for the CAP rent/utility assistance but are included in the Senior Home Sharing program; call to confirm whether a Mesa adult can be served by I-HELP.

**How to Best Connect**
I-HELP shelter: call Valentina Garcia at 480-963-1423 x112 to schedule the required interview and check bed availability. Rent/utilities: Chandler/Sun Lakes/Queen Creek — 480-963-1423 x126 or cap@azcend.org; Gilbert — 480-892-5331 x200 or cap@azcend.org. Senior Home Sharing: (480) 389-1284, info@evseniorhomesharing.org or evseniorhomesharing.org. Main office: 345 S California St, Chandler, AZ 85225; (480) 963-1423; info@azcend.org; azcend.org. Admin hours Monday–Friday 7:30 a.m.–4 p.m.

**Important Information to Know**
Mesa residents are not eligible for AZCEND's CAP rent/utility assistance (Chandler, Sun Lakes, Queen Creek and Gilbert only), but the Senior Home Sharing program specifically includes Mesa. HSRUA3 pays up to one month of past-due rent — it is not an ongoing subsidy. No fees are mentioned for the shelter or assistance programs. Capacity, waitlists and current funding are not published, so call before sending a client. AZCEND's current program list does not include an open rapid-rehousing program; its housing help is the shelter/case-management and rent/utility programs above.

phone: (480) 963-1423

address: 345 S California St, Chandler, AZ 85225 (main office; Gilbert CAP office: 132 W Bruce Ave, Gilbert, AZ 85233)

website: https://azcend.org/

hours: Admin/CAP (Chandler): Mon–Fri 7:30 a.m.–4 p.m., Tue until 6 p.m. CAP (Gilbert): Mon 7:30 a.m.–6 p.m., Tue–Thu 7:30 a.m.–5 p.m., Fri 8:30 a.m.–noon

Arizona Complete Health Avondale Resource Center (city resource center & coordinated entry access point)

b03b-avondale-resource-center · usable

A city-run family and community resource center in Avondale (Southwest Valley) that is also a Maricopa County coordinated entry location for adults and families who are homeless or at risk, and the intake site for the Southwest Valley IHELP shelter program. Staff assess situations, connect people to shelter and housing options, and provide application and referral help; services are not limited to Avondale residents.

**Services Offered**
Housing help at this center is through coordinated entry and referrals rather than its own shelter. It is listed as a Maricopa County coordinated entry location for adults and families experiencing homelessness: staff interview people about their situation, connect them to appropriate services, and discuss shelter options and a housing plan. It is also the intake site for the Southwest Valley IHELP (Interfaith Homeless Emergency Lodging Program) — call to schedule intake — and the Maricopa County Community Action Program office for Avondale, Goodyear, Litchfield Park and Cashion, giving local access to county financial-support programs. The center also offers family and community services such as application assistance and information and referral to housing programs.

**Eligibility Requirements**
Anyone can ask for information or assistance; services are not limited to Avondale residents. Each program screens separately — coordinated entry, IHELP and county financial-assistance programs each have their own rules. Call 623-333-2703 to confirm what fits a particular situation.

**Population Served**
Families and individuals in Avondale and the Southwest Valley, including households that are homeless or at risk of homelessness; center programs also serve parents with young children. Services are available in English and Spanish.

**How to Best Connect**
Call 623-333-2703 or email resourcecenter@avondaleaz.gov, or walk in at 995 E. Riley Dr., Avondale, AZ 85323. Coordinated entry walk-in hours: Mon, Wed, Thu 8 a.m.–6 p.m.; Tue 8 a.m.–7 p.m.; Fri 9 a.m.–5 p.m.; Saturday hours are limited — call first. For Southwest Valley IHELP intake, call 623-333-2703; intake windows are listed as Mon–Tue 8 a.m.–4 p.m., Wed 8 a.m.–noon, Thu–Fri 9 a.m.–noon. For Avondale street outreach/homeless services, call 623-333-2713 or email SONAR@AvondaleAZ.gov.

**Important Information to Know**
This is an access and assessment point, not a shelter or landlord — a visit does not guarantee a bed, rent money or a unit, and availability and waitlists change. The center is now the Arizona Complete Health Avondale Resource Center at 995 E. Riley Dr.; older listings show the Care1st name, a 328 W. Western Ave. address and the number 623-333-2700 — use 623-333-2703. There is no fee to ask for help; individual programs set their own rules. The center is in the Southwest Valley, a long trip from Mesa — ask whether a phone assessment or a nearer entry point can start the process before traveling.

My Sisters' Place — confidential domestic-violence shelter (Catholic Charities Community Services)

b03b-my-sisters-place-dv-shelter · usable

A confidential emergency shelter in the East Valley for people and families fleeing domestic violence, operated by Catholic Charities Community Services. All services are free, and the 24-hour hotline 480-821-1024 is the intake and availability line.

**Services Offered**
Confidential emergency shelter for people and families fleeing domestic violence: a private room (families), food and access to a full kitchen, shared laundry and recreational space, and staffing 24 hours a day. Residents get an individual service plan and weekly case management; individual and group counseling is offered to adults, children are encouraged to attend counseling, and staff help with replacing IDs and documents, enrolling children in school, employment and resume help, and applying for state assistance. Staff also refer residents to outside services such as emergency housing and housing assistance, medical and dental care, transportation and clothing. The shelter has a maximum of 30 beds in 11 rooms (nine rooms can accommodate families of various sizes; two are single-occupancy).

**Eligibility Requirements**
For people and families fleeing abuse. All services are free for clients. Space is limited and changes day to day; the hotline will confirm whether there is room and can suggest alternatives if the shelter is full.

**Population Served**
Women and men, and parents with children, escaping abuse; individuals without children may share a room with another adult. The shelter is in the East Valley and serves domestic-violence emergencies across the area, including Mesa.

**How to Best Connect**
Call the My Sisters' Place 24-hour hotline: 480-821-1024 — it is the intake and availability line, and the shelter's location stays confidential. Related Catholic Charities domestic-violence services: Pathways counseling intake 602-749-4405; Pathways victim advocacy 602-819-0082 or 480-267-5761. Backup 24/7 help: National Domestic Violence Hotline 1-800-799-7233; Arizona Coalition Against Domestic Violence 602-279-2900 or 1-800-782-6400; 211 Arizona / community information and referral 602-263-8900 or 1-800-799-7739.

**Important Information to Know**
Services are free and the location is confidential to protect survivors. My Sisters' Place is one of only two domestic-violence shelters in the East Valley. Mesa's human-services funding records list this program among funded services ("My Sister's Place: Family-Based Healing for Survivors of Domestic Violence," FY 2025/26; "My Sister's Place/Pathways," FY 2026/27), but the City does not arrange placements — admission is only through the 24-hour hotline. Length of stay is not published — an older community guide cited up to 120 days — so ask the hotline about length of stay and current bed availability. Some listings spell the name "My Sister's Place"; it is the same Catholic Charities shelter. If someone is in immediate danger, call 911.

phone: 480-821-1024 (24-hour hotline)

website: https://www.catholiccharitiesaz.org/domestic-violence

hours: 24 hours a day — the shelter is staffed and the hotline answered around the clock.

resolutionReason: Verified against Catholic Charities Arizona's current domestic-violence page: free, confidential East Valley shelter, 30 beds, staffed 24 hours, with the 24-hour hotline 480-821-1024 as the intake and availability route. Remaining unknowns: current length of stay and same-day bed availability — only the hotline can confirm. Checked the similarly named CeCe's Hope Center — it is a separate organization (sex-trafficking survivors, Tempe/Chandler), so there is no identity conflict. Central Arizona Shelter Services (CASS) — Single Adult Shelter (main campus)

b03b-cass-single-adult-shelter · usable

Arizona's largest single-adult emergency shelter, run by Central Arizona Shelter Services on the Human Services Campus in Phoenix. Open 24/7/365 with same-day placement subject to availability; single adults 18+ access it through the Brian Garcia Welcome Center (602-229-5155), and current bed availability can be checked by calling 211.

**Services Offered**
Emergency shelter for single adults: a low-barrier bed, showers, restrooms and hygiene supplies, meals, case management and housing navigation, and connections to healthcare, public benefits and employment services. On-campus help includes mail service, help obtaining IDs and enrolling in benefits, behavioral-health and recovery referrals, and veterans case management. The shelter houses up to about 600 men and women at a time and is open around the clock.

**Eligibility Requirements**
Single adults (men and women) age 18 and older who are experiencing homelessness. Intake is described as low-barrier. Policies to confirm with the Welcome Center: guests must be able to manage their daily activities; pets are allowed with a county license and vaccination record; registered sex offenders are not accepted. There is no fee.

**Population Served**
Single adults 18 and older of all backgrounds, including young adults, seniors and veterans; services available in English and Spanish.

**How to Best Connect**
Call the Brian Garcia Welcome Center at 602-229-5155 (the number CASS and Maricopa County list) — CASS says intakes and information are obtained only by calling this number — or go in person to the Welcome Center on Key Campus, 204 S. 12th Ave. (some listings show 206 S. 12th Ave.), Phoenix, AZ 85007, to complete intake and ask to be directed to the CASS shelter at 230 S. 12th Ave. Keys to Change, which operates the Welcome Center, also lists a call-ahead line at 602-883-4509. Placement is same-day and subject to availability; to check coordinated shelter bed availability, call 211. Bring a photo ID and medications if possible; help obtaining replacements is available. Families with children should call the Family Housing Hub through 211; adults 55+ can ask about The Haven. Main CASS line: 602-256-6945.

**Important Information to Know**
Shelter is free, and beds cannot be reserved by phone — demand is high, so call 211 or the Welcome Center to check availability before traveling from Mesa. Welcome Center phone listings vary slightly (CASS and Maricopa County: 602-229-5155; Keys to Change call-ahead: 602-883-4509; some older guides: 602-299-5155) — if one number doesn't connect, try the other. Some community guides describe a 90-day shelter stay; CASS's current pages do not state a fixed length of stay — ask at intake. One CASS web page shows a mistyped "604" phone number; the correct main line is 602-256-6945. The shelter is on a Central Phoenix campus; plan transportation.

phone: 602-229-5155 (Welcome Center intake) / 602-256-6945 (main)

address: 230 S. 12th Ave., Phoenix, AZ 85007 (intake campus: 204 S. 12th Ave.; some listings show 206 S. 12th Ave.)

website: https://www.cassaz.org/get-help

hours: Shelter open 24 hours a day, 365 days a year; call for current intake windows.

resolutionReason: Verified against CASS's current services, get-help and contact pages: intake via the Brian Garcia Welcome Center 602-229-5155, same-day placement subject to availability, 24/7/365 operation, capacity up to about 600, no fee. Central Arizona Shelter Services (CASS) — Rapid Re-Housing & Prevention (housing programs)

b03b-cass-rapid-rehousing-prevention · usable

CASS's housing programs (Rapid Re-Housing & Prevention) help individuals and families who are homeless or at risk of homelessness secure or keep housing, with short-term rent and utility help, deposits, case management, landlord mediation and eviction prevention. Enrollment goes through Maricopa County's coordinated entry system — call 2-1-1.

**Services Offered**
CASS's housing programs (Rapid Re-Housing & Prevention) provide short-term rental and utility assistance, security-deposit support, case management and tenancy-skills training, landlord mediation and eviction prevention, benefits enrollment and budgeting support, and housing navigation. They are designed to help individuals and families secure housing quickly or avoid returning to homelessness, and CASS runs them with Maricopa County's Coordinated Entry System.

**Eligibility Requirements**
Households experiencing homelessness or at risk of homelessness (including people facing eviction). Enrollment is through a coordinated entry assessment, and help depends on program eligibility, available funding and capacity — call 2-1-1 to start. No fee is charged to clients; the assistance itself is time-limited and funding-dependent.

**Population Served**
Single adults and families, including households with children, across Maricopa County.

**How to Best Connect**
Call 2-1-1 or 877-211-8661, or visit 211Arizona.org, to ask for housing and rental assistance and a coordinated entry assessment. If you are already in a CASS shelter or working with a CASS case manager, ask about rapid re-housing directly. General CASS line: 602-256-6945; if facing eviction, see AZEvictionHelp.org. CASS asks that its website contact form not be used for housing or rental requests.

**Important Information to Know**
An assessment does not guarantee rent help or housing — funds and openings change, so 2-1-1 can say what is currently available. Earlier materials (a 2021 state presentation) used the name "Flexible Housing Assistance"; the current public name is "Rapid Re-Housing & Prevention" — use that when asking. The program serves both individuals and families; shelter clients should loop in their case manager early.

phone: 2-1-1 / 877-211-8661 (coordinated entry); 602-256-6945 (CASS main)

address: 230 S. 12th Ave., Phoenix, AZ 85007 (CASS campus; housing intake by phone/coordinated entry)

website: https://www.cassaz.org/services

hours: No posted hours — access by phone/assessment; call 2-1-1.

resolutionReason: Verified against CASS's current services and contact pages: Rapid Re-Housing & Prevention exists with rental/utility assistance, deposit support, case management and landlord mediation, accessed through coordinated entry (2-1-1 / 877-211-8661). The 2021 'Flexible Housing Assistance' name is historical and no longer used publicly. Remaining unknown: funding and openings fluctuate — 2-1-1 can confirm what is currently available. Reviewer (2026-09-28): re-confirmed program name, service list and coordinated-entry routing on cassaz.org.

The Haven (Central Arizona Shelter Services) - emergency shelter for adults 55+

b04a-cass-the-haven · usable

The Haven is an emergency shelter exclusively for adults 55 and older, operated by Central Arizona Shelter Services (CASS) in the I-17/Northern area of Phoenix (reported street address 8152 N. Black Canyon Hwy). It provides 130 private, non-congregate units (used for single or double occupancy, up to about 170 beds) plus case management, meals, help connecting to medical and behavioral health care, dementia screening, and housing navigation toward permanent housing. Placement has been described as referral-based - call CASS or 211 to ask about current intake.

**Services Offered**
- Emergency shelter exclusively for adults 55 and older, with 130 private, non-congregate units (used for single or double occupancy) and age- and accessibility-informed design.
- Wraparound case management, with low reported caseloads (about one case manager per 15 residents).
- Connections to medical care, behavioral health services, and public benefits; dementia/cognitive screening described at check-in, and a memory clinic announced for clients experiencing cognitive decline.
- Nutritious meals served daily, including lunch and dinner; reporting has described a communal kitchen and meals provided with St. Vincent de Paul.
- Specialized housing navigation to help residents secure safe, affordable long-term housing.
- Additional wrap-around supports have included help with legal issues and pet support, including medication for residents' animals.

**Eligibility Requirements**
- Adults ages 55 and older who are experiencing homelessness; CASS describes a focus on older adults facing medical, mobility, and housing challenges.
- Placement has been described as referral-based (from CASS's downtown single adult shelter, first responders, or other agencies) rather than direct from the street. CASS materials also say its shelters operate at full capacity, so a bed is not guaranteed.
- Detailed intake criteria, documents, and priority rules are not published; call CASS at 602-256-6945 or 211 to confirm eligibility and the current intake process.

**Population Served**
- Older adults (55+) experiencing homelessness in Phoenix and the greater Maricopa County area.
- Residents commonly have chronic health conditions or mobility limitations; reporting describes COPD, heart disease, and behavioral health needs such as PTSD, and CASS screens for cognitive decline.

**How to Best Connect**
- Call CASS at 602-256-6945 (TTY 800-367-8939) to ask about The Haven, current referral/intake steps, and availability.
- To check coordinated shelter bed availability, call 211 (211arizona.org); 211 can also explain Maricopa County's coordinated entry process for homeless services.
- Location: I-17 and Northern in Phoenix (reported street address 8152 N. Black Canyon Hwy, Phoenix, AZ 85051; near 27th Avenue and Northern). Call before going so staff can confirm whether arrival is by referral and what the current steps are.

**Important Information to Know**
- Cost: CASS shelter services are provided at no cost to residents; confirm current details when calling.
- The Haven opened in January 2025 in a renovated former Phoenix Inn (grand opening celebrated Jan. 16, 2025) and is described as Arizona's first emergency shelter designed exclusively for adults 55+.
- Stays are generally around 90 days; CASS has said that is not a hard stop, and extensions have been allowed when medical needs require more time. Confirm current stay limits.
- Capacity figures have been reported differently (130 private units; up to about 170 beds when units are used for double occupancy), and a 2026 financing announcement said the project serves more than 500 people annually. The shelter has been reported full, so availability can change day to day.
- No walk-in hours are published, and placement has been described as referral-based, not direct from the street; call first and ask about the current process.

phone: 602-256-6945; TTY 800-367-8939

address: I-17 and Northern (reported street address 8152 N. Black Canyon Hwy), Phoenix, AZ 85051

website: https://www.cassaz.org/services

hours: Emergency shelter operates year-round; no walk-in hours published, and placement has been described as referral-based - call 602-256-6945 (TTY 800-367-8939) or 211 to confirm intake and availability.

resolutionReason: Retained as usable. Identity, 55+ scope, location (I-17/Northern; reported 8152 N. Black Canyon Hwy), January 2025 opening, 130 private units with up to about 170 beds, and current operation are confirmed by CASS's live site plus 2025-2026 sources (Arizona PBS, Banner Health, AZ Big Media, KTAR, Arizona Republic). Vista Colina Family Shelter (Central Arizona Shelter Services)

b04a-cass-vista-colina · usable

Vista Colina is CASS's emergency family shelter in north Phoenix for parents and children experiencing homelessness. Families get private units and family-centered case management, a food pantry and clothing closet, school enrollment support for children, and housing navigation; access is through Maricopa County's family Coordinated Entry and waitlist (CASS directs families to call the Family Housing Hub at 211 first).

**Services Offered**
- Emergency shelter for families in north Phoenix: 36 private family units (CASS's current count; December 2024 reporting after the renovation described 30 units - 12 one-bedroom and 18 two-bedroom - housing up to 30 families and 130+ people).
- Individualized, family-centered case management addressing the goals of parents, children, and the household.
- Food pantry and clothing closet; on-site amenities have included a playground, laundry facilities, and common areas.
- Support with school enrollment and transportation so children stay connected to school.
- Housing navigation and landlord engagement toward safe, stable, long-term housing.
- Children's programming focused on education, health, and emotional and behavioral well-being.

**Eligibility Requirements**
- Families with children who are experiencing homelessness (reporting describes families with children under 18).
- Placement is not immediate: CASS directs families to call the Family Housing Hub at 211 first for assessment and referral to Vista Colina or other family options; after assessment, families are added to a countywide waitlist and shelters contact the next eligible families as units open.
- Confirm current criteria, required documents, and availability with 211 or CASS at 602-256-6945.

**Population Served**
- Parents and children experiencing homelessness in Maricopa County, including larger families (earlier reporting described capacity for up to 30 families and 130+ people, including children).

**How to Best Connect**
- Call 211 first and ask for the Family Housing Hub (Maricopa County's family Coordinated Entry) for assessment and referral to Vista Colina or other family shelter options.
- CASS main line for questions: 602-256-6945 (TTY 800-367-8939).
- Maricopa County's family Coordinated Entry point has historically been the Family Housing Hub at 3307 E. Van Buren St., Phoenix (602-595-8700; walk-in hours have been listed as Monday through Thursday, 8 a.m. to 4 p.m.); confirm current details with 211, because processes can change.
- Location: 1050 W. Mountain View Rd., Phoenix, AZ 85021 (north Phoenix, near 11th Avenue and Mountain View Road).

**Important Information to Know**
- Cost: CASS family shelter services are provided at no cost to residents.
- Availability is not guaranteed: family shelter space in Maricopa County is in high demand, families are placed from a countywide waitlist, and reporting has described hundreds of families waiting.
- Stay limits are reported differently: CASS's July 2026 article says emergency family shelter stays are now up to 30 days (raised from 14); a 2026 12News report described Vista Colina as sheltering 36 families at a time in private apartments for up to six months; an older directory listed up to 90 days. Confirm current stay limits with staff.
- Unit counts also differ (36 now listed by CASS vs. 30 after the December 2024 renovation), so confirm current capacity when you call.
- The shelter reopened in December 2024 after renovations, with new children's programming added.

phone: 602-256-6945; TTY 800-367-8939

address: 1050 W. Mountain View Rd., Phoenix, AZ 85021

website: https://www.cassaz.org/services

hours: Family shelter operates year-round; placement is through Maricopa County family Coordinated Entry and a waitlist - call 211 (ask for the Family Housing Hub) or CASS at 602-256-6945 for current intake steps.

resolutionReason: Retained as usable. CASS's live pages confirm Vista Colina at 1050 W. Mountain View Rd. with private family units (36 listed now; December 2024 reporting described 30 units - 12 one-bedroom + 18 two-bedroom - and 130+ people), family services, and the current family intake route (call the Family Housing Hub at 211 first for assessment and referral). De Colores (Chicanos Por La Causa) - domestic and sexual violence shelter and services

b04a-cplc-decolores · usable

De Colores is Chicanos Por La Causa's domestic and sexual violence program, offering a 24-hour hotline and emergency shelter at a confidential central Phoenix location, plus community-based services with case management, safety planning, legal and housing support, support groups, and youth programs. It serves survivors of domestic violence, sexual assault, and human trafficking - singles and families, with or without custodial children - across Maricopa County; services are free and the program is bilingual and bicultural.

**Services Offered**
- 24-hour domestic violence hotline: 602-269-1515.
- Emergency shelter at a confidential central Phoenix location, with case management, legal support, housing and employment assistance, support groups, transportation, youth services, and referrals; on-site spaces include a meditation room, a garden, and a youth area.
- Community-based services for survivors who are not ready for shelter or who are transitioning out: crisis intervention, safety planning, legal and housing support, support groups, art classes, youth programs, financial empowerment, and transitional and rapid rehousing programs.
- Legal support includes assistance with protection orders, immigration and visa paperwork, and court accompaniment (per program listings).
- Sexual assault services (per program listings).
- Pet program: De Colores reports providing a safe haven for survivors and their pets (shelter, grooming, vet care, food, and supplies); pets are accepted at the shelter only in limited capacity.
- De Colores reports serving 961 clients in its most recent reporting year and says it is one of 7 domestic violence shelters in Maricopa County.

**Eligibility Requirements**
- Survivors of domestic violence, sexual assault, or human trafficking, with or without custodial children; CPLC describes the program as serving singles and families (per 211 Arizona and CPLC's shelter list).
- Services are described as free (211 lists no fee). The program is fully bilingual and bicultural, with tailored services for Spanish-speaking and immigrant survivors (per program listings).
- Shelter requests: call De Colores' hotline directly. CPLC's page says the countywide SAFEDVS hotline is no longer available to request DV shelter in Maricopa County, while 211's listing (last assured 03/01/2026) still says to call the Safe DVS hotline for shelter beds - see Important Information to Know.

**Population Served**
- Survivors of domestic violence, sexual assault, and human trafficking and their children in Maricopa County (211 lists the service area as all Arizona ZIP codes), including Spanish-speaking and immigrant survivors.

**How to Best Connect**
- For immediate assistance or to request shelter, call the 24-hour hotline at 602-269-1515.
- Shelter referrals can also be emailed to DeColoresReferrals@cplc.org.
- Community-based (non-shelter) services: call 602-805-9211; location reported at Vista Village Apartments, 3541 E. Van Buren St., Phoenix, AZ 85034 (parking is on the right side of the building). Some older directories list a different community-based contact (623-247-0464, option 3, at CPLC's Maryvale center) - CPLC's current page and 211 both list 602-805-9211.
- The shelter's address is confidential and not published; access is through the hotline.

**Important Information to Know**
- Costs: none reported; services are free.
- Bed availability is not guaranteed and is not published; call the hotline each time, and if De Colores is full, ask staff about other Maricopa County DV shelters.
- Shelter-access instructions conflict: CPLC's page says SAFEDVS is no longer available to request DV shelter in Maricopa County and that survivors should call individual shelters directly, but the same page still displays a 'Maricopa County shelter hotline' number (480-890-3039), and 211's listing still says to call the Safe DVS hotline for shelter beds. Calling De Colores' hotline directly is the reliable route.
- The shelter location is kept confidential for safety; do not visit or share it without going through the hotline.
- Pets are accepted only in limited capacity; ask about pet arrangements when calling.
- Hours: hotline available 24 hours; community-based services and administration have been listed as Monday through Friday, 8 a.m. to 5 p.m. (confirm when calling).

phone: 602-269-1515 (24-hour hotline)

address: Confidential central Phoenix shelter location; community-based services at 3541 E. Van Buren St., Phoenix, AZ 85034

website: https://cplc.org/programs/domestic-violence

hours: 24-hour hotline; community-based services and administration listed as Monday-Friday, 8 a.m.-5 p.m.

Chicanos Por La Causa (CPLC) - Housing & Community Development (income-restricted affordable housing communities)

b04a-cplc-housing · usable

Chicanos Por La Causa (CPLC) develops and manages income-restricted affordable apartment communities in metro Phoenix and Mesa - including Ascent on Broadway in South Phoenix (161 apartments at 40-60% AMI, opened November 2025) and the planned Mesa Royale on W. Main St. in Mesa (89 apartments planned for families at or below 60% AMI, still in development). Applications and waitlists are handled per community; the contact currently listed for CPLC's affordable-housing/real-estate division is Tiempo, Inc. (602-252-0482).

**Services Offered**
- CPLC's real estate/Housing & Community Development division develops, owns, and manages income-restricted affordable rental communities in metro Phoenix and Mesa, including Low-Income Housing Tax Credit (LIHTC) properties; CPLC's affordable-housing page reports its multifamily properties are home to 3,471 residents, plus 35 families in single-family homes, and seven senior complexes.
- Mesa example: Mesa Royale on West Main Street in Mesa (630-668 W. Main St., between N. Country Club Dr. and N. Alma School Rd.) - a redevelopment that plans 89 affordable apartment units for families at or below 60% of Area Median Income, plus for-sale townhomes (Nuevas Vistas: 56 townhomes for first-time buyers at or below 80% AMI; 10 completed as of March 2025). CPLC planned to seek 2026 LIHTC funding; construction timing had not been announced as of March 2025.
- Phoenix example: Ascent on Broadway in South Phoenix - 161 affordable apartments (10 studios, 26 one-bedroom, 86 two-bedroom, 39 three-bedroom) near the light rail at Central Avenue, reserved for households at or below 40%, 50%, or 60% AMI; 140 units were already occupied when it opened in November 2025.
- CPLC also offers related housing programs (housing counseling, home lending, homelessness services); ask about the right program when contacting CPLC.

**Eligibility Requirements**
- Households must meet each community's income limits and screening rules; rent bands are set by program rules (for example, LIHTC rents; the 40/50/60% AMI bands at Ascent on Broadway; the planned at-or-below-60% AMI apartments at Mesa Royale).
- Income limits vary by property, unit size, and household size, and each community has its own application and waitlist; no single CPLC-wide waitlist is described publicly.

**Population Served**
- Low- and moderate-income individuals and families in metro Phoenix and Mesa who need affordable apartments; specific communities may target particular income bands (Ascent on Broadway includes family-sized two- and three-bedroom units; Mesa Royale is planned as family affordable housing; CPLC also reports operating seven senior complexes).
- CPLC states its services are offered to all people regardless of ethnic background while maintaining special competence serving the Latino community.

**How to Best Connect**
- Start with Tiempo, Inc., the contact listed for CPLC's affordable-housing/real-estate division: call 602-252-0482; office at 1008 E. Buckeye Road, Suite #100, Phoenix, AZ 85034; tiempoinc.com. CPLC's main line is 602-257-0700 if you need routing.
- For Ascent on Broadway, ask CPLC/Tiempo about current leasing and applications; for Mesa Royale, ask about the development timeline and future leasing - it is not yet a confirmed open community.
- CPLC's 2024 Mesa project filings listed a project contact (Evelyn.Guerrero@cplc.org, 602-257-6727) for the Mesa development; that filing is from 2024 and may be out of date - the Tiempo contact is the current route listed on CPLC's site.
- Ask specifically about which communities have open waitlists, current rents and deposits, income limits, and application steps.

**Important Information to Know**
- Rents are income-restricted and set by program rules - below market but not uniform; exact rents, deposits, and utility allowances must be confirmed with each property.
- Waitlists and current availability were not verified for any CPLC community, and new communities open periodically; ask about both existing communities and upcoming openings.
- Mesa Royale is still in development: as of March 2025, CPLC planned to apply for 2026 LIHTC funding and construction timing had not been announced; a 2024 City of Mesa plan-of-operation filing described planned weekday business hours and management by Tiempo, but there is no confirmed leasing office yet.
- Name caution: a separate, unrelated 'Mesa Royale Apartments' at 1825 N. Stapley Dr. in Mesa is an existing 55+ community - CPLC's Mesa Royale is the West Main Street redevelopment.
- The general phone line may route through a switchboard; the Tiempo contact above is the division contact listed on CPLC's current affordable-housing page.
- Eligibility and unit availability are determined by the property and are not guaranteed.

Child Crisis Arizona · Group home for teen boys in foster care (ages 11–17, Mesa)

b04b-cca-group-home · usable

A Mesa group home run by Child Crisis Arizona that provides around-the-clock care for teenage boys in the foster care system (currently described as ages 11–17), including housing, meals, supervision and coaching in life skills. Placement is arranged through the child-welfare system rather than by walk-in, so families and caseworkers should call Child Crisis Arizona's main line to ask about referrals.

**Services Offered**
Around-the-clock (24/7) residential group-home care for teenage boys in the foster care system: a stable home with supervision and meals, physical and mental health support, and life-skills guidance to prepare for adulthood. Child Crisis Arizona describes the home as providing wraparound care while the boys are in its program.

**Eligibility Requirements**
Teenage boys in the foster care system; Child Crisis Arizona's current program page says ages 11–17 (older materials have listed 10–15, 10–18 or 11–18 over the years, so confirm the current range). Placements are arranged through the child-welfare system - the Arizona Department of Child Safety, Tribal Social Services or the youth's foster-care team - rather than through an open community application. Availability depends on placement need and space.

**Population Served**
Teenage boys in foster care; the home is in Mesa (City of Mesa reports describe the group home as located in Mesa and operated 24/7) and serves the greater Maricopa County child-welfare system. This is not an adult housing program and it does not accept walk-in requests for shelter.

**How to Best Connect**
Call Child Crisis Arizona at (480) 834-9424 or email info@childcrisisaz.org and ask about the boys' group home and how placements are made. The home's exact address is not published, and placement runs through the youth's case manager (DCS or tribal social services). If a child is in immediate danger, call 911; to report abuse or neglect, call the Arizona Child Abuse Hotline at 1-888-767-2445. Youth in crisis can text 'safe' and their location to 4HELP (44357) to be connected with a Safe Place contact.

**Important Information to Know**
This is a placement-based home, not a drop-in shelter - a caseworker referral is the normal path. Current bed capacity and openings are not published; confirm availability when you call. No cost to families is published (placements are arranged through the child-welfare system). Age-range wording varies across materials (current page says 11–17; older materials say 10–15, 10–18 or 11–18), so verify the current cutoff.

address: Mesa, AZ (exact address not published; placement is arranged through Child Crisis Arizona and child-welfare caseworkers)

Child Crisis Arizona · Emergency children's shelter — Mesa (Whiteman's Safe Haven, ages 2–10)

b04b-cca-mesa-shelter · usable

A licensed emergency shelter in Mesa for children ages 2–10 who have experienced abuse or neglect or whose families are in crisis. Children receive 24/7 care, meals, clothing, medical checks and counseling while arrangements are made; referrals come from the Arizona Department of Child Safety, Tribal Social Services, and from caregivers who need temporary safe care for a child. Call ahead about space.

**Services Offered**
A 24-hour, 7-days-a-week emergency shelter for young children: safe housing with nurturing care, balanced meals and snacks, clothing and personal-care items, medical screenings (including vaccinations and doctor or dentist visits as needed), educational support and counseling, plus enrichment activities. Child Crisis Arizona describes it as the only licensed emergency children's shelter facility for ages 2 through 10 in Maricopa County.

**Eligibility Requirements**
Children ages 2 through 10. Children are placed through referrals from the Arizona Department of Child Safety and Tribal Social Services, and also by parents or caregivers who cannot provide care and need a safe place temporarily. The program is described as open to anyone who needs help caring for their children, based on availability.

**Population Served**
Young children (ages 2–10) in Maricopa County whose families are in crisis or who have experienced abuse or neglect. It is not an adult, teen or family-housing program.

**How to Best Connect**
Call the Mesa shelter at (480) 969-2308 or Child Crisis Arizona's main line at (480) 834-9424 to ask about space and intake; email info@childcrisisaz.org for general questions. Location: 604 W 9th St, Mesa, AZ 85201 - the shelter has also been listed in community guides as 'Whiteman's Safe Haven for Children.' The shelter runs 24/7, but intake is coordinated by phone, so call before going. To report suspected abuse or neglect, call the Arizona Child Abuse Hotline at 1-888-767-2445; youth needing help can text 'safe' plus their location to 4HELP (44357).

**Important Information to Know**
The program's current community listing (findhelp, reviewed September 2026) lists the shelter as free to families; cost should not be a barrier - confirm at intake. Capacity is limited and changes daily, and child-welfare placements are prioritized. If the 480-969-2308 shelter line is not answered, use the main line (480) 834-9424. This is an emergency children's shelter, not a domestic-violence or adult shelter; staff can point elsewhere if the situation does not fit.

phone: (480) 969-2308; main line (480) 834-9424

address: 604 W 9th St, Mesa, AZ 85201

website: https://www.childcrisisaz.org/what-we-do/emergency-residential-services

hours: Open 24 hours a day, 7 days a week, year-round

Circle the City · Medical respite centers and care for adults experiencing homelessness

b04b-circle-the-city-respite · usable

Circle the City provides medical respite care in Phoenix for adults experiencing homelessness who are recovering from an acute illness, injury or surgery: a safe place to stay with medical care when someone is too sick for the street or a shelter but not sick enough to remain in a hospital. Two 50-bed centers and county-wide mobile medical teams serve Maricopa County; referrals usually come through hospitals, outreach teams or a call to intake.

**Services Offered**
Medical respite care: a safe place to stay while recovering from an acute illness, injury or surgery, with medical and nursing care, meals, physical therapy, behavioral-health support and help planning next steps. Two 50-bed centers (Downtown and Midtown Phoenix) serve adults who are too sick to recover on the street or in a shelter but not sick enough to stay in a hospital. Circle the City also runs primary-care clinics, mobile medical units and street-medicine teams across Maricopa County, plus hospital-based health navigators who connect patients to services at discharge.

**Eligibility Requirements**
Adults 18 and older who are homeless or about to become homeless, with acute or active medical needs expected to improve within about 4–6 weeks (or who need end-of-life care), and who can live in a group setting, follow facility rules and take part in their own care. Referrals typically come from a hospital or emergency-room team, community outreach workers, or family; hospital social workers and case managers can help with the process. Health insurance is not required.

**Population Served**
People experiencing homelessness in Maricopa County, including medically vulnerable adults discharged from hospitals with nowhere safe to recover. Mobile medical teams reach people living outdoors and in shelters county-wide.

**How to Best Connect**
Call Circle the City administration and intake at 602-776-0776 (Monday–Friday 8:30 a.m.–5 p.m.). Downtown Medical Respite Center: 210 S 12th Ave, Phoenix, 602-254-4282 (after-hours 602-884-4240). Midtown Medical Respite Center: 333 W Indian School Rd, Phoenix, 602-776-9000 (after-hours 480-434-2690). Ask a hospital social worker or case manager to refer directly; health navigators can be reached at 602-325-3535 or healthnavigators@circlethecity.org; mobile medical care is at 602-516-6825. Call before going - admission is coordinated, and the centers are not walk-in shelters.

**Important Information to Know**
Medical respite is free and does not require insurance, but there must be an open bed and the medical-need criteria must be met; space is limited. Stays are tied to recovery (typically weeks), not long-term housing, and discharge planning includes help finding the next place to stay. The centers are in central Phoenix, and the East Valley (including Mesa) has had no medical respite beds: Circle the City is opening an 85-bed East Valley Medical Respite Center in Mesa in fall 2026, so ask whether the new center is accepting patients yet. Confirm current referral routes and bed availability by phone; intake details can change.

phone: 602-776-0776 (administration/intake); Downtown respite 602-254-4282 (after-hours 602-884-4240); Midtown respite 602-776-9000 (after-hours 480-434-2690); mobile medical care 602-516-6825

address: Downtown Medical Respite Center: 210 S 12th Ave, Phoenix, AZ 85007; Midtown Medical Respite Center: 333 W Indian School Rd, Phoenix, AZ 85013

website: https://circlethecity.org/

hours: Administration and intake Monday–Friday 8:30 a.m.–5 p.m.; 24/7 care for admitted respite patients

City of Mesa Housing Authority · Section 8 HCV, project-based, Mainstream, VASH, FUP, EHV and TBRA/deposit assistance

b04b-mesa-housing-authority · usable

The City of Mesa's housing authority administers rental assistance for Mesa residents: Section 8 Housing Choice Vouchers, project-based vouchers, Mainstream vouchers for adults with disabilities, HUD-VASH for homeless veterans, Family Unification Program (FUP) vouchers, Emergency Housing Vouchers, and security/utility deposit help. The Section 8 wait list has been closed since 2022 (a fall 2026 reopening was reported by community trackers but could not be confirmed officially), and several programs are referral-based; call the office or use the homeless-access points to find out what is open.

**Services Offered**
Rental assistance programs for Mesa: tenant-based Section 8 Housing Choice Vouchers (help paying rent in the private market); project-based vouchers attached to La Mesita Apartments (2254 W. Main St.) and Escobedo at Verde Vista Phase II (435 N. Hibbert), which include on-site services; Mainstream vouchers for non-elderly adults with disabilities; HUD-VASH vouchers combined with VA case management for homeless veterans; Family Unification Program (FUP) vouchers; Emergency Housing Vouchers; and security/utility deposit assistance for low-income Mesa households (the city's HOME-funded tenant-based rental assistance [TBRA] deposit activity). The authority also offers a Family Self-Sufficiency program for voucher holders, and its March 2026 board presentation reported about $25.6 million administered in voucher assistance, assisting roughly 1,825 families.

**Eligibility Requirements**
Households must meet HUD income limits (generally very-low income; under current FY2026 limits, about $56,200 a year for a family of four in the Phoenix–Mesa area - confirm current limits). Project-based vouchers require proof of chronic homelessness as the city defines it (at least two episodes of shelter living plus a substantiated need for long-term case management and supportive services) and also elderly, disabled or supportive-service status. HUD-VASH requires referral through the VA. Mainstream vouchers are for non-elderly people with disabilities. Deposit assistance is income-limited (listed at up to 80% of area median income; confirm).

**Population Served**
Low- and very-low-income Mesa households - families, elderly and disabled individuals - plus homeless households and homeless veterans through HUD-VASH. The housing authority serves the City of Mesa, with homeless referrals coordinated through the Maricopa County Continuum of Care access points.

**How to Best Connect**
Call 480-644-3536 (Monday–Thursday 7:30 a.m.–5 p.m.; Spanish-language information is available at the same number; AzRelay 7-1-1 for deaf or hard-of-hearing callers). Office: 200 S. Center St., Building 1, Mesa, AZ 85210; fax 480-644-2923; mail: P.O. Box 1466, Mesa, AZ 85211; Housing Specialist visits are by appointment. The Section 8 Housing Choice Voucher wait-list application has been closed; check the city's 'Application & Wait List' page for a reopening and apply online when it opens - and note that community trackers reported a fall 2026 opening that the city had not confirmed, so verify first. Use the Applicant Portal to update your information. For homeless households seeking project-based vouchers or other homeless housing programs: adults without children can call the Brian Garcia Welcome Center at 602-229-5155; families with minor children should call the Family Housing Hub at 602-595-8700 before going in person. Veterans should ask the VA about HUD-VASH.

**Important Information to Know**
The Housing Choice Voucher waiting list has been closed since 2022, and status reports conflict: the city's page has said the application is closed, while some third-party wait-list sites reported a reopening scheduled for August 25, 2026 - confirm the current status with the office (480-644-3536) or the city's Application & Wait List page before acting. Project-based, VASH, Mainstream, FUP and Emergency Housing Vouchers are mostly referral-based with limited openings, and Emergency Housing Vouchers were a time-limited federal program - confirm current status with the office or a referral partner. Applying is free; never pay anyone to apply. If you are on a list, keep your address and phone current (the office says changes must be reported within 10 business days). Voucher holders generally pay about 30% of adjusted household income toward rent.

phone: 480-644-3536

address: 200 S. Center St., Building 1, Mesa, AZ 85210 (mailing: P.O. Box 1466, Mesa, AZ 85211)

website: https://www.mesaaz.gov/Resident-Resources/Housing

hours: Monday–Thursday 7:30 a.m.–5 p.m. (lobby; Housing Specialist visits by appointment)

resolutionReason: Verified through City of Mesa housing pages (Application & Wait List; Project-Based Voucher Program; staff contacts) and the March 26, 2026 Housing Services Governing Board presentation: the authority administers HCV/Section 8, project-based vouchers (referral only), Mainstream, HUD-VASH, Family Unification Program and Emergency Housing Vouchers, plus HOME-funded security/utility deposit assistance (TBRA activity); office at 200 S. City of Mesa Housing Authority · Security and Utility Deposit Program

b05a-mesa-security-utility-deposit · usable

One-time City of Mesa help paying the refundable parts of a security deposit and utility deposit so a low-income household can move into a rental in Mesa. Payments go directly to the landlord and the utility company. You must apply before you sign a lease or move in, and the unit has to pass an inspection.

**Services Offered**
Financial help with the refundable portion of a security deposit and, when requested with the deposit application, a utility deposit for a rental unit in Mesa. Security-deposit help is limited to two months' rent; utility-deposit help is only approved together with security-deposit help and can cover SRP, City of Mesa utilities (electric and/or gas, water, sewer or trash), or Southwest Gas accounts - the application form lists example caps (SRP up to $235; City of Mesa utilities up to $265). Payments are made directly to the landlord and the utility provider.

**Eligibility Requirements**
The prospective rental unit must be inside the City of Mesa; total household income must be at or below 80% of Area Median Income (limits published for 2025-26: $62,850 for one person, $71,800 for two, $80,800 for three, $89,750 for four, up to $118,500 for eight - ask staff for the current-year limits); the applicant must not have signed a lease or already moved in; the household must not have received City of Mesa security-deposit assistance in the last three years; the landlord and utility provider must accept the program's letter of intent; and the unit must pass an inspection before approval. Utility-deposit help requires a security-deposit application.

**Population Served**
Low-income Mesa families and individuals who are preparing to move into a rental unit in Mesa - including people who are homeless, older adults and people with disabilities (the application asks about these situations), along with other income-eligible Mesa residents.

**How to Best Connect**
Apply online through the City of Mesa Limited-Income Assistance Programs pages (mesaaz.gov, Resident Resources) or get and return a paper application at the Mesa Housing Authority office, 200 S. Center St., Building 1, Mesa, AZ 85210. Call 480-644-3536 with questions or to request a disability or language accommodation (AzRelay 7-1-1 for deaf or hard-of-hearing callers). Applications can also be faxed to 480-644-2923 or placed in the secure drop box at the office. Submit the application and documents at least 14 days before your move-in date; the 2026-27 application says to allow about 14 days for processing (older materials said 10 business days). New applications were temporarily closed as of August 5, 2026 with a planned reopening on August 25, 2026 - call first to confirm the program is accepting applications.

**Important Information to Know**
This assistance covers deposits only, not rent, and it depends on funding availability. Do not sign a lease or move in before approval - if you have already moved in or signed, you are not eligible, and the unit must pass inspection. Documents include photo ID, Social Security cards, an unsigned proposed lease showing the refundable deposit amount (no more than two months' rent), proof of income, and bank statements; utility help also requires a letter from the utility with your account number and deposit amount. If your household is not on Section 8, extra income/eligibility documents may be requested. One City document for the federally funded (HOME) version of this deposit assistance lists a lower 60% AMI limit while current program materials use 80% - ask staff which limit applies to your case.

phone: 480-644-3536

address: 200 S. Center St., Building 1, Mesa, AZ 85210

website: https://www.mesaaz.gov/Resident-Resources/Housing/Human-Services/DepositAssistanceProgram

hours: Lobby hours are published as Monday-Thursday 7:30 a.m.-5:00 p.m. (211 Arizona); Housing Specialist visits are by appointment. Confirm current hours and application status by phone - new applications were temporarily closed as of August 5, 2026 with a planned reopening on August 25, 2026.

City of Mesa - Office of Homeless Solutions · Mesa Homeless Resource Line (480-644-HOPE)

b05a-mesa-homeless-resource-line · usable

The City of Mesa's phone line for people who are experiencing homelessness in Mesa and for community members with questions or concerns. Staff answer calls, take referrals, and connect people to shelter, street outreach, and other homeless services.

**Services Offered**
A staffed navigation and referral line for homelessness in Mesa: people who are homeless (or someone calling on their behalf) can get help and be routed to shelter, street outreach and other services, and community members can ask questions or offer to help. The line is the City's public front door to the Office of Homeless Solutions. It is not itself a shelter, does not guarantee a bed or housing, and is not an emergency or crisis line.

**Eligibility Requirements**
No eligibility rules are published - the line is open to people experiencing homelessness in Mesa and to community members with questions, concerns or offers to help.

**Population Served**
People experiencing homelessness in Mesa, plus Mesa residents, businesses and others who need information about homelessness or want to help.

**How to Best Connect**
Call (480) 644-HOPE - (480) 644-4673 - Monday through Thursday (a City program document lists 7 a.m.-6 p.m.; confirm current hours when you call). For emergencies call 911; for a mental-health crisis, the Crisis Response Network is available 24/7 at 602-222-9444; for non-emergency police matters call 480-644-2211.

**Important Information to Know**
This is a referral line, not a walk-in shelter - shelter and housing options are limited and often have waiting lists. Published city materials say the line is monitored Monday-Thursday (one program document lists 7 a.m.-6 p.m.), but current hours are not posted; older city material described the line as paired with the shelter hotline for immediate assistance around the clock, so confirm hours when calling. If no one answers, try again on a weekday or use the crisis numbers above. The line does not publish an email; written questions about the Off the Streets shelter program can go to offthestreets@mesaaz.gov.

phone: (480) 644-4673

City of Mesa - Office of Homeless Solutions · Off the Streets emergency shelter (Windermere Hotel site)

b05a-mesa-off-the-streets · usable

Mesa's main emergency shelter and temporary housing program for people who are unsheltered - up to about 90 days in hotel rooms with meals, case management and a housing plan, run by the City of Mesa's Office of Homeless Solutions. Entry is only by referral; there are no walk-up beds.

**Services Offered**
Emergency shelter and temporary housing (generally up to 90 days, with extensions possible in special cases) for people who are unsheltered in Mesa: hotel rooms at the Windermere Hotel site (65 city-leased rooms / 111 beds as of 2026), meals, 24/7 on-site staffing, case management, help connecting to medical, mental-health and substance-use treatment, employment services, schools and housing programs, and help with the next housing step. The program reported an 86% positive exit rate for 2025 and is described as Mesa's only shelter for families, women and seniors. A closed campus with police presence day and night supports safety.

**Eligibility Requirements**
People experiencing homelessness in Mesa, with placement priority for the most vulnerable - families with children, single women, seniors, veterans and domestic-violence survivors. Participants must be referred (Mesa first responders, the Maricopa County Continuum of Care, or nonprofit partners) and must follow program rules: drug-free environment, 10 p.m. curfew, room checks, no unauthorized visitors, and active work on a housing plan. People who do not follow the rules or refuse services can be removed.

**Population Served**
Mesa's most vulnerable unsheltered residents. In the 12 months before May 2026 the City reported 458 people served, including 235 children, 100 seniors and 202 domestic-violence survivors; many participants live with disabilities.

**How to Best Connect**
Do not send someone to the site to ask for a bed - there are no walk-ups or drop-ins. To ask about a referral, call the Mesa Homeless Resource Line at 480-644-HOPE (480-644-4673) during Monday-Thursday business hours, or email offthestreets@mesaaz.gov. Referrals come through Mesa police, fire and park rangers, the Maricopa County Continuum of Care, and nonprofit partners; community members and agencies can call the same line about someone who needs help. Site (for enrolled participants): Windermere Hotel, 5750 E. Main St., Mesa (East Main Street and Recker Road area).

**Important Information to Know**
Capacity is limited and waits are common - in May 2026 the City reported roughly a six-week wait with about 109 single adults on the waitlist, after reducing from 85 to 65 rooms. On May 21, 2026 the City Council voted 4-2 to keep Off the Streets at the Windermere Hotel for three more years using federal funds; the city-owned Sunaire Hotel at 6733 E. Main St. is not the operating site, and the city is still working out what to do with Sunaire - selling it could require repaying about $13 million in federal and county funds - so Sunaire addresses seen elsewhere are out of date. The city selected Mercy House, a nonprofit shelter operator, to take over day-to-day operations in 2026, replacing Community Bridges, Inc.; some City pages still name Community Bridges, so confirm the current operator when you call. Because the operator, room count and referral details can change, confirm current status through the resource line before promising placement. No participant fees are published; ask staff about any costs.

phone: 480-644-4673

address: Windermere Hotel site - 5750 E. Main St. (East Main Street and Recker Road area), Mesa, AZ 85205; referral only, no walk-up services

website: https://www.mesaaz.gov/Resident-Resources/Housing/Human-Services/Office-of-Homeless-Solutions/Off-the-Streets

hours: Program operates 24/7 for enrolled participants; new placements are by referral only - call 480-644-HOPE (Mon-Thu business hours) or email for referral questions.

City of Phoenix Housing Department · Public Housing & Section 8 Housing Choice Voucher programs

b05a-phoenix-housing-department · usable

The City of Phoenix's public housing authority - one of the largest in Arizona - runs public housing apartments and the Section 8 Housing Choice Voucher program, plus special vouchers for veterans and other groups. Mesa residents can ask about applying, but the lists have their own rules and preferences, and the Section 8 waiting list is currently closed.

**Services Offered**
Long-term affordable housing and rent help through the City of Phoenix's public housing authority: (1) public housing - City-owned apartments where rent is generally about 30% of adjusted household income; (2) Section 8 Housing Choice Voucher - a subsidy paid to a private landlord of your choice, with the household paying at least 30% of its monthly income toward rent; (3) special-purpose vouchers, including HUD-VASH (903 vouchers for homeless veterans, with case management through the VA) and HOPWA housing help for people living with HIV/AIDS in Maricopa and Pinal Counties (through Care Directions, 602-264-2273). The department also manages affordable apartment communities that accept applications at each property.

**Eligibility Requirements**
HUD income limits apply - generally at or below 50% of Area Median Income for public housing and Section 8 (about $56,200 a year for a four-person household in the Phoenix-Mesa-Chandler area under FY2026 limits). At least one household member must be a U.S. citizen, national, or eligible non-citizen. Families and single people can apply, including seniors (62+) and people with disabilities. Local preferences (such as living or working in Phoenix) affect waiting-list position, and special vouchers like HUD-VASH require a referral rather than a standard application.

**Population Served**
Low- and very-low-income people in Phoenix and the surrounding area, including families, single adults, seniors, people with disabilities, veterans, people experiencing homelessness, domestic-violence survivors and people living with HIV/AIDS.

**How to Best Connect**
Call the Section 8/HCV office at 602-534-1974 (S8intake@phoenix.gov) or the Housing Department administration at 602-262-6794 (housing@phoenix.gov) to ask which waiting lists are open and how to apply. In person: 830 E. Jefferson St., Phoenix, AZ 85034 (Section 8/HCV) or 251 W. Washington St., 4th Floor, Phoenix, AZ 85003 (administration). The Section 8 (HCV) waiting list is currently closed - watch for the City's public notice when it reopens; openings are announced widely and can be brief. Homeless veterans seeking HUD-VASH should contact their local VA medical center or call 1-877-4AID-VET (877-424-3838).

**Important Information to Know**
The City's official Section 8 page says the HCV waiting list is closed. The public-housing waiting list is listed as open by multiple independent waiting-list trackers (updates in mid/late 2026), but that status is not posted on the City's website - call 602-262-6794 to confirm before telling anyone which list they can join. Waiting lists can close without notice, and applicants must keep their address, phone and household information updated or they can be removed. This is a City of Phoenix program: Mesa residents may be able to apply to some programs, but local preferences favor Phoenix residents, and voucher rules can require leasing in Phoenix at first. Federal housing applications are free - never pay anyone to apply. Special vouchers (HUD-VASH and similar) come through referrals from partner agencies.

phone: 602-534-1974 (Section 8 office) / 602-262-6794 (administration)

address: 830 E. Jefferson St., Phoenix, AZ 85034 (Section 8/HCV office); 251 W. Washington St., 4th Floor, Phoenix, AZ 85003 (administration)

website: https://www.phoenix.gov/administration/departments/housing.html

hours: Call for current hours; 211 Arizona lists office hours Monday-Friday 8 a.m.-5 p.m. for the public-housing programs.

HOPWA (Housing Opportunities for Persons with AIDS) — STRMU, Permanent Housing Placement, TBRA and supportive housing (City of Phoenix)

b05b-hopwa-city-of-phoenix · usable

City of Phoenix is the HUD HOPWA grantee for the Maricopa–Pinal region. HOPWA helps households with an HIV/AIDS diagnosis keep or obtain stable housing — short-term rent, mortgage and utility assistance (STRMU), move-in help like deposits and first/last month's rent (Permanent Housing Placement), tenant-based rental assistance (TBRA), and transitional/supportive housing — plus housing information, advocacy and employment services. Mesa is inside the service area. Start with the City's Central Intake Line, (602) 534-2433 (option 3 — ask for HOPWA), or apply online through phoenix.gov/HOPWA.

**Services Offered**
- Short-term Rent, Mortgage and Utility (STRMU) assistance to prevent eviction, foreclosure or utility disconnection, including rent, mortgage and utility payments and related late fees; short-term, need-based and limited — city materials describe up to three assists in a rolling 12-month period.
- Permanent Housing Placement (PHP) move-in help: security deposits, first/last month's rent, application, credit, administrative and holding fees, and utility connection/deposit fees.
- Tenant-Based Rental Assistance (TBRA) — a rental subsidy (administered via the City of Phoenix Section 8 Office) for a private-market unit; referral through the Area Agency on Aging's Care Directions housing information program, (602) 264-2273.
- Transitional housing (up to 24 months; rent is 30% of income, $0 if no income) and permanent supportive/independent housing through partner agencies such as Southwest Behavioral & Health Services; also housing information and waitlist placement, eviction-prevention advocacy, case management and employment services.

**Eligibility Requirements**
- At least one household member must be diagnosed with HIV or AIDS; household income at or below 80% of Area Median Income; the household must currently reside in Maricopa or Pinal County.
- STRMU is need-based and cannot duplicate housing or utility assistance from another housing program; households must show they would be at risk of homelessness or utility disconnection without help.
- The intake line confirms required documentation (identity, income, HIV/AIDS status, housing/utility obligation) when you apply.

**Population Served**
- Households living with HIV/AIDS in Maricopa and Pinal counties — individuals, couples and families, including households with children (a minor in the household can qualify the household); low-income households at or below 80% AMI. Program flyers are published in English and Spanish.

**How to Best Connect**
- Call the City of Phoenix Central Intake Line: (602) 534-2433 (602-534-AIDE) or toll-free 1-866-882-1778. Follow the prompts: choose option #3, choose a Family Services Center, then choose #3 to speak to center staff — and ask for HOPWA assistance.
- Or apply online through the City's HSD Community Services Program Portal, linked from phoenix.gov/HOPWA.
- Business hours: Monday–Friday, 8 a.m.–5 p.m. per the current flyer (older city materials list 7 a.m.–6 p.m. for the appointment line — confirm when calling); appointments are required. Mesa clients can start by phone or online. For TBRA, ask about referral through Care Directions at (602) 264-2273.

**Important Information to Know**
- Applying is free.
- Funding is limited and demand is high; if no appointment is available, try again the following week.
- STRMU cannot duplicate assistance from another housing program and is limited in frequency (see Services).
- Which HOPWA programs and nonprofit partners are funded can change with funding cycles — confirm current availability, program caps and required documents when you call.
- Mesa residents are inside the service area (Maricopa County), but the standard eligibility rules above still apply.

phone: (602) 534-2433

website: https://www.phoenix.gov/HOPWA

hours: Mon–Fri, 8 a.m.–5 p.m. per the current HOPWA flyer (appointments scheduled through the Central Intake Line; older city materials list 7 a.m.–6 p.m. for the appointment line — confirm when calling)

Sue's Espacio bridge shelter — City of Tempe (Mercy House case management)

b05b-sues-espacio · usable

Sue's Espacio is the City of Tempe's city-owned bridge shelter — 40 private, motel-style rooms (formerly the Rodeway Inn) at 2101 E. Apache Blvd. for individuals, couples and families experiencing homelessness in Tempe. Guests stay short-term (typically about 30 days) while case managers help them move to longer-term shelter or permanent housing, employment (Tempe Works), health care and veterans services. Pets are allowed. Admission is by referral through the City's HOPE team and partner agencies: call the 24/7 CARE & HOPE Line at 480-350-8004 or email HOPE@tempe.gov.

**Services Offered**
- Non-congregate bridge shelter: private motel-style rooms (full bathroom, locking storage/wardrobe, mini-fridge; rooms can be set up for double occupancy), on-site laundry and a small dog park — one of the few Tempe shelters that allows pets.
- On-site case management (with partner Mercy House Living Centers) to move residents into longer-term shelter or permanent housing; help connecting to employment (Tempe Works), health care and veterans services; occasional hot meals, food boxes and hygiene supplies from community partners.

**Eligibility Requirements**
- For people experiencing homelessness in Tempe. Admission is low-barrier: guests are not denied shelter because of sobriety, mental-health treatment or low/no income. All guests enter by referral through the City's identified referral partners — it is not a walk-up shelter.

**Population Served**
- Individuals, couples and families (including many families with children) experiencing homelessness in Tempe, including people with chronic homelessness, mental or physical health needs, or substance-use issues.

**How to Best Connect**
- Call the City of Tempe CARE & HOPE Line at 480-350-8004 (answered 24/7) or email HOPE@tempe.gov. The HOPE homeless outreach team does assessments and places people into the bridge shelter; people have been placed shortly after meeting the team.
- HOPE's office is in the Tempe Public Library building at 3500 S. Rural Road (call or email before visiting — the team is often out in the field). If you already work with a Tempe provider (for example TCAA), ask them to refer you.

**Important Information to Know**
- This is a bridge shelter, not permanent housing: stays are short — typically about 30 days — while residents work on housing plans.
- Rooms and vacancies vary; placement depends on a HOPE assessment and current capacity.
- Pets are generally allowed and there is a small dog park on site, but city discussions have tied pet accommodation to guests actively working toward housing within their stay — confirm pet details when you call.
- The city also bought a second motel — the former Apache Inn (about 58 rooms) near Apache Blvd. and McClintock Dr. — to add bridge-shelter capacity; ask HOPE whether it is open and accepting referrals now.

phone: 480-350-8004

address: 2101 E. Apache Blvd., Tempe, AZ 85281

website: https://www.tempe.gov/government/community-health-and-human-services/housing-services/ending-homelessness/housing-and-shelter

hours: Shelter operates 24/7; CARE & HOPE Line answered 24/7

Step Into Housing — City of Tempe / Tempe Community Action Agency shelter-to-housing pathway

b05b-step-into-housing · usable

Step Into Housing is a City of Tempe shelter-to-housing pathway run with Tempe Community Action Agency (TCAA): people staying in TCAA's I-HELP shelter use a 90-day stay to become document- and housing-ready, then move into available non-congregate shelter (bridge shelter) aiming for a housing resolution within 30–90 days. It is not a separate walk-in program — enrollment works through TCAA's I-HELP shelter intake, (480) 590-5514 (or (480) 389-1277 to check availability), or the City's CARE & HOPE Line, 480-350-8004.

**Services Offered**
- A coordinated shelter-to-housing pathway: case management at TCAA's I-HELP shelter helps guests get documents and become housing-ready during a 90-day stay, then moves them into available non-congregate shelter (bridge shelter) with a goal of a housing resolution within 30–90 days.
- I-HELP itself provides overnight shelter at rotating faith-community sites, meals, assessments, service plans, ID/benefit help and housing navigation.

**Eligibility Requirements**
- People experiencing homelessness in Tempe who are in (or entering) TCAA's I-HELP shelter and the city shelter system; screening happens through shelter intake, and participants work with a case manager on a housing plan.

**Population Served**
- Adults experiencing homelessness in Tempe (I-HELP is an adult shelter program; about 40 adults are hosted nightly).

**How to Best Connect**
- Start with TCAA about I-HELP: call (480) 590-5514 to schedule an intake appointment, or (480) 389-1277 to check availability. In-person intakes run 8–10 a.m. Monday–Friday at TCAA's services site, 2146 E. Apache Blvd., Tempe (listed as the Oasis Drop-In Center in some older city guides — call first). TCAA's front desk is (480) 422-8922 ext. 5882 as a fallback.
- Or call the City's CARE & HOPE Line at 480-350-8004 (24/7) or email HOPE@tempe.gov, and ask specifically about the Step Into Housing pathway.

**Important Information to Know**
- This is a pilot-style pathway inside the shelter system, not a separate walk-in service — confirm with TCAA or the City that it is currently operating and that space is available before counting on it.
- The city renewed its agreement with TCAA for the Step Into Housing pilot (January 2024); the first nine months reported 22 of 23 participants (95.7%) housed. Current 2026 capacity is not published and regional shelter space is limited.
- Phone numbers differ across TCAA's own pages ((480) 590-5514 vs. (480) 389-1277) — if one does not answer, try the other.
- TCAA is building an expanded shelter campus on Apache Blvd. (planned around Fall 2026, 80 beds) — ask where intake currently happens.
- No fees and no separate application form — access is through shelter intake.

phone: (480) 590-5514 (I-HELP intake); (480) 389-1277 (I-HELP hotline)

address: TCAA services site: 2146 E. Apache Blvd., Tempe, AZ 85281 (in-person intakes 8–10 a.m. weekdays; call first)

website: https://tempeaction.org/get-help/housing-stability/
