Tempe Housing Services / Tempe Housing Authority — Section 8 HCV (waitlist closed), HOME TBRA and special-purpose vouchers

b05b-tempe-housing-services · usable

Tempe Housing Services (Tempe Housing Authority, AZ031) runs the city's rental-housing programs: Section 8 Housing Choice Vouchers (waitlist currently closed), a HOME-funded Tenant-Based Rental Assistance (TBRA) program for households who are homeless or at risk of homelessness in Tempe — including domestic-violence survivors — and special-purpose vouchers (Mainstream for non-elderly adults with disabilities, HUD-VASH for veterans via the VA, and Emergency Housing Vouchers via coordinated entry). Programs prioritize people who live, work or are homeless in Tempe. Call 480-350-8950; office in Suite 202 on the 2nd floor of the Tempe Public Library building, 3500 S. Rural Road.

**Services Offered**
- Section 8 Housing Choice Voucher (HCV) rental subsidies — tenants pay about 30% of adjusted income; the waitlist is currently closed (last opening Feb 28–Mar 13, 2023, using a lottery for 3,000 pre-applications).
- HOME Tenant-Based Rental Assistance (TBRA HPP): monthly rental help for up to 24 months for households who are homeless or at risk of homelessness in Tempe, including survivors of domestic violence, dating violence, sexual assault or stalking; can cover security deposits (up to two months) paid to the landlord; designed as a bridge until longer-term assistance.
- Special-purpose vouchers: Mainstream (non-elderly adults with disabilities), HUD-VASH (veterans, referred through the VA) and Emergency Housing Vouchers (referred through the region's coordinated entry system); landlord recruitment and housing-search support for voucher holders.

**Eligibility Requirements**
- HCV: HUD income limits apply (very low income, updated annually); waiting-list preferences have included people who live or work in Tempe, are homeless in Tempe, are elderly or disabled, or were involuntarily displaced. Applications are only accepted when the waitlist is open.
- TBRA: must reside or be homeless in Tempe (or work/be hired to work in Tempe); income at or below 50% of area median income at move-in (assistance may continue until income exceeds 80% AMI); must sign a self-sufficiency agreement; cannot receive another rental subsidy at the same time; a case manager is required to help access the program, and referrals from community organizations or local government programs under agreement with the City of Tempe (including homeless and domestic-violence providers) receive preference.
- Mainstream, VASH and EHV follow their own program rules (disability; veteran status via the VA; coordinated-entry eligibility).

**Population Served**
- Low-income individuals and families; households experiencing homelessness or at risk of homelessness in Tempe; domestic-violence survivors; veterans; non-elderly adults with disabilities. Elderly and disabled applicants receive preference.

**How to Best Connect**
- Call 480-350-8950 (Monday–Friday; phone help 8 a.m.–noon and 1–5 p.m.).
- Visit the office in Suite 202 on the 2nd floor of the Tempe Public Library building, 3500 S. Rural Road, Tempe, AZ 85282 — call before visiting to confirm current hours.
- While the Section 8 waitlist is closed, no application can be submitted — ask to be notified about future openings (openings are announced at least 10 days in advance) and ask about TBRA and referral-based vouchers.
- For TBRA, ask how a case-manager or partner-agency referral can be arranged; for VASH, referrals go through the VA; for EHV, through the Maricopa coordinated entry system.

**Important Information to Know**
- The Housing Choice Voucher waitlist is closed per current city and 2-1-1 information — there is no open direct application for most Mesa households right now.
- TBRA and special-purpose vouchers are referral-based and limited; availability depends on funding and turnover.
- Tempe programs prioritize people who live, work or are homeless in Tempe, so Mesa households generally need a Tempe connection or a referral through a Tempe-serving agency.
- Applications are free — never pay anyone to apply for a voucher or waitlist. Some older listings show a different downtown address; current listings point to 3500 S. Rural Road — call before visiting.

phone: 480-350-8950

address: 3500 S. Rural Road, Suite 202 (2nd floor, Tempe Public Library building), Tempe, AZ 85282

website: https://www.tempe.gov/government/community-health-and-human-services/housing-services

hours: Mon–Fri, 8 a.m.–5 p.m.; phone help 8 a.m.–noon and 1–5 p.m.

Tempe River Bottom – Resource Village (City of Tempe with nonprofit/agency partners)

b06a-tempe-resource-village · needs-resolution

A multi-partner service village that the City of Tempe ran near the south bank of the Salt River bed during its 2022–2023 river-bottom homeless response. It bundled shelter and housing access with medical care, veterans supports, warrant resolution, mobile cooling centers, restrooms, showers, storage for personal belongings and pet services. City materials do not show the village operating after that operation, so treat it as historical and unverified and use Tempe's current CARE & HOPE Line for any present-day need.

**Services Offered**
During Tempe's 2022–2023 Salt River bed response, the Resource Village gave people living in the river bottom a daily place to meet outreach staff and partner agencies in air-conditioned trailers and connect with services. Documented services included shelter and housing access, medical care, veterans supports, Tempe Municipal Court's Warrant Resolution Program, and pet services; documented amenities included mobile cooling centers, restrooms, showers, bottled water, and dedicated storage for personal belongings.

**Eligibility Requirements**
The village served people experiencing unsheltered homelessness in and around the Tempe river bottom as part of the city's outreach operation; no separate application or eligibility rules were published. For current help, anyone can call Tempe's CARE & HOPE Line about themselves or another person.

**Population Served**
People experiencing homelessness in the Tempe river-bottom area, including people with pets and veterans; shelter, treatment and family-reconnection options were offered from the site. This was a time-limited response population, not an ongoing program.

**How to Best Connect**
There is no current phone number, address, hours or intake for the Resource Village; the former site was near the south bank of the Salt River bed, and the river bottom is a no-trespass area. Do not send anyone to the former site. For any current Tempe outreach need — including pet-friendly help — call the City of Tempe CARE & HOPE Line at 480-350-8004 (24/7) or email HOPE@tempe.gov. In an emergency, call 911.

**Important Information to Know**
The Resource Village appears in City of Tempe materials from the 2022–2023 river-bottom operation, which ended with people vacating the area and the city moving into debris removal and long-term maintenance. It could not be confirmed that the village — or any of its services, especially pet services — still operates anywhere in Tempe, and no current city page or listing shows it as an active service. Treat this record as historical and unverified; confirm with the city before relying on it.

City of Tempe CARE & HOPE Line / HOPE Street Outreach Team

b06a-tempe-care-hope-line · usable

Tempe's 24/7 CARE & HOPE Line (480-350-8004) is the city's main entry point for homeless outreach help. Anyone can call — for themselves or for another person — and the city's HOPE (Homeless Outreach Program Effort) team responds with street outreach, shelter and housing connections, and referrals. The line serves people located in Tempe (Maricopa County); email HOPE@tempe.gov for non-urgent concerns.

**Services Offered**
A 24/7 phone line plus a city street-outreach team. Callers can ask for help for themselves or report a person in crisis or experiencing homelessness in Tempe. HOPE outreach specialists engage people in the field and connect them to emergency shelter, housing solutions and housing navigation, diversion help such as reconnecting with family or support systems, treatment programs, and practical assistance such as replacing IDs. The team also responds to emails sent to HOPE@tempe.gov and works with police, fire, parks and community partners. In a January 2024 update, Tempe reported that since April 2022 about 31% of outreach engagements resulted in accepted services, and about 25% of those who accepted services accepted a shelter or housing solution; current results may differ.

**Eligibility Requirements**
No application or enrollment is needed to call. The line accepts self-referrals and third-party reports for people who are in Tempe. What is offered depends on the person's situation, the person's consent, and the availability of shelter and housing options.

**Population Served**
People experiencing homelessness or in crisis in Tempe, including individuals and families. Outreach is concentrated in areas such as Mill Avenue, high-need parks and around ASU, and the team coordinates with campus and community partners.

**How to Best Connect**
Call 480-350-8004 — answered 24/7. Email HOPE@tempe.gov for general concerns or non-urgent requests. The HOPE team works in the field; no walk-in office for the team is published, and staff go to where the person is. For immediate danger, call 911. The line serves Tempe — for someone elsewhere in Maricopa County, use that area's homeless-services access point.

**Important Information to Know**
This is a request-and-response outreach service, not a guaranteed shelter bed or housing placement; Tempe has limited shelter capacity, so timing depends on availability, and some people choose not to accept services. Staffing and response times vary. Programs the team refers to (shelter, treatment, housing) have their own eligibility rules and waitlists. The 24/7 number and the HOPE@tempe.gov email are the city's documented current contact routes.

phone: 480-350-8004

website: https://homeless-solutions.tempe.gov/

hours: CARE & HOPE Line: 24 hours a day, 7 days a week.

resolutionReason: The CARE & HOPE Line number, 24/7 availability, email and the HOPE team's outreach-to-shelter/housing role are supported by current City of Tempe pages and partner sources; no identity, geography or access conflict remains. Capacity and placement limits are stated, and the January 2024 outcome figures were restated precisely (about 31% of engagements accepted services; about 25% of those accepted a shelter/housing solution).

Sources: City of Tempe — Connect page (CARE & HOPE Line) · City of Tempe — Homeless Solutions · City of Tempe — Homeless Solutions Update (Jan. 11, 2024) · Arizona State University — Homelessness near ASU

Consider this: Adds Tempe's 24/7 outreach-and-referral line for clients physically in Tempe - complements the starter 211 and Phoenix-based entry points.

Coffelt-Lamoreaux Apartment Homes — 30 units reserved for DDD members

b06a-coffelt-lamoreaux-ddd · usable

Coffelt-Lamoreaux Apartment Homes is a 301-unit affordable apartment community in Phoenix where 30 units are reserved for adults with developmental disabilities who are DDD members, through a partnership between the Arizona DES Division of Developmental Disabilities and the Housing Authority of Maricopa County. Access is by referral from a DDD Support Coordinator — not a general public waitlist. Rent is 30% of the household's combined income; a deposit and first month's rent may be required. Call the DDD Customer Service Center at 1-844-770-9500, option 1, to start.

**Services Offered**
Affordable rental housing: 30 of the community's 301 units are made available for qualified DDD members under the DDD/HAMC partnership. The DDD affordable housing quick-reference guide describes the set-aside as 30 one- to four-bedroom duplex units; DES's current housing page describes the community as one-, two- and three-bedroom floor plans. Rent is 30% of the household's combined income, and the federally funded property pays for reasonable accommodations/modifications when a health care provider documents the need and its connection to the disability.

**Eligibility Requirements**
Referral by a DDD Support Coordinator. The member must be a DDD member; have a head of household aged 18–61; have a current Person-Centered Service Plan (per the DDD quick-reference guide); and meet household financial eligibility requirements and HUD requirements. The quick-reference guide also lists DDD and Arizona Long Term Care System (ALTCS) eligibility. Every adult living in the unit — including live-in caregivers — undergoes a criminal background check; only people listed on the lease may live there; federal property rules apply (no drugs or smoking). A deposit and the first month's rent may be required, and utilities may need to be set up before moving in; live-in caregiver income is not counted in the household's combined income.

**Population Served**
Adults with developmental disabilities who are eligible for Arizona DDD services and are 18–61, especially those who need an affordable, integrated apartment with DDD supports. Households may include family members or roommates when the combined household meets income and housing requirements.

**How to Best Connect**
Start with the member's DDD Support Coordinator and ask for a referral to the DDD affordable housing program / Coffelt-Lamoreaux. If there is no Support Coordinator, call the DDD Customer Service Center at 1-844-770-9500, option 1 (email DDDCustomerServiceCenter@azdes.gov); the DES housing page also lists the DDD Affordable Housing Unit contact (Megan Akens, 1-844-770-9500 option 1). The community is at 1510 S. 19th Dr., Phoenix, AZ 85009 — do not apply to the property directly for the DDD set-aside; the referral goes through DDD.

**Important Information to Know**
Availability depends on vacancies and DDD referrals; waitlists and timing are not published, and current openings cannot be confirmed here. Exact rent (beyond the 30%-of-income rule), deposit amounts, utility costs and unit sizes should be confirmed with DDD and the property. Unit-size descriptions differ slightly between DDD documents (quick-reference guide: one- to four-bedroom duplexes reserved; current DES page: community floor plans of one to three bedrooms). The guide is also published in Spanish (DDD-2313A-S). DES's DDD housing page also lists other housing pathways for members (for example, housing vouchers and Section 811 PRA rental assistance) that a Support Coordinator can discuss.

phone: 1-844-770-9500

address: 1510 S. 19th Dr., Phoenix, AZ 85009

website: https://des.az.gov/services/disabilities/developmental-disabilities/individuals-and-families/supports-and-services/housing

resolutionReason: Current DES/DDD sources (Member Housing Opportunities page and the DDD affordable-housing quick-reference guide) confirm the property's identity, the 30-unit DDD set-aside, eligibility rules and the Support Coordinator / Customer Service Center referral route (including the DDD Affordable Housing Unit contact). Vacancy, waitlist and exact cost details are not published and are flagged; the 1–4 vs 1–3 bedroom description discrepancy is preserved; none of these block a referral inquiry.

Community Bridges, Inc. (CBI) — Mesa Tenant-Based Rental Assistance (TBRA)

b06a-cbi-mesa-tbra · usable

Community Bridges, Inc. (CBI) is a Mesa-based behavioral health and housing nonprofit that administers Tenant-Based Rental Assistance (TBRA) and supportive housing for people experiencing homelessness in Mesa. City of Mesa reports describe CBI's TBRA work — rental payments plus application fees, security and utility deposit help, and housing navigation/case management — and recent city funding continues the program (FY 2025/26: CDBG for CBI Mesa TBRA Navigator Staff and HOME TBRA funds for rental payments and deposits). Start by calling CBI's 24/7 access line at 877-931-9142 and ask about the Mesa housing/TBRA program.

**Services Offered**
Tenant-based rental assistance for households in Mesa: temporary rental subsidy paid on behalf of the household while it stabilizes in its own unit, combined with help for application fees, security and utility deposits, plus housing navigation and case-management support. City of Mesa reporting describes the TBRA program run with CBI as assisting people and families experiencing homelessness in Mesa (166 households in that reporting period), and says CBI's award covered rental payments, deposits, housing management fees and supportive housing services. CBI's wider Mesa continuum also includes street outreach/navigation, shelters and permanent supportive housing/rapid rehousing with wrap-around services, so a navigator can point people to other options if TBRA is not the right fit.

**Eligibility Requirements**
Households in Mesa who are experiencing homelessness or housing instability and meet program income rules. City of Mesa HOME TBRA documents describe eligibility as incomes at or below 60% of the area median for Mesa residents who are homeless, with a required tenant share of rent and utilities, unit inspections (Housing Quality Standards), and rental assistance contracts of one to two years. Participants work with a CBI navigator/case manager, and the rental unit must meet program requirements. Exact current income limits, documents and referral criteria are set by the program and its city funding — confirm by calling CBI.

**Population Served**
Individuals and families experiencing homelessness in Mesa, including people reached through Mesa's street-outreach and navigation system.

**How to Best Connect**
Call CBI's 24/7 access-to-care line at 877-931-9142 and ask for the Mesa housing / tenant-based rental assistance (TBRA) program. CBI's Mesa office is at 1855 W Baseline Rd #101, Mesa, AZ 85202 — call before visiting. CBI lists appointment scheduling at 888-224-6593 (scheduling@cbridges.com), and its Mesa location card also shows 888-224-2087; the general office number is 480-831-7566. If you are already working with a Mesa outreach team, police/fire referral or another navigator, ask them to refer you to CBI housing services.

**Important Information to Know**
This is time-limited, subsidized rental assistance — not an open-ended voucher and not a guaranteed apartment. Funding is awarded by the City of Mesa year by year; recent cycles used HOME TBRA funds for rental payments and deposits (the FY 2025/2026 draft CAPER reports $200,000 in HOME TBRA funds to CBI with a $50,000 agency match) and CDBG funds for CBI's Mesa TBRA navigator staff ($78,933 in FY 2025/26 funding recommendations). Current openings, income limits and how long a household can receive help are not published — City HOME TBRA documents describe one- to two-year contracts; confirm with CBI. The 877-931-9142 number is CBI's general access line, so expect to be routed — ask specifically for the Mesa TBRA/housing program. CBI's Mesa administration office is listed as open Monday–Friday (8 a.m.–5 p.m. per a 211 Arizona listing); program-specific hours are not published.

Community Bridges, Inc. (CBI) - Coordinated Entry, PATH Outreach & Hotel Bridge Housing

b06b1-cbi-ce-path-bridge-housing · usable

Community Bridges, Inc. (CBI) is a Maricopa County behavioral health and homeless services provider listed as a coordinated entry access point for people experiencing homelessness across the county. CBI's 24/7 access line (877) 931-9142 handles coordinated entry, shelter and housing referrals, while its 24-hour PATH hotline (844) 691-5948 connects people showing signs of mental illness to street outreach. CBI also operates emergency shelters and Healing Centers and a converted-hotel bridge shelter at Rio Fresco (2425 S. 24th St., Phoenix) with case management and housing navigation toward Rapid Rehousing or Permanent Supportive Housing.

**Services Offered**
- Coordinated entry access: Community Bridges, Inc. (CBI) is listed among Maricopa County's coordinated entry access points, offering access to specialized programs for people experiencing homelessness, with locations across the county.
- PATH outreach: CBI PATH provides street outreach to people displaying signs and symptoms of mental illness, with a 24-hour PATH hotline at (844) 691-5948; outreach includes engagement, assessment, case management and referrals to shelter, housing, treatment and benefits.
- Shelter and housing: CBI's navigation teams operate emergency shelters and Healing Centers using a closed-campus model and provide housing resources such as Rapid Rehousing and Permanent Supportive Housing with wrap-around services (Housing First).
- Hotel bridge housing: CBI operates the Rio Fresco site at 2425 S. 24th Street, Phoenix, a converted hotel used as a bridge shelter with temporary lodging, case management, behavioral health connections, meals and 24/7 security while people wait to move into permanent housing.
- Other supports in CBI's continuum include mental health and substance use treatment, physical health services, peer support/navigation, counseling, life-skills building and vocational rehabilitation.
- Service area: CBI reports operating in Gila, Maricopa, Pima and Yuma counties, with this coordinated entry/PATH route centered on Maricopa County.

**Eligibility Requirements**
- People experiencing homelessness in Maricopa County; coordinated entry is the region's front door and uses an assessment to determine which programs and priority a person may qualify for.
- PATH outreach targets adults who show signs and symptoms of mental illness (PATH is designed for people with serious mental illness who are homeless or at risk of homelessness).
- Bridge housing at Rio Fresco is for people prioritized within the homeless service system and is arranged through referral and case management, not as a direct walk-up.
- No income limits, insurance requirements or fees are published for these programs; ask CBI what assessment or documents are needed.

**Population Served**
- CBI's shelter and outreach programs state they serve individuals and families experiencing homelessness, including veterans; pregnant or postpartum women with substance use disorders and their children; survivors of domestic violence and human trafficking and their children; and youth ages 18-24.
- The PATH outreach line serves people experiencing homelessness who show signs and symptoms of mental illness.
- CBI does not publish which specific site or program accepts which population; the access line can route people to the appropriate CBI program.

**How to Best Connect**
- Call CBI's 24/7 Access to Care line: (877) 931-9142 - the main route for coordinated entry intake, shelter/housing questions and program referrals.
- Call the 24-hour PATH hotline: (844) 691-5948 for street outreach related to mental illness.
- Appointments and scheduling: (888) 224-6593 or scheduling@cbridges.com; CBI administration office: 1855 W. Baseline Rd. #101, Mesa, AZ 85202; (480) 831-7566.
- Website: communitybridgesinc.org (see the Shelters & Outreach page).
- Locations are across Maricopa County; phone first, since no walk-up intake or online application is published for these programs and bridge housing is referral-based.
- Regional context: Maricopa County's homeless-help page routes single adults 18+ to the Brian Garcia Welcome Center (602-229-5155) and families to the Family Housing Hub (602-595-8700); veterans can call the Phoenix VA Community Resource and Referral Center (602-248-6040). CBI is listed as a separate coordinated entry access point, so ask CBI which route fits.

**Important Information to Know**
- Costs are not published; ask CBI directly. Nothing reviewed states whether shelter or bridge housing carries a fee.
- Space is not guaranteed: shelter and bridge housing depend on assessment, priority, referrals and current capacity, which can change daily.
- CBI uses a closed-campus shelter/Healing Center model, and bridge housing serves people prioritized in the system - expect an intake process rather than immediate self-placement.
- Current signal: City of Phoenix Ordinance S-52891, adopted May 20, 2026, extends CBI's contract for the Rio Fresco converted-hotel bridge shelter for fiscal year 2026-27 (term on or about July 1, 2026, through June 30, 2027); city records describe bridge placement for people prioritized within the homeless service system, with on-site case management, behavioral health connections, meals and 24/7 security.
- Program-specific hours, payment/insurance details and exact intake steps are not published; the 24/7 access line and 24-hour PATH hotline are the reliable contact routes.

Community Bridges, Inc. (CBI) · Supportive Housing for Adults with Serious Mental Illness

b06b2-cbi-smi-supportive-housing · usable

Community Bridges, Inc. (CBI) is an Arizona behavioral-health provider whose housing programs include permanent supportive housing and rapid rehousing with wrap-around services, plus housing supports for adults with serious mental illness. Clients can call CBI's 24/7 access line to ask about current openings, the SMI/Mercy Care referral path, and costs.

**Services Offered**
Community Bridges, Inc. (CBI) provides permanent supportive housing (PSH) and rapid rehousing (RRH) with wrap-around services for people who are experiencing homelessness or have significant barriers to stable housing, including on-site support services and individualized care plans at co-located apartment communities in the Phoenix-Mesa area. CBI's services for adults with serious mental illness (SMI) include SMI community programs (Forensic Assertive Community Treatment and Assertive Community Treatment) with supports such as housing, employment, managing finances and family life, and its PATH program serves people with SMI and/or co-occurring disorders who are homeless or at imminent risk of becoming homeless, including people with an SMI determination who are not well connected to a clinic. CBI also operates emergency shelters, street outreach/navigation teams and healing centers in Maricopa County as part of the same continuum.

**Eligibility Requirements**
- Supportive housing is described for individuals experiencing chronic homelessness or with significant barriers to stable housing; each program applies its own eligibility and priority rules - ask CBI to confirm.
- SMI services are for adults determined to have a serious mental illness; PATH serves people with SMI and/or co-occurring disorders who are homeless or at imminent risk.
- Coverage: CBI says it accepts all patients and is contracted with all Arizona Complete Care (AHCCCS) health plans (Mercy Care is one) and many private insurances; uninsured or underinsured patients are helped to apply for AHCCCS.
- Entry: CBI says a new-patient referral can be started by a secure form in person, by phone or electronically, and an initial appointment is usually made within 7 days. How SMI supportive-housing enrollment is routed (CBI care team vs. community referral) is not spelled out online - ask the access line.
- Rent and income screening details are not published; ask at intake.

**Population Served**
Adults with a serious mental illness (SMI), including people with co-occurring mental-health and substance-use conditions who are experiencing homelessness or at imminent risk of homelessness. CBI's housing and shelter programs also list individuals and families experiencing homelessness, veterans, pregnant or postpartum women with substance use disorders and their children, survivors of domestic violence and human trafficking (and their children), and homeless and runaway youth ages 18-24 among those served.

**How to Best Connect**
- Call CBI's 24/7 Access to Care line: (877) 931-9142 - ask about supportive housing for someone with a serious mental illness, current openings and referral steps.
- Appointments/scheduling: (888) 224-6593 or scheduling@cbridges.com.
- Administration office: 1855 W. Baseline Rd., Suite 101, Mesa, AZ 85202; (480) 831-7566 - call before visiting; this is CBI's administration office.
- If you are already connected with a CBI care team, ask them directly about SMI housing supports.
- Online: communitybridgesinc.org (Supportive Housing and Serious Mental Illness pages). CBI says initial appointments are usually made within 7 days of first contact, and its housing/shelter sites are across Maricopa County including Mesa and Phoenix.

**Important Information to Know**
- Capacity, waitlists and unit availability are not published - confirm by phone; asking does not guarantee a place.
- Costs are not published: ask at intake what a resident pays and how insurance or benefits apply.
- The combined "SMI supportive housing with Mercy Care" description is not shown as a named program on CBI's website - its SMI services and its supportive housing are documented separately. CBI says it contracts with all Arizona Complete Care plans, which include Mercy Care; call to confirm the exact referral path for a Mercy Care member with an SMI determination.
- CBI's shelters and outreach operate in Gila, Maricopa, Pima and Yuma counties; this listing covers the housing supports available in the Mesa/Maricopa area.

Dismas Charities, Inc. · Phoenix Residential Reentry Center

b07a-dismas-phoenix-rrc · needs-resolution

Dismas Charities is a national nonprofit that runs residential reentry centers (supervised, temporary housing with support programming) and home confinement services for people leaving incarceration, mostly under U.S. Bureau of Prisons contracts. A Dismas-operated 'Phoenix Residential Reentry Center' could not be confirmed; Dismas' verified Arizona reentry center is in Tucson, and reentry-center placement normally comes through the Bureau of Prisons or state corrections rather than community self-referral.

**Services Offered**
Dismas Charities operates residential reentry centers: supervised, temporary housing combined with employment, education, life-skills, and support programming for people leaving incarceration. It also provides home confinement and day reporting services, with a stated focus on sustainable employment, stable housing, and family reunification. Whether Dismas currently operates a Phoenix facility is not confirmed.

**Eligibility Requirements**
Placement normally goes to people releasing from federal or state incarceration or under community supervision who are referred by the U.S. Bureau of Prisons, probation officers, or state corrections agencies. This is not a community self-referral program: admission and eligibility are controlled by the placing agency, and bed availability varies.

**Population Served**
Adults returning to their communities from incarceration; Dismas says its federal programs are co-correctional, serving women and men. 211 Arizona describes Dismas' Arizona services as for newly released male and female ex-offenders. The confirmed Arizona center is in Tucson; Phoenix-area service is unconfirmed.

**How to Best Connect**
No verified Phoenix phone number or address is available, so do not give clients a Phoenix address. If the person is leaving federal custody, ask the Bureau of Prisons case manager or unit team about residential reentry center placement, including any Phoenix-area options; for state or county cases, ask the supervising corrections or probation office. General facility information is on dismas.com, and the 211 Arizona entry (Dismas Charities - Arizona, 3443 S. Richey Blvd., Tucson) lists 'call' as the application step.

**Important Information to Know**
- Unresolved: a current Dismas-operated 'Phoenix Residential Reentry Center' could not be confirmed, and the verified Dismas Arizona center is in Tucson, outside Maricopa County. Verify any Phoenix facility, its contracts, and the referral route before referring.
- Access limit: reentry center beds are assigned through corrections agencies rather than an open application, so an office cannot directly enroll someone.
- Cost: 211 Arizona lists no fees for Dismas' Arizona reentry services.
- Dismas is an established national provider (public materials describe about 39 centers across 15 states, mostly under Bureau of Prisons contracts, with active federal contract activity in recent years).

DOVES (Area Agency on Aging, Region One) - domestic violence services for older adults (50+)

b07a-doves-aaar1 · usable

DOVES is the Area Agency on Aging, Region One's program of domestic violence services for older adults. It serves Maricopa County residents age 50 and older who are victims of late-life domestic or sexual violence. Through the 24-hour Senior HELP LINE (602-264-4357), DOVES provides crisis support, safety planning, case management and advocacy, counseling and support groups, legal and financial help, and transitional housing and housing assistance.

**Services Offered**
24/7 helpline and crisis intervention; safety planning and domestic violence education; case management, mobile advocacy, and goal planning; transitional housing (a current program profile lists 16 beds and a maximum stay of about two years) along with housing counseling and transportation; counseling for residents and non-residents and peer support groups, including virtual groups; help accessing public benefits; legal help such as orders of protection, court accompaniment, and financial empowerment training; job skills and job search/placement help; health and wellness programs and medical accompaniment; and referrals to other services.

**Eligibility Requirements**
Services are for victims of late-life domestic violence and sexual violence, age 50 and older (program materials say '50 years +'), and cover Maricopa County. Housing and transitional-housing help involves screening by DOVES staff, and admission depends on current bed availability; call the helpline to start the assessment.

**Population Served**
Older adults age 50+ in Maricopa County; program profiles list women and men and also note deaf, disabled, immigrant, and LGBTQ older adults among populations served; the program is wheelchair accessible.

**How to Best Connect**
Call the 24-hour Senior HELP LINE at 602-264-4357 (toll-free 1-888-783-7500) - this is the intake number for DOVES. Say you are looking for DOVES services for an older adult (50+) in a domestic violence situation, and ask about counseling, advocacy, and transitional housing. For non-urgent administrative questions, call the Area Agency on Aging, Region One at 602-264-2255 (Monday-Friday 8 a.m.-5 p.m.) or email answers@aaaphx.org. Deaf or hard-of-hearing callers can text 520-775-1899 as published. Website: aaaphx.org.

**Important Information to Know**
- The 602-264-4357 number is the Area Agency's Senior HELP LINE and the DOVES intake line; it is answered 24/7.
- Shelter and transitional housing locations are confidential, and availability varies; a call is required to check current options.
- No fee information was published; ask about any costs when calling.
- Language support: the program profile lists English and Spanish; ask when calling to confirm.
- If there is immediate danger, call 911 first.

phone: 602-264-4357

website: https://www.aaaphx.org/

hours: 24/7 crisis helpline (602-264-4357); Area Agency offices Monday-Friday 8 a.m.-5 p.m.

Escobedo at Verde Vista Phase II (LIHTC and project-based voucher community)

b07a-escobedo-verde-vista-ii · usable

Escobedo at Verde Vista Phase II is an income-restricted apartment community at 435 N. Hibbert in downtown Mesa (Low-Income Housing Tax Credit; 62 units). Some homes are assisted with project-based vouchers administered by the City of Mesa Housing Authority, and the city's Project-Based Voucher Program page currently lists the property as 'Referral Only.' Contact Mesa Housing to confirm how referrals work and whether any waiting list is open.

**Services Offered**
Income-restricted apartments (Low-Income Housing Tax Credit) with some homes supported by project-based rental assistance administered by the City of Mesa Housing Authority. Phase II has 62 units, one- to four-bedroom (mostly three-bedroom); the wider development (Phase I and Phase II) totals about 132 units, and public materials describe on-site resident services such as job-search help and family programming. Access to Phase II is currently listed by the city as 'Referral Only,' so start with the Mesa Housing Authority rather than a standard open application.

**Eligibility Requirements**
The city's published project-based voucher (PBV) eligibility requires applicants to meet its chronic-homelessness definition - at least two episodes of shelter living (emergency and/or transitional housing) plus a substantiated need for long-term case management and supportive services - and to be elderly individuals or families, disabled individuals or families, or families needing supportive services; income limits apply (city materials list a maximum of 50% of area median income for PBV eligibility, with tenancy at these Low-Income Housing Tax Credit properties also subject to extremely-low-income limits), and background screening applies. Because the city lists this property as 'Referral Only,' confirm with Mesa Housing who may refer, what documentation is needed, and whether a waiting list is open.

**Population Served**
Low-income families and individuals, including working families, seniors, and extremely low-income and supportive-services households; public program materials also describe households experiencing homelessness among those served. Units range from one to four bedrooms.

**How to Best Connect**
Contact the City of Mesa Housing Authority (Housing & Community Development) at 480-644-3536 - the number published with Mesa's project-based voucher information; confirm it when calling - or use the city's Project-Based Voucher Program page at mesaaz.gov. Ask: (1) the current status of Escobedo at Verde Vista Phase II; (2) how 'Referral Only' referrals are made and who may refer; (3) whether a waiting list is open and how to be screened; (4) the Phase II leasing office contact. The city's PBV page also directs adults without children to a coordinated entry access point or the Brian Garcia Welcome Center (602-229-5155) and families with minor dependent children to the Family Housing Hub (602-595-8700) before coming in person. Property address: 435 N. Hibbert, Mesa, AZ 85201. Published City Housing office hours: Monday-Thursday, 7:30 a.m.-5 p.m.; confirm when calling.

**Important Information to Know**
- Access caution: the city describes the PBV program as referral-based and lists Phase II as 'Referral Only,' while older city materials described an open waiting list - confirm current referral and waitlist status before promising anything.
- Income limits and selection rules are set by the federal/state programs and the housing authority; exact limits change annually.
- Ownership/management: not confirmed - the property is commonly associated with Chicanos Por La Causa, and directory listings disagree on the current manager; verify with Mesa Housing.
- No fee information was published; ask about application costs or deposits.
- Phase I and Phase II are separate phases on the same site; make clear which phase you are asking about.

Family Promise of Greater Phoenix – Mesa House family shelter (plus congregation-network shelter)

b07b-family-promise-mesa-house · usable

Family Promise of Greater Phoenix helps families with children who are homeless or facing eviction. Mesa House, opened in Mesa in November 2025, is the organization's family shelter — serving up to 60 families per year, with stays reported up to 90 days in the opening announcement (the organization describes its Emergency Shelter Program as 60 days). Families receive case management, employment and financial coaching, meals and help moving into permanent housing; pets are welcome. Call (480) 659-5227 for a phone screening with the social work team.

**Services Offered**
* Emergency family shelter through Family Promise of Greater Phoenix, which runs shelter facilities in south Scottsdale, Glendale and Mesa plus a network of 34 faith congregations that host families overnight one week at a time (private bedrooms, meals and volunteers), with van transportation to Family Day Centers.
* Mesa House (Mesa) opened in November 2025; the opening announcement reported it serves up to 60 families per year with a maximum stay of up to 90 days, with case management, employment help, financial training and help finding housing for each family.
* The organization describes its Emergency Shelter Program as a 60-day program: safe private overnight shelter, all meals, and support at Family Day Centers (Glendale, Mesa and two in south Scottsdale) — daycare/school enrollment, after-school support, employment help, health-insurance access, budgeting and next-step housing. A shared-housing step is available for families progressing well.
* "Promise for Pets" keeps families together with their pets during shelter, with private pet shelter, food and veterinary care for typical domestic pets.
* The Graduate "Connections" program keeps families engaged for up to 24 months after they move into permanent housing, with move-in support for qualifying families (minimum $500); prevention and diversion services are also offered, but rental assistance is limited to families who previously graduated from a Family Promise program — the agency states it does not provide general rental or eviction assistance to families who have not participated in its programs.

**Eligibility Requirements**
* Families with at least one child with them (the organization says "at least one child" and any number of pets); the Mesa Family Shelter Program is described by the City of Mesa as serving families with youth under 18.
* Families complete a phone screening with the social work team before intake; after pre-screening, an intake form link is provided on the "Need Shelter?" page.
* City of Mesa materials describe Family Promise's bridge model as up to 10 families with minor children and stays up to 60 days — confirm current limits during screening.
* No income or documentation rules are published; ask what is needed.

**Population Served**
* Families with children (parents and children together) who are homeless or at risk of homelessness in Mesa, the East Valley and the greater Phoenix area; pets are welcomed as part of the family. Most families served are single-parent households (about 70%), mostly moms.

**How to Best Connect**
* Call (480) 659-5227 for a phone screening with the social work team; after pre-screening, an intake form link is provided on the "Need Shelter?" page at familypromiseaz.org.
* Program offices: 7447 E. Earll Dr., Scottsdale, AZ 85251; email info@FamilyPromiseAZ.org.
* Mesa House's street address is not published; staff share location details during screening.
* The organization notes families find it through referral or the Valley's Family Housing Hub; Maricopa County's family coordinated-entry route is the Family Housing Hub (602-595-8700) — call Family Promise's line and ask which route is fastest.

**Important Information to Know**
* Length-of-stay figures differ: the organization describes its shelter program as 60 days, the Mesa House opening announcement reported up to 90 days, and City of Mesa materials say up to 60 days — confirm what applies.
* Capacity is limited (Mesa House: up to about 60 families per year) and current openings are not published; contacting Family Promise does not guarantee shelter placement — availability is one of the determining factors.
* Pets are accepted during shelter stays — mention your pets during screening.
* Limited rental assistance exists only for families who previously graduated from a Family Promise program; it is not a general eviction-prevention service.
* Funding: City of Mesa's FY 25/26 funding recommendations list an $80,035 CDBG award for the Family Promise "Mesa Family Shelter Program"; no shelter fees are published, and no eligibility or place is guaranteed.

phone: (480) 659-5227

address: Mesa, AZ (Mesa House); program office: 7447 E. Earll Dr., Scottsdale, AZ 85251

website: https://familypromiseaz.org/

First Place AZ – First Place–Phoenix Apartments (supportive apartments; residential Transition Academy with SARRC)

b07b-first-place-phoenix-apartments · usable

First Place–Phoenix is a supportive apartment community at 3001 N. Third St. in midtown Phoenix for adults with autism and other neurodiversities. One- and two-bedroom suites come with on-site life-skills, employment and community-life programming; a separate two-year residential Transition Academy is run with the Southwest Autism Research & Resource Center (SARRC). It is not a group home or assisted-living facility. Published costs: suites $4,600–$4,800/month plus a $750 nonrefundable application fee; Transition Academy tuition $42,343/year when insurance funding applies. Call 602-464-6600 or email info@firstplaceaz.org for tours, availability and eligibility.

**Services Offered**
* Supportive independent-living apartments in midtown Phoenix (one- and two-bedroom suites across three residential floors) with on-site supports — life skills, employment, community life, and health & wellness — and amenities including a teaching kitchen, community room, fitness room, health spot with telehealth and an exam room in partnership with Dignity Health, reading lounges, art studio, game room, outdoor pool and patio, and 24/7 concierge.
* The website currently advertises "Now Leasing: 2026 Supportive Living Apartments."
* On-site employment support includes interview preparation, job search and public-transit navigation; daily community-life activities are offered.
* A separate two-year residential Transition Academy, operated with the Southwest Autism Research & Resource Center (SARRC), provides independent-living and career-readiness training (Learn4Independence curriculum, clinical assessment and services, employment readiness); students live in four-bedroom shared suites and progress toward more independent living.
* The property states it is not a group home or a licensed congregate-care or assisted-living facility and is not designed for people who need a medical home.

**Eligibility Requirements**
* Apartments: built for adults with autism and/or other neurodiversities; rental and income requirements apply — a criteria sheet and application process are published, and you can ask First Place for current tenant-selection criteria and income limits.
* Transition Academy (training program): age 18+ with completion of high school or a learning-resource program; autism diagnosis; basic self-care and medication management with oversight; ability to communicate needs and participate in small-group learning; no current or chronic addiction or serious mental illness; no severe aggression, self-injury or property destruction in the past 12 months; no interfering behaviors requiring intensive one-on-one intervention; able to function safely without direct supervision during unstructured time; motivated to participate. SARRC completes the clinical determination.
* A subset of units is targeted to low-income tenants (16 units per the project's financing record) — ask how those units are allocated and what income limits apply.

**Population Served**
* Adults with autism and/or other neurodiversities; Transition Academy participants are adults with autism (18+). Supports are designed for people who can live with limited supervision; reported resident ages have ranged from the early 20s to mid-40s.

**How to Best Connect**
* Call 602-464-6600 or email info@firstplaceaz.org; tours and inquiries can be scheduled through the website ("Schedule Tour" / "Tour & Inquire").
* Address: 3001 N. Third St., Phoenix, AZ 85012 — midtown Phoenix, near public transit.
* Apartment process: take a tour, submit the $750 nonrefundable application fee, confirm financial and rental requirements (criteria sheet), then sign the lease and pay move-in fees.
* Transition Academy steps: schedule an overview meeting, take a tour, submit the registration form and fee, meet financial and rental requirements, complete the SARRC clinical determination and interview, then enroll.
* Ask directly about current apartment availability, waitlist, rents and leasing timeline, since availability changes.

**Important Information to Know**
* Published costs: apartment suites are $4,600/month (1 bed/1 bath) and $4,800/month (2 bed/2 bath, single occupancy), plus a $750 nonrefundable application fee; Transition Academy tuition is $42,343/year ($3,750/month) when insurance funding applies — $89,593/year ($7,935/month) if insurance funding lapses, and insurance funding is subject to re-authorization. A scholarship fund (Baylee Barradas) exists for the academy; ask what scholarships or funding (including AHCCCS/DDD) can apply.
* Unit-count reports vary (67 apartments per the project's financing record, including 16 targeted to low-income tenants; other accounts cite 55 units at opening) — confirm the current unit mix and what is available.
* Access limits: not a licensed care setting; people needing a medical home or intensive supervision are not a fit, and behavioral criteria apply for the Transition Academy.
* The "Now Leasing" status is from the website and can change — confirm before promising anything; no place is guaranteed.

b07b-flocrit-uplift · usable

Foster360 – housing and wraparound services for youth aging out of foster care

b07b-foster360 · usable

Foster360 provides safe housing and wraparound support for young adults who have aged out of foster care, with housing in Mesa ("Stage One"; The Pepper Place) and Tempe ("Stage Two & Three") and services across the East Valley. Support includes case management, life-skills workshops, education and employment help, therapy, transportation and basic needs. It is a two-year voluntary program; youth pay a monthly participation fee for housing and must meet program rules (no children or pets, drug/alcohol-free, background check). Contact info@foster360az.org or 480-834-2115; the age range is 18–24 per program materials (the website's help section says 18–23).

**Services Offered**
* Safe, stable housing for young adults transitioning out of foster care at two East Valley facilities — a Mesa campus ("Stage One") and a Tempe campus ("Stage Two & Three") — in a trauma-informed, community-centered environment; Mesa housing includes The Pepper Place apartments.
* Wraparound services: case management, life-skills workshops (cooking, financial literacy, health advocacy, healthy relationships), education and employment support (GED preparation, resume building, interviews, job placement), therapy and counseling, therapeutic services through partners (including art, music, physical, speech and occupational therapy, and traumatic brain injury therapy with Barrow Neurological Institute), transportation help (including rideshare vouchers), and basic needs (clothing, groceries, hygiene items, bedding).
* Partnerships include Barrow Neurological Institute, HonorHealth and Northern Arizona University, per program summaries. Foster360 is a two-year, voluntary program and an "Azura Solutions Initiative."

**Eligibility Requirements**
* Young people who have transitioned out of foster care or are in extended foster care; program materials describe ages 18–24, while the website's help page says ages 18–23 — confirm the current age range.
* Program rules: be drug- and alcohol-free and able to pass a drug test; no charges or convictions for a violent offense (a background check is conducted); gain employment if not currently employed; no children or pets; reside in Arizona.
* Youth may come from anywhere in Arizona's foster-care system, but housing and services concentrate in the East Valley/Maricopa County; most participants enter with little or no income and no stable housing.
* Participants are expected to attend weekly case management meetings, life-skills workshops and employment, and they pay a monthly participation fee for housing. Ask about openings, the intake assessment and whether referrals are needed.

**Population Served**
* Young adults (about 18–24) aging out of foster care in Maricopa County, especially Mesa, Tempe and surrounding East Valley communities; program reporting notes high rates of trauma and traumatic brain injury (85% identified brain injury per a City of Mesa presentation; 20–30% diagnosed or suspected TBI per a 2026 grant packet) and that participants are roughly 40% female and 60% male.

**How to Best Connect**
* Email info@foster360az.org or call 480-834-2115; office address 137 E. University Dr., Mesa, AZ 85201; website foster360.org — the "Our Program" page's "How to Apply" section includes an online interest form for youth or their caseworkers.
* Ask about current openings, the intake process and whether referrals are needed; no walk-in hours are published — contact first to arrange next steps.

**Important Information to Know**
* Cost: youth pay a monthly participation fee for housing (amount not published) — ask what the fee is and whether any stipend or assistance covers it.
* Capacity is limited: recent filings describe about 11 young adults in the program; a February 2026 grant packet describes up to 15 youth at The Pepper Place and a goal of 12 young adults housed and supported annually, with a long-term goal of at least 100.
* Eligibility screening includes a drug test and background check, and youth with children or pets are not eligible.
* The age range differs across materials (18–23 on the site's help page vs. 18–24 in program materials and grant documents) — confirm current eligibility.
* Funding includes Mesa United Way and other community grants, including a February 2026 SRP grant for the housing program; services and bed availability can change, and no placement is guaranteed.

b08a-allthrive-365-affordable-housing · usable

AllThrive 365 — the nonprofit formerly known as Foundation for Senior Living (FSL) — owns and manages affordable apartment communities across Arizona, including six in the Phoenix area, for older adults, adults with disabilities, and families with limited incomes. Each community has its own age and income rules and a local manager who handles applications.

**Services Offered**
AllThrive 365 (the organization formerly called Foundation for Senior Living / FSL) owns and manages affordable apartment communities across Arizona, including six in the Greater Phoenix area:
- Acacia Heights — 78 units, ages 62+; 615 W. Pierson St., Phoenix 85013; manager Jeff Weist, 602-767-5812, JeffW@allthrive365.org
- Spectrum Courtyard — 25 units, family housing and adults with developmental disabilities; 5330 N. 21st Ave., Phoenix 85015; Jeff Weist, 602-767-5814, JeffW@allthrive365.org
- Vianney Villas Apartments — 50 units, ages 62+; 750 S. 4th St., Avondale 85323; Sylvia Ontiveros, 623-932-2036, SylviaO@allthrive365.org
- Sweetwater Gardens Apartments — 24 units, adults 18+ with mobility impairments; 2035 E. Sweetwater Ave., Phoenix 85022; Scott Van Soest, 602-867-4549, ScottV@allthrive365.org
- Village on Roeser — 112 units, ages 55+ and households 18+; 454 E. Roeser Rd., Phoenix 85040; Elvira Lopez, 602-268-5100, ElviraL@allthrive365.org
- 29 Palms Apartments — 21 units, ages 55+ and adults with developmental disabilities; 4901 E. Holly St., Phoenix 85008; Elvira Lopez, 602-595-7901, ElviraL@allthrive365.org
The organization also runs communities outside Maricopa County (Cottonwood, Flagstaff, Holbrook, Mayer, Payson, Prescott, Prescott Valley, Show Low, Williams, Coolidge, Yuma, Tucson, Lake Havasu City, Kingman and Wickenburg — full list at allthrive365.org/housing/affordable-housing), plus a Resource Navigator program that helps older adults and caregivers find services, and home energy-efficiency help.

**Eligibility Requirements**
Eligibility varies by property and is based on income and household size; each community is limited to a specific population — ages 62+, ages 55+, adults with disabilities (developmental disabilities or mobility impairments), or family housing. Exact income limits, rents and screening requirements are not published online; the manager for the specific community can explain the criteria and current limits.

**Population Served**
Older adults (55+ and 62+ communities), adults with developmental disabilities or mobility impairments, and families and individuals with limited incomes. Six communities are in the Phoenix metro area (Maricopa County); the rest are elsewhere in Arizona.

For a specific community, contact its manager directly using the names, phone numbers and emails listed above — managers handle applications and can explain availability, rents and paperwork. Note that "Foundation for Senior Living" and "FSL" now operate as AllThrive 365 (fsl.org redirects to allthrive365.org), so search both names when looking this up.

**Important Information to Know**
- The rebrand from Foundation for Senior Living (FSL) to AllThrive 365 took effect in April 2025 — it is the same housing program under a new name.

b08a-gift-of-mary-home · usable

Gift of Mary Home is a small emergency shelter for women in central Phoenix, operated by the Missionaries of Charity next to Our Lady of Fatima Parish Mission. It takes single women 18+ and, per published listings, women with young children; stays are short (about 30 days or less) and free. Intake is by phone — call before going.

**Services Offered**
Gift of Mary Home is a small emergency shelter for women in central Phoenix, run by the Missionaries of Charity (the religious order founded by St. Mother Teresa) next to Our Lady of Fatima Parish Mission. Published shelter information lists about 10 beds for single women plus a small number of beds for women with children, free of charge, with short stays (listed as about 30 days or less).

- Women 18 and older.
- Women with children have been accepted: a 2020 community resource list says girls of any age and boys age 5 or younger; another public listing says children 5 or younger. Confirm the current age limits by phone.
- A photo ID is required.
- Space is very limited — call first to check availability.

**Population Served**
Single adult women and mothers with young children who are experiencing homelessness in Phoenix and Maricopa County.

- Free shelter with no website of its own. Intake details (hours, child age limits, length of stay, capacity) come from published shelter listings and community reports rather than an official page — call first and confirm everything before traveling.
- The shelter is very small, so a bed should never be assumed.
- St. Mother Teresa established Gift of Mary Home next to the parish in 1989; the Missionaries of Charity sisters still serve there, and a November 2025 community item referenced donations to the home — it is believed to be operating, but confirm current status when you call.

UMOM New Day Centers / Helping Hands Housing Services — Permanent Affordable Housing Communities

b08a-umom-helping-hands-affordable-housing · usable

UMOM New Day Centers builds and operates permanent, income-restricted apartment communities around the Valley through its subsidiary, Helping Hands Housing Services (founded 1993, merged into UMOM in 2009). The communities are managed by Biltmore Properties, Inc., and applications go directly to the property — not through UMOM's shelter or crisis intake.

**Services Offered**
UMOM New Day Centers builds and operates permanent, income-restricted apartment communities around the Valley through its subsidiary, Helping Hands Housing Services. Current communities include:
- La Esperanza Terrace — 3101 W. McDowell Rd., Phoenix; 96 units (1–3 bedrooms); families at or below 60% of Area Median Income; opened April 2025; community room, computer lab, fitness center, playground; computer training, financial literacy and job-seeking support for residents.
- Salamanca Square — 4439 N. 39th Ave., Phoenix; under development (groundbreaking in 2026); planned at roughly 192 units (1–4 bedrooms) for families at 50–60% AMI; not leasing yet.
- 9 South — 60 units (2–3 bedrooms), opened 2023.
- Newsom Village — 60 units (2–3 bedrooms) in south Phoenix, opened 2022.
- Bethany Crossing — Glendale; 72 family units, opened 2021.
- 19 West — north Phoenix; 54 family units, opened 2019.
- South 7th Village — south Phoenix; community for people 55+, with priority given to veterans.
- Legacy Crossing — 80 permanent affordable units across from UMOM's main campus.
- Parsons Village — 48 units of affordable and permanent supportive housing, plus employment services, a computer lab and life-skills classes.
Many communities offer on-site resident services (job readiness, financial coaching, computer classes, English-language and life-skills support). UMOM states it aims to open one new affordable community per year.

**Eligibility Requirements**
- Income limits apply at every community (commonly at or below 60% of Area Median Income; newer projects at 50–60% AMI); standard rental screening (income verification, background/credit checks) is handled by the property manager.
- South 7th Village is for people 55+ (veterans priority); other communities primarily serve families and individuals.
- UMOM shelter or program participants may apply, but they receive no preference, and UMOM does not subsidize rent at its own properties.

**Population Served**
Low-income families and individuals, older adults 55+ (South 7th Village, with veterans priority), and people moving out of homelessness into permanent supportive housing (Parsons Village). Communities are in Maricopa County (Phoenix and Glendale).

**How to Best Connect**
- Apply to a specific community: all UMOM affordable communities are managed by Biltmore Properties, Inc. (main office: 2330 W. Mission Ln., Suite 10, Phoenix, AZ 85021; 602-997-0013, weekday hours). Leasing hours and waitlists vary by property — call ahead.
- Program questions: UMOM, 3333 E. Van Buren St., Phoenix, AZ 85008; 602-275-7852; umom.org/affordable-housing; listed office hours Monday–Friday 8 a.m.–5 p.m.
- If you are a family in an immediate housing crisis, call the Family Housing Hub at 602-595-8700 or 2-1-1 — the affordable communities are not crisis or shelter intake.

**Important Information to Know**
- These are permanent apartments, not emergency shelter; demand is high and waiting lists are common.
- Rents are income-restricted but not published (one project announced rents of roughly $828–$1,600 depending on unit size and income while it was under construction — confirm current figures).
- Applications go to the property manager; UMOM clients receive no preferential treatment.
- UMOM states it has built hundreds of Valley units to date (its site prints "over 1,00 units" — treat the exact total as approximate).
- Key links: umom.org/affordable-housing; umom.org/find-help; Maricopa County release on La Esperanza Terrace (maricopa.gov).

Homeward Bound — Thunderbird Apartments (A New Leaf)

b08a-homeward-bound-thunderbird · usable

Homeward Bound, a Phoenix nonprofit founded in 1990 to shelter families experiencing homelessness, was acquired by A New Leaf in 2024 and renamed and repositioned in early 2026. Its campus at 2302 W. Colter St. now operates as Thunderbird Apartments — 75 affordable two-bedroom homes with optional on-site support — and A New Leaf continues serving families in crisis through the countywide Family Housing Hub.

**Services Offered**
Thunderbird Apartments (the former Homeward Bound campus at 2302 W. Colter St.) offers 75 two-bedroom apartment homes (76 affordable units on the campus) for low-to-moderate-income households, with housing vouchers accepted and a waitlist possible depending on availability. On-site services are optional and include case management, employment guidance, financial coaching, life-skills opportunities and referrals; amenities include playgrounds, outdoor fitness, laundry, gated parking and a secure pedestrian gate. The campus also hosts the Strong Foundations Early Learning Center (licensed child care for children 0–5, serving residents and neighbors) and partner appointments with Homeless ID Project, Adelante Healthcare, Family Legal Services and Phoenix Children's Hospital, plus a residents-only food and basic-needs pantry. Homeward Bound's family shelter and transitional housing legacy continues under A New Leaf; families experiencing homelessness enter through the countywide Family Housing Hub.

**Eligibility Requirements**
- Thunderbird Apartments: income qualifications for low-to-moderate-income households. Published rental criteria include household income at least twice the monthly rent (or twice the portion not covered by a voucher), government photo ID for all adults, an established credit file (no minimum score), and a background review — felony convictions must be more than 3 years old with sentence completion at least 1 year ago; no violent felony (or drug-manufacturing/distribution) convictions within the last 5 years; drug- or alcohol-related convictions with no injury must be more than 6 months old; no active warrants; no registered sex offenders; and no evictions in the past 12 months. Chapter 7 bankruptcy must be discharged before applying and Chapter 13 must have been filed at least 1 year before; balances owed to prior landlords over $5,000 may cause denial; guarantors are allowed (income at least 4.5× rent, credit score 600 or higher). Lease start must be within 21 days of the unit's available date. Pets are allowed (maximum 2, 50 lbs or less each, breed restrictions). Households generally may not exceed 2 people per bedroom plus 1.
- Family shelter: for families with children; entry is through the Family Housing Hub, and placement is not guaranteed.

**Population Served**
Families and individuals with low-to-moderate incomes (apartments), families with children experiencing homelessness (shelter routing through the Family Housing Hub), and children ages 0–5 (early learning center). Services are based in Phoenix and serve the greater Valley/Maricopa County.

**How to Best Connect**
- Apartments: call the leasing office at 602-899-8089 (AZ Property Brokerage) or email thunderbirdapts@azpropertybrokerage.com; submit an application and meet income/screening criteria; waitlists possible. Current unit listings may be posted on the leasing agent's site ("Thunderbird Apartments on 23rd Ave," linked from A New Leaf's Thunderbird page). Address: 2302 W. Colter St., Phoenix, AZ 85015.
- Family shelter: Family Housing Hub — 602-595-8700 or 2-1-1 / 877-211-8661; the hub is on the UMOM campus at 3333 E. Van Buren St., Phoenix. Placement is not guaranteed.
- Child care: hbreception@turnanewleaf.org or 602-374-8770.
- General housing help: housing@turnanewleaf.org; A New Leaf main office: 868 E. University Dr., Mesa, AZ 85203; 480-969-4024.

**Important Information to Know**
- Homeward Bound was acquired by A New Leaf in 2024, and the property was officially renamed and repositioned as Thunderbird Apartments in early 2026 — longer-term affordable homes replacing the former short-term shelter model. Unit counts vary by source (75 two-bedroom homes on the current page; 76 campus units on the merger page; a 2026 news report describes renovating 80 apartments) — confirm current numbers with the leasing office. When a family says "Homeward Bound," this is the current operation (the old homewardboundaz.org site redirects to A New Leaf); families needing shelter now enter through the countywide Family Housing Hub.
- Costs: rents are set for low-to-moderate-income households; a leasing-agency unit listing seen 2026-09-28 showed a 2-bedroom at $995/month with electric, water/sewer/trash included. Confirm current availability, rent, what is included, and any deposits or fees before applying — published criteria state deposits and fees may be required in as little as 24 hours after acceptance.
- Federal subaward records show a December 2024 City of Mesa CDBG award to Homeward Bound ($65,300) for housing, case management, employment and navigation services for 70 people. Mesa's FY 2025/26 funding lists name other A New Leaf programs rather than Homeward Bound, and the FY 2026/27 plan could not be reviewed during this check — treat current Mesa contracting details as unverified and confirm with A New Leaf.
- Key links: turnanewleaf.org (Thunderbird Apartments page and 2026 fact sheets); turnanewleaf.org/homeward-bound-merger.

House of Miracles, Inc. (HOMI) · Transitional & supportive housing navigation and basic-needs referrals

b08b-homi-housing-navigation · usable

House of Miracles, Inc. (HOMI) is a Phoenix-based nonprofit that helps people who are unhoused or low-income find stable, supportive housing and connects them with food, clothing, hygiene supplies, mental-health and substance-use support, and job-readiness help. HOMI works as a resource and referral agency - it assists clients in locating transitional and supportive housing rather than advertising its own housing units.

**Services Offered**
HOMI helps individuals and families locate transitional and supportive housing - its website says it assists people in finding stable, safe, and supportive living arrangements - and its Community Cares/Contexture referral program connects clients to affordable and supportive housing, workforce and job-readiness assistance, clothing, nutrition assistance, and hygiene supplies. HOMI also offers food-assistance referrals around Maricopa County, SNAP outreach and application help, mental-health and substance-use counseling and education, employment readiness training, mentoring and life coaching, financial and nutrition workshops, and youth programs. Once someone becomes a client, HOMI completes an entrance assessment and matches services to their needs.

**Eligibility Requirements**
No formal eligibility rules are published. HOMI says it serves people facing barriers such as eviction history, poor credit, mental illness, substance use, low but sustainable income, and criminal convictions, and it receives daily social-service referrals from partner agencies through Unite Us/Community Cares. For housing assistance, contact HOMI to ask what it can offer in your situation and whether a partner-agency referral is needed.

**Population Served**
People who are unhoused or at risk of homelessness and low-income individuals and families in Maricopa County. HOMI states its programs reach seniors, teens, low-income families, unhoused people, people with mental-health or substance-use conditions, and people connected to the criminal justice system; it also describes a history of serving men and women in early recovery from drugs and alcohol and people transitioning from homelessness.

**How to Best Connect**
Call (623) 203-7381 or email admin@houseofmiraclesinc.com (alternate email: homihouse2020@gmail.com) to ask about housing navigation and the Community Cares/Contexture program. Partner agencies can refer clients through Unite Us/Community Cares. No walk-in hours are posted, so call or email first; if a case manager at another agency already works with the household, ask them to send a referral.

**Important Information to Know**
HOMI operates as a navigation and referral service rather than a confirmed housing provider - it is not clear whether HOMI directly leases or operates housing units, so ask exactly what housing it can connect you to and how long that may take. No fees or costs are published; confirm any costs directly. The website does not list walk-in hours; a federal NPI registration lists an office at 1951 W. Camelback Rd., Suite 255, Phoenix - confirm by phone before visiting. HOMI is a 501(c)(3) nonprofit (EIN 86-1009934) and states it is retired-veteran operated. HOMI does not advertise emergency shelter; for immediate shelter needs call 2-1-1 (1-877-211-8661).

website: https://houseofmiraclesinc.com/

House of Refuge · Transitional housing program for families experiencing homelessness (Mesa, AZ)

b08b-house-of-refuge-mesa · usable

House of Refuge is a faith-based nonprofit in southeast Mesa that provides transitional housing for families with children who are homeless, at risk of homelessness, or fleeing domestic violence. Families live in one of 88 furnished two-bedroom homes on a 20-acre neighborhood and receive case management, employment and education support, and children's programs while they work toward stable housing.

**Services Offered**
Furnished transitional housing in two-bedroom homes (88 homes on a 20-acre neighborhood in southeast Mesa) plus wrap-around services: an assigned case manager and individualized case plan, a housing plan for the move to permanent housing, employment and education support through an on-site Employment & Education Center, life-skills, financial-literacy and nutrition classes, after-school programming for children 5-11, a teen center for ages 12-17, and access to a donation center for clothing, furniture and household items plus a certified food bank. Participants sign a lease and pay $400 per month with utilities included; the program describes itself as a transitional housing work program with a roughly year-long stay.

**Eligibility Requirements**
You must be deemed homeless, have received an eviction notice, or be fleeing domestic violence; currently reside in Arizona; have verifiable income of at least $1,170 per month (examples: pay stubs, SSI, child support, spousal maintenance - cash jobs are not accepted) and maintain it throughout the stay; pass a urine drug test and a background check (no sex-offense charges or convictions); be drug- and alcohol-free; have an adult 18 or older to sign the lease; and have custody of at least one minor child living with you or be in your third trimester of pregnancy. If income at move-in is not from employment, you must agree to gain employment within 45 days. Eligibility does not guarantee acceptance.

**Population Served**
Families with at least one minor child and pregnant women in their third trimester who are homeless, at risk of homelessness, or fleeing domestic violence. House of Refuge reports serving about 160 families (514 individuals) in a recent year, with children making up roughly 62% of residents.

**How to Best Connect**
Call 480-988-9242 or email info@houseofrefuge.org. When the program has openings, the Housing Inquiry Form is posted on the Need Housing page at houseofrefuge.org/need-housing/ - the page says to check back often. Administrative/mailing office: 6935 E. Williams Field Rd., Mesa, AZ 85212; the housing neighborhood is in southeast Mesa. House of Refuge does not offer emergency or immediate shelter - if you need shelter right away, dial 211 (1-877-211-8661).

**Important Information to Know**
Costs: $400 per month rent (utilities included); new residents have also been required since January 1, 2025 to pay a $200 security deposit and the last month's rent ($400) at move-in. The inquiry form is only available when the program has openings, so it may not be possible to apply at any given time - call to ask about openings and timing. The program requires work, minimum income, sobriety, background and drug screening, Arizona residency, and custody of a minor child (or third-trimester pregnancy). Eligibility does not guarantee acceptance, and there may be wait times. The program is faith-based and located in Mesa.

phone: 480-988-9242

address: 6935 E. Williams Field Rd., Mesa, AZ 85212 (administrative/mailing office; housing neighborhood in southeast Mesa)

website: https://houseofrefuge.org/

hours: Administrative office: Monday-Friday, 9 a.m.-4 p.m. Housing intake is by phone/email/inquiry form; the program is not an emergency shelter - for immediate shelter call 211 (1-877-211-8661).

resolutionReason: Retained as a usable Housing resource: current official pages establish the Mesa transitional-housing program, eligibility and costs, and the phone/email/Housing Inquiry Form intake route; openings are limited and the inquiry form is posted only when openings exist.
