Phoenix Dream Center · Life Recovery Program (residential recovery for men and women)

b11a-phoenix-dream-center-life-recovery · usable

No-cost, Christ-centered residential recovery for men and women leaving homelessness and addiction, with 160 beds for young men and 45 beds for young women, onsite medical and behavioral health care, eye and dental care, and career training. Intake by phone.

**Services Offered**
A free residential recovery program for people who are homeless and/or struggling with addiction. It provides safe housing (160 beds for young men and 45 beds for young women), structured daily programming, addiction-recovery classes, counseling and mentoring, an onsite medical center, a behavioral health team, eye care and dental care, physical wellness programs, life-skills training and career/job preparation. The center reports serving about 500 people a year through this program. The organization also runs a crisis-response outreach team and a separate residential program for survivors of human trafficking (Where Hope Lives).

**Eligibility Requirements**
The program is for men and women dealing with addiction and homelessness; all services are described as completely free, and the center says its services are not conditional on a person's faith. Specific rules — age range, medical or mental-health criteria, program length — are not published. Call the men's or women's intake line to confirm fit and requirements.

**Population Served**
Men and women; the center describes its beds as for young men and young women and serves people leaving homelessness, addiction and related crises. Exact age limits are not published — confirm by phone. The organization also describes serving people who have experienced incarceration (including formerly incarcerated individuals) or foster care, and a separate program serves trafficking survivors.

**How to Best Connect**
Call the program directly: men's Life Recovery 602-816-6582; women's Life Recovery 602-706-1876; general assistance 602-346-8716; main line 602-346-8700. Or use the contact form on the Get Help page: phoenixdreamcenter.org/get-help. The page advises that call lines are busy and asks callers to allow up to 72 business hours for a response; if you are in an emergency or an unsafe situation, call 911 or seek medical care. Location: 3210 Grand Ave., Phoenix, AZ 85017 (public tours Tuesdays at noon; for help, call first).

**Important Information to Know**
There is no charge for the recovery program or its services. The program is faith-based (Christ-centered) while the center states services are not conditional on faith. Current openings and wait times are not published — ask when you call. Length of stay, age limits and screening rules are not published and older community listings may not reflect current policy, so verify before referring.

phone: 602-816-6582 (men's program); 602-706-1876 (women's program); 602-346-8716 (general assistance); 602-346-8700 (main)

address: 3210 Grand Ave., Phoenix, AZ 85017

website: https://phoenixdreamcenter.org/get-help/

hours: Not published; intake is by phone — allow up to 72 business hours for a callback

Phoenix Rescue Mission · Changing Lives Center (women and children residential recovery)

b11a-prm-changing-lives-center · usable

Phoenix Rescue Mission's women-and-children campus: a 12-month, faith-based residential recovery program plus a 7–30 day short-term crisis stay for women facing homelessness, addiction and trauma; children can stay with their mothers, with childcare and school continuity. Intake by appointment.

**Services Offered**
Residential recovery for women and their children at the Changing Lives Center for Women & Children. The core program, Transformations, is a 12-month faith-based residency with four 13-week phases: addiction and mental-health treatment classes, individual and group counseling, case management, life skills, workforce development and job preparation, legal support, medical care and medication access, and transportation to appointments. Childcare is provided for infants and toddlers, and a local elementary-school partnership keeps school-age children in class; parent reunification is a goal when child-welfare involvement exists. Graduates receive six months of aftercare plus alumni support. The first step can be short-term residential care (Rescue-Assess-Place): a typical 7–30 day stay with food, a bed, clothing, showers, assessment and referrals.

**Eligibility Requirements**
For women dealing with homelessness, addiction and/or trauma; mothers may bring their children. The programs are voluntary — the person must want change. Intake includes an assessment to place each person in the right program; age and medical criteria are not published. No fee is stated for the short-term program, and basic needs are covered in the year-long program — confirm any other costs at intake.

**Population Served**
Women, including mothers with children, experiencing homelessness, addiction and trauma. The Mission's short-term care also serves women fleeing domestic violence. Men are served separately at the Transforming Lives Center (1801 S. 35th Ave., Phoenix).

**How to Best Connect**
Call (602) 233-3000 to schedule an intake appointment. Intake office hours: Monday–Friday, 8 a.m.–4 p.m.; appointments are required and walk-ins are not allowed, but same-day appointments are often available. Location: Changing Lives Center for Women & Children, 338 N. 15th Ave., Phoenix, AZ 85007. Family members may call on behalf of a willing loved one. Program information: phoenixrescuemission.org/help/substance-abuse-trauma/recovery-program/.

**Important Information to Know**
The program is faith-based (Christ-centered). The short-term stay is typically 7–30 days; the recovery program runs 12 months in four 13-week phases. Appointments are required — no walk-ins. Current capacity, wait times and exact screening rules are not published; call to ask about openings. Some older listings show different intake numbers; (602) 233-3000 is the current intake line.

phone: 602-233-3000 (intake)

address: 338 N. 15th Ave., Phoenix, AZ 85007

website: https://phoenixrescuemission.org/help/substance-abuse-trauma/recovery-program/

hours: Intake office: Monday–Friday, 8 a.m.–4 p.m. (appointments required; no walk-ins)

Phoenix Rescue Mission · Street Outreach Hotline (Hope Coach) and Get Help line

b11a-prm-street-outreach · usable

Street-outreach hotline and help line for people living unsheltered in Maricopa County: request a Hope Coach visit (water, hygiene supplies, case management, ID help, housing navigation) or start the Mission's housing-assistance and residential-program intake by phone.

**Services Offered**
A hotline to request help from the Mission's street-outreach team: Hope Coach vehicles travel the streets of Maricopa County with water, hygiene kits (Hope Totes), seasonal items and case management from peer-support-certified case managers, help obtaining IDs and documents, and connection to housing or recovery programs. Through the Housing Assistance Program, staff help with housing applications, documents, locating an apartment, vouchers (decided case-by-case), security deposits and basic move-in needs, furniture and household items, and continuing case management. The Mission's short-term residential care (7–30 days) and recovery programs also start through the same intake line.

**Eligibility Requirements**
No eligibility rules are published for the outreach hotline; it serves people experiencing homelessness in Maricopa County. Residential programs have their own screening and are voluntary; housing vouchers are generally state- or federally funded and decided case-by-case. Requirements differ by program — ask when you call. Families with children are generally routed to Maricopa County's Family Housing Hub (602-595-8700).

**Population Served**
People living unsheltered in Maricopa County; the Mission describes outreach to men, women and children on the streets, and its case managers place hundreds of people into housing or recovery programs each year. Spanish-language pages and materials are available; ask about Spanish-speaking staff when you call.

**How to Best Connect**
Official pages list two phone routes: the Get Help page directs Hope Coach visit requests to 602-346-3361; the Homeless Outreach and Housing Assistance pages direct visit requests, intake and housing help to 602-233-3000. Give your nearest cross streets when requesting a visit. Online: help/inquiry form at phoenixrescuemission.org/help. Email: outreach@phxmission.org. Address for mail/administration: Mission Support Center, 1540 W. Van Buren St., Phoenix, AZ 85007 — call first; services are mobile or by appointment. Residential campuses: men's, 1801 S. 35th Ave.; women and children's, 338 N. 15th Ave.

**Important Information to Know**
This is not a walk-in emergency shelter — help starts with a phone call, and outreach teams come to people on the street. Both published numbers are official Mission routes; if one does not connect, try the other. For emergencies, call 911. No fees are listed for Mission programs; the short-term program states no fee and housing vouchers are case-by-case. Hotline hours and outreach schedules are not published — call to confirm; teams serve Maricopa County. Phoenix Rescue Mission serves as a Maricopa County coordinated-entry point for adults without children through this outreach hotline. Capacity and wait times are not published.

phone: 602-346-3361 (street outreach/Hope Coach hotline); 602-233-3000 (main line — outreach requests, intake, housing help)

address: 1540 W. Van Buren St., Phoenix, AZ 85007 (Mission Support Center — call first; outreach is mobile)

website: https://phoenixrescuemission.org/help/homeless-outreach/

hours: Hotline hours not published; residential intake office Monday–Friday, 8 a.m.–4 p.m. by appointment

Safe Place — Youth Crisis Entry (Ages 12–17), Maricopa County

b11b-safe-place-youth-crisis · usable

Immediate crisis help for youth ages 12–17 in Maricopa County who are homeless, have run away, or need help for any reason. Youth can call or text for help, or walk into any designated Safe Place site — QuikTrip stores, Valley Metro locations, libraries, fire departments, transit stops and participating businesses — ask staff for Safe Place, and wait in a safe, private area until responders arrive to arrange placement and services.

**Services Offered**
Safe Place is a national youth-outreach program. Youth ages 12–17 who have run away, are homeless, or need help for any reason can use it to get immediate assistance. A youth can go into any designated Safe Place site — look for the Safe Place sign — ask staff for help, and wait in a safe, private place until trained responders arrive. QuikTrip convenience stores, libraries, fire departments, public transit stops and local businesses across the Valley are designated Safe Place sites (per Maricopa County's current help page), and Child Crisis Arizona states that all QuikTrip and Valley Metro locations are designated as Safe Place locations. Youth (or anyone helping them) can also call the 24-hour crisis line at 602-841-5799 or text SAFE and their location/address to 44357 (4HELP). Child Crisis Arizona is the local Safe Place agency: its staff respond to these calls and several beds in its Independent Living Program are reserved for youth who need an overnight place to stay.

**Eligibility Requirements**
Youth ages 12–17 in Maricopa County who are runaway, homeless, or 'seeking assistance for any reason,' including family conflict and abuse. No income test, application, or formal referral is described — help starts by calling, texting, or going to a Safe Place site. The program serves youth only; adults need different entry points in the county's homeless services system.

**Population Served**
Youth ages 12–17 across Maricopa County, including Mesa and the East Valley, through valley-wide sites and transit access. Users include youth who are homeless, have run away, or are in an unsafe or unstable family situation.

**How to Best Connect**
- Call the 24-hour crisis line: 602-841-5799.
- Text SAFE plus your current location or address to 44357 (4HELP).
- Or go to any Safe Place site and ask staff for help: QuikTrip stores, libraries, fire departments, transit stops and other participating businesses across Maricopa County. Staff will contact the Safe Place line, and the youth can wait on site until help arrives; site staff can make the call for a youth who arrives in person.
Use the current instructions above (see the note below about the older 69866 code).

**Important Information to Know**
- The currently published instruction is to text SAFE and your location to 44357; the older code 69866 — still used by Safe Place programs elsewhere and in older Maricopa-area guides — was the earlier text-to-locate instruction. Use 44357 or the crisis line to avoid confusion.
- Child Crisis Arizona has been the local home of Safe Place since 2021; the program was run locally before that by Tumbleweed and then UMOM. The county's current help page lists the same crisis line for youth ages 12–17.
- What happens after contact depends on the situation and bed availability; overnight shelter is not guaranteed, and no costs or fees are published — ask when calling.

phone: 602-841-5799 (24-hour crisis line); text SAFE and your location to 44357

website: https://www.childcrisisaz.org/

hours: Crisis line answered 24 hours a day; Safe Place sites (QuikTrip stores, libraries, fire departments, public transit stops and other participating businesses) can be used during each site's public hours. Valley-wide sites and the phone/text lines cover Mesa and all of Maricopa County.

resolutionReason: Usable: currently published Maricopa County, regional Continuum of Care, and Child Crisis Arizona sources confirm Safe Place as the youth (12–17) crisis entry with working call/text routes (602-841-5799; text SAFE + location to 44357); 69866 is confirmed as the older/legacy instruction used in earlier Maricopa guides; the local operator history (Tumbleweed, then UMOM, then Child Crisis Arizona since 2021) and the Independent Living Program bed link are documented. Shelter bed availability is not guaranteed and is stated.

Sources: Maricopa County — Help with Homelessness (youth ages 12–17: call/text Safe Place at 44357 or 602-841-5799; stationary site network) · Child Crisis Arizona — 'Child Crisis Arizona is home to Safe Place' (local program; 24-hour crisis line; Independent Living Program beds; all QuikTrip and Valley Metro locations) · Maricopa Regional Continuum of Care — Get Help (youth ages 12–17: Safe Place 602-841-5799; text SAFE and address to 44357) · Maricopa Regional CoC Homeless Resources (older guide listing the legacy youth text code 69866)

Sanctuary Recovery Centers / Sanctuary Sober Living — Residential Treatment and Recovery Housing, North Phoenix

b11b-sanctuary-recovery-centers · usable

Residential behavioral-health treatment with integrated substance-use care and built-in supportive housing at licensed recovery homes in the Phoenix–Scottsdale area. The core model is a recommended 90-day residential program, with step-down to PHP, IOP, virtual IOP, sober living, and case management; Phoenix sites include SRC 28th (17632 N 28th St) and SRC Friess (4324 E Friess Dr), plus additional provider-listed homes. Entry is through admissions and a clinical assessment, not a rental application.

**Services Offered**
- Residential (inpatient-style) behavioral health treatment with integrated substance-use care; a 90-day residential stay is the recommended core program, with stabilization and, when clinically appropriate, ambulatory detox as part of the plan.
- Supportive housing is built into the residential program — clients live in the agency's licensed behavioral health residential homes during treatment — and continued care includes sober living, a partial hospitalization program (PHP), intensive outpatient (IOP), virtual IOP, case management, medication management (including medication-based treatments for opioid and alcohol use disorders), and individual, group, and family therapy.
- Homes listed by the provider include The Friess Home, The Rose Garden, and The Roadrunner Home (the adolescent home), among others (Blossom, Rosemonte, Charter Oak, Mercer). Registry and Medicaid-directory addresses include SRC 28th (17632 N 28th St) and SRC Friess (4324 E Friess Dr), with SRC Rose Garden at 2027 E Rose Garden Ln; the administrative office is 11645 N Cave Creek Rd, Phoenix 85020.
- The provider also runs a reentry program (work and life skills) and discharge/next-step planning; the provider states it is Joint Commission certified at all levels of care and LegitScript certified.

**Eligibility Requirements**
- Adults who need residential-level care for substance use and/or co-occurring mental-health conditions; admission follows a clinical assessment arranged through the admissions/intake line.
- Directory listings describe the Friess and Rose Garden homes as adult men's residences (ages 18–25 and 26–64), and the operator describes gender-specific homes for adults — confirm current gender and age rules for the specific home at intake.
- The agency also runs a separate adolescent program (ages 12–17) at its Roadrunner Home in Phoenix; that is a different program from the adult recovery residences described here.
- No income-based eligibility is published; payment and coverage are reviewed at intake.

**Population Served**
Adults (18+) in the Phoenix metro area with substance-use and co-occurring mental-health needs who require residential structure and recovery housing; at least two homes (Friess, Rose Garden) are listed as serving adult men, and the provider states its homes are gender-specific. Separate adolescent services exist for ages 12–17.

**How to Best Connect**
- Call admissions/intake: 877-978-8783 or 844-978-8783 (toll-free), 480-309-9945 (local/intake), or 602-612-3816 (registry-listed main number).
- Website: sanctuaryrecoverycenters.com (contact form and insurance verification). Ask directly about current openings, an assessment appointment, which home fits (SRC 28th vs. Friess vs. Rose Garden), and coverage.
- Office: 11645 N Cave Creek Rd, Phoenix, AZ 85020; office hours Monday–Friday 8:30 a.m.–5:30 p.m.
- If you have AHCCCS/Medicaid or private insurance, ask intake to confirm network participation for the specific home before committing.

**Important Information to Know**
- This is treatment-linked housing: entry is through a clinical admissions process (assessment), not a housing waitlist; expect a structured program rather than a standard lease.
- Costs are not published, and coverage signals conflict: one third-party directory for the Friess home lists private and public insurance accepted but no payment assistance or sliding fee scale; listings for other homes show more limited insurance data; the registry record carries an Arizona Medicaid identifier. Verify cost and coverage for the specific home at intake; low- or no-cost placement is not established.
- Bed availability and exact referral requirements are not published; call for current capacity. The company is registered in the national provider registry as a substance-abuse rehabilitation facility and describes its homes as licensed behavioral health residential properties.
- Numbers and hours differ across pages and directories (the website displays multiple phone lines; a Medicaid-plan directory lists SRC 28th as Monday–Friday 9 a.m.–4 p.m. while the operator site lists office hours 8:30 a.m.–5:30 p.m.) — admissions can route callers and confirm a specific home's hours.
- A third-party description of the reentry/sober-living track says securing work is required for continued housing — confirm this for the specific program at intake.

ARM of Save the Family — Affordable Rental Housing (Mesa and Maricopa County)

b11b-stf-arm · usable

ARM (Affordable Rental Movement) is Save the Family's affiliated affordable-housing nonprofit: more than 170 scattered single-family rental homes across Mesa, Tempe, Scottsdale, Gilbert and Chandler, plus co-developed affordable communities — including Escobedo at Verde Vista in Mesa (families and individuals, with a focus on people facing behavioral and mental-health challenges), Valor on 8th in Tempe (veteran women and families) and the upcoming Phoenix Scholar House. Supportive services are available to ARM residents. The ARM waitlist is temporarily closed to new applications — call for status.

**Services Offered**
- Permanent affordable rental housing through more than 170 scattered single-family homes across Mesa, Tempe, Scottsdale, Gilbert, and Chandler (ARM page; 2025 annual report).
- Co-developed affordable communities: Escobedo at Verde Vista in Mesa (families and individuals, with a focus on people experiencing behavioral and mental-health challenges); Valor on 8th (created with veteran women and families in mind); and the upcoming Phoenix Scholar House (planned to provide affordable housing along with academic and family support services for low-income single parents with children).
- Each complex includes a Save the Family Resource Center where a case manager provides workforce-development workshops and access to community resources; Save the Family's supportive services — career help, budgeting, parenting and life-skills classes, children's programming, and mental-health support — are available to ARM residents.
- Rental assistance is provided through HUD HOME funds, so rent is based on household income.

**Eligibility Requirements**
- Income at or below 60% of the HOME income limits (2025 limits effective June 1, 2025).
- Applicants must be employed and/or have stable, verifiable income; when a unit is available, at least six months of verifiable income and financial documentation is required for all household members over 18.
- No pets allowed; service and therapy/emotional support animals require supporting documentation.
- Reasonable accommodations can be requested, including help reading/completing the application and Spanish-to-English translation onsite.
- The waitlist is currently closed to new applications (temporarily, due to high volume) — ask about reopening. Existing applicants can only confirm whether they are on the list, and address/contact changes must be submitted in writing.

**Population Served**
Low-income working families and individuals in the East Valley. Specific communities focus on particular populations: Escobedo at Verde Vista (families and individuals, including people with behavioral and mental-health challenges), Valor on 8th (veteran women and families), and Phoenix Scholar House (single parents with children, in development).

**How to Best Connect**
- Call (480) 898-0228 Monday–Friday, 8 a.m.–5 p.m., and ask about 'ARM of Save the Family,' the waitlist status, and how to apply when it reopens.
- Online: savethefamily.org/arm for ARM details, the income chart, and waitlist notices; the office is at 125 E. University Dr., Mesa, AZ 85201.
- For existing applications or address/contact updates, use the email or mail instructions on the ARM page — phone changes are not accepted.
- If your family is currently experiencing homelessness and needs Save the Family's supportive housing programs, the county's family entry point is the Family Housing Hub, 3307 E. Van Buren St., Phoenix, 602-595-8700. ARM is the organization's affordable-housing side, separate from its temporary and rapid-rehousing programs.

**Important Information to Know**
- ARM is affordable rental housing, not emergency shelter or short-term rental assistance; rent is income-based (amounts are not published — ask).
- The waitlist is temporarily closed to new applications at the time of this research; the ARM page says it will reopen at a future date.
- Vacancies are unpredictable and ARM cannot anticipate where they will occur; applicants who decline two offered units are removed from the waitlist.
- ARM is funded through HUD; housing is also supported by the Maricopa County HOME Consortium, Arizona Department of Housing, and City of Mesa HOME funds.
- No pets (with service/emotional-support animal documentation exceptions).

phone: (480) 898-0228

address: 125 E. University Dr, Mesa, AZ 85201

website: https://savethefamily.org/

hours: Office: Monday–Friday 8:00 a.m.–5:00 p.m.; closed Saturday and Sunday.

Save the Family — Rapid Rehousing and FACES Supportive Services (Mesa)

b11b-stf-rrh-faces · usable

Rapid Rehousing for families with children facing homelessness in Mesa and Maricopa County — help finding housing plus time-limited, stepped-down rental assistance — paired with Save the Family's FACES (Families, Adults and Children's Empowerment Services) supportive services, case management, and children's programming on the path to permanent housing and self-sufficiency.

**Services Offered**
- Rapid Rehousing: help locating housing in the community (in many cases families hold their own lease) and time-limited, stepped-down rental assistance — public descriptions include help with utility deposits and rental application fees, with support scaled back as the family's income grows.
- Case management: a case manager helps parents set and reach goals — employment, budgeting, benefits, documentation, referrals — throughout the family's journey and after move-in.
- FACES (Families, Adults and Children's Empowerment Services): the organization's supportive-services program — career development services, parent support services (life skills, budgeting, parenting, domestic-violence/trauma healing, legal services), mental health and substance-abuse support, and children's services (childcare, after-school/summer programs). FACES also serves residents of ARM affordable housing.
- Additional programs: Save the Family also operates Tenant-Based Rental Assistance and Step Up to Independence transitional housing (described as up to two years, targeting families in doubled-up or temporary situations); ask which programs are accepting families now.

**Eligibility Requirements**
- Families with children who are experiencing homelessness; the program serves parents and children together, and children's programming is a standard part of services.
- Families in Maricopa County are screened through the Family Housing Hub, the county's coordinated-entry point for families — call 602-595-8700. Save the Family states it accepts incoming clients from the Family Housing Hub.
- Specific income rules and required documents are not published; intake staff will explain what is needed.

**Population Served**
Families with children who are homeless or facing homelessness in Mesa, the East Valley, and Maricopa County. Program descriptions include families in shelter and families who are doubled up, living in cars, or couch-surfing.

**How to Best Connect**
- First step for families: call the Family Housing Hub at 602-595-8700 (3307 E. Van Buren St., Phoenix) — the county's coordinated entry for families — and ask about Save the Family's programs.
- Direct questions: Save the Family, (480) 898-0228, 125 E. University Dr., Mesa, AZ 85201; office hours Monday–Friday 8 a.m.–5 p.m. Ask for Rapid Rehousing and supportive-services (FACES) information.
- For Step Up to Independence, Save the Family's Find Help page links a pre-screen questionnaire; website savethefamily.org ('How We Help' and 'Find Help').

**Important Information to Know**
- Rapid Rehousing is time-limited, stepped-down rental assistance — not a guaranteed long-term subsidy or an immediate placement. Current openings depend on funding and capacity, which are not published; call to check.
- Eligibility is not guaranteed by the office; families are screened through the coordinated-entry process, and participation includes working with a case manager.
- Save the Family describes support over 6–12 months as families build income and stability, with follow-up care continuing up to six months after exit.
- Costs: families take on more of the rent as assistance steps down; exact rent-share and any fees are not published — ask intake.

phone: (480) 898-0228 (Save the Family); 602-595-8700 (Family Housing Hub intake)

address: 125 E. University Dr, Mesa, AZ 85201

website: https://savethefamily.org/

Society of St. Vincent de Paul (Phoenix) · Coordinated Entry Access Point and Charitable Assistance

b12a-svdp-ce-access · usable

St. Vincent de Paul's Phoenix campus is a central charitable-help location in Maricopa County and is listed as a coordinated-entry access point for seniors experiencing homelessness. It connects people to shelter and housing resources and offers one-time rent/utility help through neighborhood church conferences, food and meals (including a Mesa dining room), weather relief, and medical/dental services. Call 602-266-4673 (602-266-HOPE) to confirm what is available before going.

**Services Offered**
- Coordinated-entry access point for seniors experiencing homelessness, listed at SVdP's 420 W. Watkins Rd. campus, connecting people to shelter, services and housing resources.
- One-time rent, mortgage and utility assistance through volunteer-run St. Vincent de Paul conferences based in neighborhood Catholic churches; each conference is limited to its own geographic area.
- Food boxes and hot meals, including the Mesa Dining Room at 49 W. Broadway Rd. (meals daily 9:30–11:30 a.m.; summer heat relief with cold water, air conditioning and temporary shelter June 1–Sept. 30, 12–4:30 p.m.).
- Shelter and seasonal weather relief across the Valley; medical and dental clinics for people without insurance (adult dental uses a lottery); diabetes classes in English and Spanish; pet services (veterinary care, food and supplies) for people at risk of or experiencing homelessness.
- Help finding the correct neighborhood conference for your address.

**Eligibility Requirements**
- Call for eligibility screening; required documents depend on the service, and fees vary by service.
- Rent/utility assistance is one-time and restricted to the neighborhood served by each volunteer conference.
- Medical clinic: uninsured or under-insured patients. Dental clinic: uninsured patients; adult dental uses a lottery.
- The coordinated-entry resource list that includes this site focuses on seniors experiencing homelessness.

**Population Served**
- People in Maricopa County who are experiencing homelessness or at risk of homelessness; the senior resource list places a coordinated-entry access point at SVdP's campus for seniors.
- SVdP describes its wider programs as serving older adults, adults living with disabilities, veterans and work-ready adults, plus families (the family meal program requires children under 18).

**How to Best Connect**
- Call 602-266-4673 (602-266-HOPE); email info@svdpaz.org. Campus: 420 W. Watkins Rd., Phoenix, AZ 85003.
- Hours vary by listing: the Main Campus page lists Monday–Saturday 8 a.m.–5 p.m.; the senior coordinated-entry listing lists Monday–Friday 8 a.m.–4 p.m.; 2-1-1 lists Monday–Friday 8 a.m.–5 p.m. for administration offices. Call before visiting.
- Rent/utility help: find your neighborhood conference at stvincentdepaul.net (Get Help) or call 2-1-1; if no conference serves your area, call 602-619-9903.
- Transitional-housing screening (Ozanam Manor, De Paul Manor, Rosalie's Place) runs on a separate line: 602-850-6900, 8 a.m.–3 p.m. For SVdP's year-round overnight shelter at the Phoenix Dining Room, call 602-478-8172 about open beds.
- Mesa Dining Room: 49 W. Broadway Rd., Mesa; 602-619-9411.

**Important Information to Know**
- Coordinated-entry routing differs by population: Maricopa County's current homeless-services page directs single adults (Brian Garcia Welcome Center, 602-229-5155), families (Family Housing Hub, 602-595-8700), veterans (VA Community Resource & Referral Center, 602-248-6040), youth 12–17 (Safe Place, 602-841-5799) and domestic-violence survivors (SAFEDVS, 480-890-3039 or 844-723-3387) to other entry points; SVdP is not on that county list — confirm which entry point fits before going specifically for coordinated entry.
- Help is limited and not guaranteed: conference funds, hours and capacity vary; some services are by appointment or lottery; no place is reserved by calling.

Society of St. Vincent de Paul (Phoenix) · Ozanam Manor (Interim/Transitional Housing)

b12a-ozanam-manor · usable

Ozanam Manor is a St. Vincent de Paul interim/transitional housing community in Phoenix for men and women experiencing homelessness who are 55 or older or 18+ with a disability, with select beds for veterans. Residents get a bed in a dorm-style community, case management, activities and help moving into permanent housing. Intake starts with a phone screening at 602-850-6900 (8 a.m.–3 p.m.), followed by an in-person interview. No cost if you have no income; otherwise 30% of income.

**Services Offered**
- Interim/transitional housing at Ozanam Manor, part of St. Vincent de Paul's Interim Housing Communities in Phoenix (alongside De Paul Manor and Rosalie's Place).
- Residents have a bed in a dormitory-style community and can join group activities and life courses; case managers meet one-on-one to work toward independent living and finding an affordable place to live.
- The same intake line screens for SVdP's other transitional sites — De Paul Manor (310 W. Watkins Rd.) and Rosalie's Place (320 W. Watkins Rd.).

**Eligibility Requirements**
- Adults 55+; adults 18+ living with a disability; adults 18+ working or looking for work (workforce housing); select beds for veterans at Ozanam Manor — veterans should work through the VA.
- A phone pre-screen is followed by a face-to-face interview with a case manager to confirm the program is a good fit.

**Population Served**
- Men and women experiencing homelessness who are older adults (55+), adults 18+ with a physical or mental disability, or adults 18+ working or looking for work; veterans (select beds).
- SVdP describes its interim housing communities as serving veterans, seniors and adults living with disabilities.

**How to Best Connect**
- Call 602-850-6900 between 8 a.m. and 3 p.m. for eligibility screening; then an in-person interview if screened in.
- Location: 320 W. Watkins Rd., Phoenix, AZ 85003. Program page: stvincentdepaul.net/locations/ozanam-manor.
- Veterans: work through the VA to enter Ozanam Manor's veteran beds.

**Important Information to Know**
- Cost: no cost if you have no income; otherwise 30% of income.
- Bed availability and waitlists vary; a pre-screen does not guarantee a bed.
- Age rule: current SVdP materials say 55+; older listings sometimes said 50+ — confirm at screening.
- Ozanam Manor is separate from SVdP's coordinated-entry/charity campus nearby; transitional housing intake runs on its own phone line (602-850-6900).

phone: 602-850-6900

address: 320 W. Watkins Rd., Phoenix, AZ 85003

website: https://stvincentdepaul.net/locations/ozanam-manor

Sojourner Center · Emergency Domestic-Violence Shelter and 24/7 Hotline

b12a-sojourner-center · usable

Sojourner Center runs one of Maricopa County's largest domestic-violence shelters and a 24/7 hotline. It offers up to 120 days of emergency shelter with 24-hour staff, children's services and pet support, plus transitional housing for up to two years. Call the 24/7 hotline at (602) 244-0089 for safety planning, screening and current availability; the shelter location is confidential.

**Services Offered**
- 24/7 hotline for crisis support, safety planning and direct access to shelter when available.
- Emergency shelter: up to 120 days of services in a secure living area with 24-hour staff support; trauma-informed case management; three meals daily; children's and youth services; a licensed Quality First child development center (birth to age 12); pet companion services (pet daycare and support); support groups and workshops; on-site community partner services (health, behavioral health, housing, workforce); and lay legal support for orders of protection and court.
- Transitional housing: up to two years of supportive services in 32 transitional apartments; referrals by email at TransitionalHousingReferrals@sojournercenter.org.
- Community outreach: mobile case management and safety planning for survivors who are not seeking shelter; the outreach team can be reached at (602) 244-0089 during business hours (in addition to the 24/7 hotline).
- Sojourner says it operates about 30% of Maricopa County's emergency shelter beds for domestic-violence survivors and their children.

**Eligibility Requirements**
- For people affected by domestic violence, sexual assault or human trafficking, including adults and children; call the hotline for screening and current availability.
- Emergency shelter stays up to 120 days; transitional housing up to 2 years by referral.
- Pets are supported; confirm pet logistics at screening.

**Population Served**
- Survivors of domestic violence, sexual assault and human trafficking in the Phoenix area (Maricopa County), including adults and children; families with children are served.

**How to Best Connect**
- Call the 24-hour hotline at (602) 244-0089 — call 911 if in immediate danger. The hotline does screening and can connect directly to shelter when available.
- Main office: (602) 244-0997; info@sojournercenter.org; mail: P.O. Box 20156, Phoenix, AZ 85036. The shelter location is confidential and access is by phone.
- Transitional housing referrals: TransitionalHousingReferrals@sojournercenter.org.
- Other support: National Domestic Violence Hotline 800-799-7233; 2-1-1 Arizona.

**Important Information to Know**
- Availability varies and waitlists are possible. Maricopa County's current homeless-services page lists SAFEDVS (480-890-3039 or 844-723-3387) as the county domestic-violence crisis line; Sojourner's own 24/7 hotline remains active, and Sojourner says it strengthened hotline capacity when the county centralized line closed (May 2026).
- Name check: some older lists tie 'Autumn House' to this shelter; current public information describes Autumn House as a separate East Valley shelter run by A New Leaf (480-890-3039). If you were told to call 'Autumn House,' confirm the agency.
- No fee is published for shelter; confirm program rules, pet arrangements and child development center enrollment when you call.

phone: (602) 244-0089 (24/7 hotline); (602) 244-0997 (main office)

address: Confidential shelter location, Phoenix; mailing address: P.O. Box 20156, Phoenix, AZ 85036

website: https://www.sojournercenter.org/

Southwest Behavioral & Health Services (SB&H) · HOPWA Housing Programs (HIV/AIDS)

b12a-sbh-hopwa · usable

Southwest Behavioral & Health Services (SB&H) is a long-time HOPWA housing provider for people living with HIV/AIDS in Maricopa and Pinal counties. Its HOPWA program provides transitional housing (up to 24 months in leased or agency-owned units) with intensive supportive services and help moving toward permanent housing; clients contribute a portion of income toward rent, and referrals come through Care Directions' Housing Information Services (602-264-2273). Call SB&H at 602-285-4288 or 602-265-8338 to ask about current openings.

**Services Offered**
- HOPWA transitional housing: leased or agency-owned units for up to 24 months with intensive supportive services, aimed at moving residents into permanent housing; residents contribute a portion of their income toward rent.
- Housing case management and support: intake, screening and assessment; individualized housing plans; unit oversight; referrals and follow-up; help with health, income and community resources.
- SB&H has provided HOPWA-funded housing and supportive services since 1995 (per City of Phoenix funding materials) and describes itself as the largest provider of housing and support services for persons with HIV (HOPWA).
- The broader City of Phoenix HOPWA network (accessed through the routes below) also offers permanent supportive housing, permanent independent housing, tenant-based rental assistance through the Section 8 office, short-term rent/mortgage/utility assistance (STRMU), move-in assistance (PHP), housing information/referrals and employment services.

**Eligibility Requirements**
- At least one household member diagnosed with HIV or AIDS; household income at or below 80% of the Area Median Income; currently residing in Maricopa or Pinal counties.
- STRMU requires that the household is not receiving housing or utility assistance from another housing program.
- Transitional housing residents pay part of their income toward rent; ask about documentation (HIV diagnosis, income) at intake.

**Population Served**
- Low-income people living with HIV/AIDS and their families in Maricopa and Pinal counties.

**How to Best Connect**
- Call SB&H: 602-285-4288 (information/appointment line; ask for the HOPWA housing program) or 602-265-8338 (main office). Website: sbhservices.org (Services → Housing).
- Referral route: Care Directions Housing Information Services, 602-264-2273 — the City's HOPWA housing information program, which matches callers to HOPWA housing providers in Maricopa and Pinal counties (1366 E. Thomas Rd., Suite 108, Phoenix, AZ 85014).
- For STRMU/move-in assistance: City of Phoenix Human Services Central Intake, (602) 534-AIDE (2433), option #3; or apply online at phoenix.gov/HOPWA; business hours Monday–Friday 8 a.m.–5 p.m.
- No walk-in hours are published for the housing program; call ahead to confirm.

**Important Information to Know**
- Phone history: older materials list a 2020-era SB&H HOPWA line at 602-318-1715 (program appointments at 5717 N. 7th St.) that may be dated; the City's current HOPWA Fact Sheet (revised 12.3.2025) lists 480-589-5890 for SB&H's HOPWA Employment Services. Start with SB&H 602-285-4288 / 602-265-8338 and Care Directions 602-264-2273.
- Availability and waitlists vary; HOPWA eligibility is income- and diagnosis-based; this is not an emergency shelter — if you need shelter tonight, call 2-1-1.
- Help is limited to Maricopa and Pinal counties.
- Costs: transitional housing has an income-based resident contribution; STRMU/move-in help depends on eligibility and available funding.

Prisma Community Care (formerly Southwest Center for HIV/AIDS) – HIV housing assistance (HOPWA)

b12b-prisma-hopwa · usable

Phoenix HIV/AIDS health and wellness organization (renamed from Southwest Center for HIV/AIDS on Jan. 1, 2025) that people living with HIV/AIDS can contact about housing assistance; the City of Phoenix HOPWA program it is connected to provides short-term rent, mortgage and utility help, move-in costs and housing case management in Maricopa and Pinal counties.

**Services Offered**
Prisma Community Care — formerly Southwest Center for HIV/AIDS — is a Phoenix HIV/AIDS health and wellness organization that people living with HIV/AIDS can contact about housing assistance. It is one of the local organizations funded through the City of Phoenix HOPWA (Housing Opportunities for Persons with AIDS) program. HOPWA housing help in this area includes short-term rent, mortgage and utility payments to stop an eviction, foreclosure or utility shut-off; move-in costs such as security deposits, first and last month's rent, and application fees; and housing case management. Ask specifically about HOPWA housing assistance when you call.

**Eligibility Requirements**
At least one household member must be diagnosed with HIV or AIDS; household income must be at or below 80% of area median income; the household must currently live in Maricopa or Pinal County; and for short-term rent/utility help, the household generally must not already be receiving housing or utility assistance from another housing program. Prisma serves people living with HIV and the wider community; final eligibility for each housing program is decided by the agency administering it.

**Population Served**
People living with or affected by HIV/AIDS and their families, including partners and spouses. The organization also serves 2SLGBTQIA+ people, people of color, and uninsured or underinsured Arizonans. Services are provided in English and Spanish, with interpretation available for other languages.

**How to Best Connect**
Call Prisma at 602-307-5330 (1101 N. Central Ave., Ste. 200, Phoenix; email contactus@prismacare.org) and ask about HOPWA housing assistance. The citywide HOPWA intake is also available: call 602-534-AIDE (602-534-2433), choose option 3, then choose a Family Services Center, then option 3 again to speak with staff — say you are asking about HOPWA. You can also apply online at phoenix.gov/HOPWA. Intake hours are Monday–Friday, 8 a.m.–5 p.m. The office is in downtown Phoenix; plan for a drive or transit trip from Mesa.

**Important Information to Know**
The organization changed its name from Southwest Center for HIV/AIDS on January 1, 2025 — both names mean the same place. Prisma's website highlights medical and wellness services and does not spell out its housing help, so confirm what housing support is currently available for your situation when you call; the citywide HOPWA intake line is the most reliable route to HOPWA housing programs. HOPWA help is grant-funded for eligible households and is not guaranteed — waitlists, appointment availability and funding vary. Published office hours differ slightly between Prisma's website and other directories, so call before visiting. Expect to verify HIV diagnosis, income and county of residence during eligibility screening.

Tempe Community Action Agency (TCAA) – I-HELP Emergency Shelter

b12b-tcaa-ihelp · usable

Rotating overnight emergency shelter for adults experiencing homelessness (men and women), run by Tempe Community Action Agency with faith-community host sites; about 40 spaces with meals, showers, laundry and case management; intakes take place weekday mornings and Mesa's emergency-solutions funding supports this shelter.

**Services Offered**
Overnight emergency shelter for adults experiencing homelessness, operated by Tempe Community Action Agency (TCAA) through a rotating network of faith-community host sites in Tempe. Participants receive a safe sleeping space each night, evening meals, shower and laundry services, and intensive case management and resource navigation aimed at moving from the street to shelter to housing. Daytime resources at TCAA's services site include computers and phones, employment help through TCAA's Financial Success Center, emergency to-go food bags and a hydration station. The shelter serves up to about 40 adults at a time.

**Eligibility Requirements**
Adults (18 and older) who are experiencing homelessness — men and women. Intakes are handled in person on weekday mornings at 2146 E. Apache Blvd., Tempe; call first to confirm the current intake window and process. A City of Mesa resource list notes that I-HELP does not accept individuals with sex-offender status. Whether the Mesa funding contract gives priority to particular households (for example seniors or couples) could not be confirmed — ask staff what is currently being accepted.

**Population Served**
Adults experiencing homelessness in Tempe and the East Valley, including men and women. Mesa's emergency-solutions grant funding supports this shelter for FY 2025/26, and City of Mesa and East Valley shelter lists include it among options for people in the area.

**How to Best Connect**
Call TCAA's I-HELP shelter line at (480) 389-1277 — or the intake-scheduling line at (480) 590-5514 — to check availability, or go to the intake site at 2146 E. Apache Blvd., Tempe, on a weekday morning. Published intake details differ: TCAA's program page lists intakes 8–10 a.m., while a 211 listing updated April 2026 describes arriving at 8 a.m. to get on a first-come waitlist, with intakes between 10 a.m. and 2 p.m. Call first to confirm which process is in effect. Space is limited (about 40 adults) and a bed is not guaranteed; 211 notes that no ID or other documents are required for intake. TCAA's services-site front desk is (480) 422-8922, ext. 5882.

**Important Information to Know**
There is no fee to stay in I-HELP (211 lists fees as none). Demand is high — TCAA reports more than 1,000 adults and children experiencing homelessness in Tempe at any given time, while I-HELP assists up to about 40 people at a time — so call early and have a backup plan. TCAA's website lists separate I-HELP shelters in Mesa and Chandler run by other organizations (the Mesa program is for adult women), so if you are in Mesa, ask TCAA which program fits your situation. Host-site schedules, intake rules and bed availability can change; treat this listing as a lead to verify by phone, not a guaranteed place.

website: https://tempeaction.org/get-help/housing-stability/

Chandler Salvation Army center offering emergency financial help that can include short-term rent and utility/energy assistance, plus food boxes and a cooling/heat-relief site; help is limited to the Chandler, Gilbert or Sun Lakes area, funding is not guaranteed, and requests are handled by phone message.

**Important Information to Know**
There is no fee to apply. Do not promise a motel or shelter voucher through this location: current Salvation Army and 211 listings show rent/utility assistance, food boxes and heat relief, not shelter or motel vouchers — ask when you call in case programs have changed. Assistance is limited and allocated first-come; even eligible households may be turned away when funds run out. Hours and services can change seasonally — verify by phone before going.

VA Community Resource & Referral Center (CRRC) – Phoenix (veteran housing and coordinated entry)

b12b-va-crrc · usable

VA one-stop center in Phoenix for veterans who are homeless or at risk of homelessness: housing assessments and coordinated-entry referrals for transitional and permanent housing and eviction prevention, plus health care, employment help, benefits and community referrals; walk-in, no referral required, free of charge.

**Services Offered**
A VA one-stop center for veterans who are homeless or at risk of homelessness. Staff complete housing assessments and refer veterans to transitional housing, permanent housing and eviction-prevention programs, and place eligible veterans on the community-wide housing priority list — this is the veterans' coordinated-entry site for the Phoenix area. The center also helps with immediate food and shelter needs, job training and employment referrals, justice-system navigation and community reentry from jail, financial support to prevent homelessness, addiction and depression treatment, and health and dental care. The National Call Center for Homeless Veterans, 877-424-3838 (877-4AID-VET), answers 24/7.

**Eligibility Requirements**
Veterans who are homeless or at risk of becoming homeless — including veterans transitioning from jail. No referral is required, there is no cost, and you can walk in with or without an appointment. Bring identification and any military/VA paperwork you have; some on-site health services require VA eligibility or enrollment in a VA homeless program.

**Population Served**
Veterans and veteran families experiencing homelessness or housing instability in Maricopa County, including veterans leaving jail or prison.

**How to Best Connect**
Walk in or call: 1500 E. Thomas Rd., Suite 106, Phoenix, AZ 85014; phone 602-248-6040; email VHAPHOCRRCStaff@va.gov. Open Monday–Friday, 7:30 a.m.–4 p.m., closed federal holidays. It is a walk-in clinic, so arrive as early in the day as possible — same-day referrals, including to transitional housing, may not be possible if you arrive late in the afternoon; if that happens, staff can still provide community shelter referrals. After hours, call 877-424-3838.

**Important Information to Know**
The center is in central Phoenix — plan your transportation from Mesa. No referral is needed to walk in and ask for help, and services are free of charge. A housing assessment is not an immediate placement: referrals depend on program capacity and the community priority list, so timelines vary. Veterans who also need health, mental-health, benefits or employment help can often start those services during the same visit.

phone: 602-248-6040; 877-424-3838 (24/7 National Call Center for Homeless Veterans)

address: 1500 E. Thomas Rd., Suite 106, Phoenix, AZ 85014

website: https://www.va.gov/phoenix-health-care/health-services/homeless-veteran-care/

hours: Mon–Fri 7:30 a.m.–4 p.m., excluding federal holidays; walk-in clinic — arrive early for same-day referrals.

resolutionReason: Retained as usable: VA pages confirm the CRRC as the Phoenix veterans' one-stop/coordinated-entry site with walk-in access, no referral required, no cost, Mon–Fri 7:30 a.m.–4 p.m. hours, phone 602-248-6040, email and housing referral scope (transitional, permanent, eviction prevention); corroborated by Maricopa County and 211 records including the 24/7 national line. No unresolved identity, geography or access conflict.

HUD-VASH (HUD-VA Supportive Housing) — VA Phoenix Health Care System

b13a-hud-vash-va-phoenix · usable

HUD-VASH pairs a HUD Housing Choice Voucher (rental assistance for a private-market apartment or home) with VA case management and supportive services so veterans experiencing homelessness can move into and stay in permanent housing. Veterans connect through VA — the Phoenix VA Community Resource and Referral Center (602-248-6040, walk-in) or the National Call Center for Homeless Veterans (877-424-3838, 24/7).

**Services Offered**
Permanent supportive housing for veterans: a HUD Housing Choice Voucher that pays part of the rent for a qualifying privately owned apartment or house, paired with ongoing VA case management and clinical support — including connection to primary care, mental health care and substance-use treatment, help with budgeting and leases, and long-term support to remain housed. Vouchers are administered locally by public housing authorities in partnership with VA, and the Phoenix VA homeless programs team handles referrals for Mesa and Maricopa County veterans.

**Eligibility Requirements**
For veterans who are experiencing homelessness and need ongoing, intensive case management (for example, due to chronic health conditions or other complex challenges). Veterans must be eligible for VA health care and meet HUD-VASH income limits — as of HUD's 2024 policy change, initial income eligibility is set at the Low level (80% of area median income) for all housing agencies, and service-connected disability benefits are not counted when determining income eligibility. A referral through VA is required; veterans cannot get a HUD-VASH voucher directly from a housing authority. Veterans who do not need intensive case management are usually served through other VA or community programs.

**Population Served**
Veterans — including veterans with families — who are experiencing homelessness in the Phoenix VA catchment area, which includes Mesa and Maricopa County.

**How to Best Connect**
Call or walk in to the Phoenix VA Community Resource and Referral Center (CRRC): 1500 E. Thomas Rd., Suite 106, Phoenix, AZ 85014; phone 602-248-6040; email VHAPHOCRRCStaff@va.gov. Hours are Monday–Friday 7:30 a.m.–4:00 p.m., excluding federal holidays. It is a walk-in clinic, so arrive as early as possible for a same-day referral (very late arrivals may instead receive a community shelter referral). Veterans can also call the National Call Center for Homeless Veterans at 877-424-3838 (free, confidential, 24/7) or ask their VA primary care team or social worker for a HUD-VASH referral.

**Important Information to Know**
Cost: the voucher pays the portion of rent above what the veteran contributes — generally about 30% of adjusted monthly income; utility allowances can adjust that amount. A referral does not guarantee a voucher, an available unit, or a timeline — voucher and case-manager capacity are limited, and the veteran must find a landlord who accepts the voucher; ask about current local availability when you call. HUD-VASH is separate from the regular Section 8 waiting list (Phoenix's regular Housing Choice Voucher list is currently closed); the City of Phoenix Housing Department administers HUD-VASH vouchers with the VA (about 903 vouchers awarded), and referrals still come through the VA, not the city.

phone: 602-248-6040 (Phoenix VA Community Resource & Referral Center); 877-424-3838 (National Call Center for Homeless Veterans, 24/7)

address: 1500 E. Thomas Rd., Suite 106, Phoenix, AZ 85014 (Phoenix VA Community Resource & Referral Center — walk-in veteran access point)

website: https://department.va.gov/homeless/hud-vash/

hours: Walk-in access at the Phoenix VA Community Resource and Referral Center: Monday–Friday 7:30 a.m.–4:00 p.m. (excluding federal holidays); arrive early for same-day referrals. The National Call Center for Homeless Veterans phone line is answered 24/7.

resolutionReason: Identity and program mechanics confirmed on current VA and City of Phoenix pages; a practical local walk-in referral route (Phoenix VA CRRC with phone, email and hours) plus a 24/7 national line is supported. Voucher availability, local case-manager capacity and wait times remain unknown and are stated for the client; they do not block a responsible referral.

Sources: HUD-VASH — VA Homeless Programs (program overview, voucher plus case management, national contact) · Homeless Veteran care — VA Phoenix health care (CRRC walk-in access, phone, email, hours) · Phoenix VA Clinic — Community Resource & Referral Center (address, hours, walk-in homeless care) · VA: Understanding the Policy Change that Increased Access to HUD-VASH (Low 80% AMI income eligibility; disability benefits excluded) · City of Phoenix Section 8 Housing Choice Voucher — Housing Choice Vouchers for Veterans (HUD-VASH referrals through the VA medical center)

Consider this: Adds the voucher-plus-case-management program behind the starter CRRC referral - explains what veterans can be referred into; capacity is limited.

Identity judgment: Voucher program; distinct from the CRRC referral point.

U.S.VETS Phoenix — veteran housing and supportive services

b13a-usvets-phoenix · usable

U.S.VETS Phoenix provides housing and wraparound services for veterans in Maricopa County: transitional housing for veterans experiencing homelessness, on-site permanent supportive housing, and SSVF help (rapid re-housing, rent and security-deposit assistance, eviction prevention) for very low-income veteran families — plus mental health counseling, career services and case management. Veterans seeking housing should contact the outreach team.

**Services Offered**
Transitional housing for veterans experiencing homelessness with wraparound support (case management, life skills, counseling, career training, VA benefits assistance); on-site permanent supportive housing where veterans can take over the lease; SSVF (Supportive Services for Veteran Families) rapid re-housing and homelessness-prevention help, including rent payments, security deposits, moving costs and transportation; free mental health and substance-use counseling; employment and career services; aftercare case management after a veteran moves into permanent housing. Housing campuses include the M.D. Hawkins Veterans Center (opened 2026, with a dedicated wing for women veterans) and Ashley's Place, a transitional housing program for women veterans.

**Eligibility Requirements**
Veteran who served in the active military, naval service or Coast Guard with a discharge other than dishonorable; must be homeless or at imminent risk of losing housing; very low income for most services. Permanent housing programming is aimed at veterans who can live independently and are economically self-sufficient. Ashley's Place serves women veterans who experienced military sexual trauma, intimate partner violence or domestic violence during or after service and who can live independently in a shared housing environment; eligibility screening applies. Typical documents: photo ID, DD Form 214 (or letter of service) showing discharge status, recent proof of household income, proof of homelessness (or current lease and eviction notice if housed), and Social Security cards for household members.

**Population Served**
Veterans in Maricopa County, including women veterans and veteran families. U.S.VETS Phoenix reports it supports more than 300 veterans experiencing homelessness every day and has served more than 10,000 veterans since opening in 2001.

**How to Best Connect**
For transitional housing, call the outreach team at 602-757-9723 or 602-859-7329. For SSVF and family housing help, call 602-441-0612. Main line: 602-717-6682. Offices: 3507 N. Central Ave., Suite 200, Phoenix, AZ 85012; residential campus: M.D. Hawkins Veterans Center, 12027 N. 28th Dr., Phoenix, AZ 85029. Calling, emailing or visiting the office starts the process; veterans working with VA can ask their VA case manager to refer them. Hours: SSVF Monday–Friday 7 a.m.–4 p.m. and by appointment; aftercare 9 a.m.–5 p.m.; residential site open 24/7; case management by appointment.

**Important Information to Know**
Most services are free; transitional housing fees vary. Program openings, beds and screening outcomes are limited — call ahead to confirm current availability, and do not count on immediate placement. Some programs serve specific groups (Ashley's Place: women veterans; SSVF: very low-income veteran households). Hours and program details differ by service and can change; confirm by phone before traveling.

phone: 602-717-6682 (main); 602-757-9723 or 602-859-7329 (housing outreach team); 602-441-0612 (Supportive Services for Veteran Families)

address: 3507 N. Central Ave., Suite 200, Phoenix, AZ 85012 (offices); residential campus: M.D. Hawkins Veterans Center, 12027 N. 28th Dr., Phoenix, AZ 85029

website: https://usvets.org/locations/phoenix/

hours: Supportive Services for Veteran Families: Monday–Friday 7 a.m.–4 p.m. and by appointment; aftercare: 9 a.m.–5 p.m.; residential campus open 24/7; case management by appointment. Call before visiting — hours vary by program.

Family Housing Hub — family coordinated entry and shelter referrals (UMOM New Day Centers)

b13a-umom-family-housing-hub · usable

The Family Housing Hub is the single front door for families with children under 18 who are homeless or at risk of homelessness in Maricopa County. Families call for an assessment, a housing plan, diversion help, and referral to family emergency shelter and housing programs — including rapid re-housing, transitional and permanent supportive housing. Mesa families can use this route; 2-1-1 is the recommended first call.

**Services Offered**
Coordinated entry and assessment for families with children under 18 who are experiencing or at risk of homelessness in Maricopa County: connection to family emergency shelter (including placement on the family shelter waiting list), diversion help that can resolve a housing crisis without entering shelter when possible, a short- and long-term housing plan, and referrals to rapid re-housing, transitional housing and permanent supportive housing programs. The Hub also connects families to other community resources (food, benefits, employment, health care) and serves families that include a military veteran.

**Eligibility Requirements**
Families with children under 18 who are homeless or at risk of homelessness. Families should call (602) 595-8700 before going in person, and all adults 18+ in the household are required to take part in the call or visit. An assessment does not guarantee shelter or a housing voucher — referrals depend on eligibility and limited availability.

**Population Served**
Families with dependent children under 18 in Maricopa County, including Mesa. Help is available in English and Spanish.

**How to Best Connect**
Call (602) 595-8700, or dial 2-1-1 (877-211-8661). UMOM recommends calling 2-1-1 or checking 211arizona.org before visiting to get matched with the best resources for your family, including options that may prevent homelessness. Location: 3307 E. Van Buren St., Phoenix, AZ 85008 (near the UMOM campus); program website: fhhub.org. Families already on the list use the Hub's check-in number for weekly check-ins. Confirm hours by phone before traveling — published hours differ between sources.

**Important Information to Know**
There is no fee. Wait times are the main challenge: in September 2026, more than 200 families were on the family shelter waitlist with waits described as about three months (Mesa has cited 2–4 month coordinated-entry waits); ask about diversion, rapid re-housing referrals and 2-1-1 options while you wait. Earlier community guides listed Mesa-area entry points (Mesa CAN at 635 E. Broadway and Save the Family at 125 E. University Dr.) on certain weekdays, but later notices said the East Valley and West Valley sites were closed and families should call the main line — confirm current options by phone. If the fhhub.org website does not load, call (602) 595-8700 directly.

phone: 602-595-8700; 2-1-1 (or 877-211-8661)

address: 3307 E. Van Buren St., Phoenix, AZ 85008 (call before visiting)

website: https://fhhub.org/

hours: Call to confirm — posted hours differ between sources: a current 211 Arizona listing shows Monday 1–6 p.m. and Tuesday–Friday 10 a.m.–3 p.m.; earlier notices list Monday 8 a.m.–7 p.m., Tuesday–Thursday 8 a.m.–4 p.m. and Friday 10 a.m.–4 p.m.

resolutionReason: Coordinated-entry role, eligibility, fees, service area and the contact route (602-595-8700 or 2-1-1) are supported by a current 211 record and UMOM's help page. Posted hours conflict between sources and wait times are long; both are flagged with call-first guidance and a 2-1-1 backup so a responsible referral can proceed.

Sources: 211 Arizona: Housing Related Coordinated Entry — UMOM Family Housing Hub (eligibility, hours, application process, fees) · UMOM New Day Centers — Find Help (Family Housing Hub single point of entry; call 2-1-1 before visiting) · Maricopa Regional Continuum of Care Homelessness Resources (call FHH main line; East/West Valley sites closed) · FOX 10 via Yahoo News, Sept 19, 2026: family shelter waitlist of 200+ families described as about three months

UMOM Summer Family Heat Relief — heat respite and hotel stays for families (City of Phoenix partnership)

b13a-umom-family-heat-relief · usable

A seasonal, referral-based heat-relief program run by UMOM with the City of Phoenix for families with minor children experiencing homelessness: daytime and overnight shelter (including indoor tent and cot space) on UMOM's Phoenix campus, plus hotel stays used as overflow when respite space is full. Families get help through the family homeless-services system — call 2-1-1 or the Family Housing Hub (602-595-8700).

**Services Offered**
Heat-relief respite for families with minor children experiencing homelessness: a 24/7 space with daytime and overnight shelter (including cots in indoor tents — 16 tents were added as the site filled up), meals and staff support, operated on UMOM's Phoenix campus during the extreme-heat season. UMOM also assesses each family for diversion and shelters those who cannot be diverted, and provides hotel stays as overflow when respite capacity is full. By late August 2026, UMOM reported serving 757 people, including 449 children (data as of Aug. 25), with expectations of more than 1,000 people served before the season ended; the city reported more than twice as many families served this year as in all of summer 2025.

**Eligibility Requirements**
Families with minor children who are experiencing homelessness. Access is by referral — the City of Phoenix contract specifies referrals from other heat relief sites and City of Phoenix outreach workers; there is no public application or walk-up intake for the hotel stays. Families should be assessed through the family homeless-services system (call 2-1-1 or the Family Housing Hub).

**Population Served**
Families with minor children experiencing homelessness in Phoenix and the surrounding Maricopa County area. UMOM is the region's family coordinated entry provider and the state's largest family shelter provider.

**How to Best Connect**
There is no direct intake line for this program — referrals come through heat relief sites and City of Phoenix outreach teams, and UMOM's own guidance for families in a housing crisis is to call 2-1-1. Families with children can also call the Family Housing Hub at 602-595-8700 for assessment and shelter referral. UMOM main line: 602-275-7852; campus: 3333 E. Van Buren St., Phoenix, AZ 85008. Ask specifically about summer heat relief and family shelter availability when you call.

**Important Information to Know**
This is a seasonal program tied to the heat season, and 2026 availability is time-limited — the City of Phoenix contract runs through October 31, 2026, and UMOM reported extending heat relief through the end of October 2026. Capacity has been under heavy strain: in September 2026, some families with hotel stays were reportedly told to check out earlier than planned — ahead of a reported Sept. 30 end date for the hotel portion — while UMOM looked into the cause, so treat hotel availability as limited and confirm at the time of need. The service is city-funded; no family fees are described in the contract. Outside the heat season, use 2-1-1 and the Family Housing Hub for year-round family shelter and housing help.

phone: 602-275-7852 (UMOM main); 2-1-1 for family housing and shelter referrals; 602-595-8700 (Family Housing Hub)

address: UMOM campus, 3333 E. Van Buren St., Phoenix, AZ 85008 (access by referral; not a public walk-in intake)

website: https://www.umom.org/

hours: Seasonal heat-season program; the 2026 City of Phoenix contract runs through October 31, 2026, and UMOM reported extending heat relief through the end of October 2026. The heat relief site operated 24/7 (daytime and overnight) during the season. No direct public intake — access is by referral.

resolutionReason: Program existence, operator, city contract term, service scope and referral-based access are supported by a City of Phoenix ordinance record, UMOM's own communications and September 2026 reporting. There is no direct public intake and hotel availability proved inconsistent in mid-September 2026; both are flagged and families are routed through 2-1-1 or the Family Housing Hub, so the lead remains actionable with those warnings. Reviewed 2026-09-28: served-count updated to UMOM's Aug. 25 data (757 people / 449 children), the 16-tent detail and the reported Sept. 30 hotel-voucher end date added; heat relief through the end of October re-verified.

Sources: City of Phoenix File #26-1767, Ordinance S-53179 (adopted 9/9/2026): amend UMOM heat-respite contract; hotel vouchers as overflow; term ends Oct 31, 2026 · UMOM: Summer Family Heat Relief program sees surging demand for 24/7 summer heat shelter · UMOM: Collaboration in Crisis (summer heat-relief initiative — daytime and overnight shelter on UMOM's Phoenix campus) · 12News via Yahoo News, Sept 4, 2026: Phoenix family shelter adds tents (16 tents with cots); heat relief extended through end of October; about 200 families waiting via Family Housing Hub · FOX 10 via Yahoo News, Sept 19, 2026: hotel voucher program, early checkouts, shelter waitlists

Consider this: Adds seasonal heat-relief and hotel overflow for families - summer-specific, access by referral, and hotel capacity has been inconsistent.

UMOM Family Emergency Shelter (New Day Center)

b13b-umom-family-shelter · usable

UMOM's New Day Center family emergency shelter provides safe temporary shelter and intensive support for households with children experiencing homelessness in Maricopa County. Families are screened through the UMOM-operated Family Housing Hub, the countywide coordinated entry point for families; the campus provides private rooms, meals, case management, employment services, children's programs and help moving to permanent housing.

**Services Offered**

Emergency shelter on the UMOM New Day Center campus for households with children. Families receive private rooms; fresh meals served 7 days a week; one-on-one case management; employment services and job training (including UMOM's Homegrown culinary and warehouse/logistics tracks); help connecting to benefits such as WIC and unemployment; advocacy with property managers, landlords and courts; connections to community resources; help finding permanent housing, including housing vouchers when available; rapid rehousing and permanent affordable/supportive housing pathways after shelter; on-site children's services, including an on-site Boys & Girls Club and a children's health clinic run by Phoenix Children's Hospital; adult health care through partners (Health Care for the Homeless and Circle the City); and daily school bus service that keeps children in their home school.

**Eligibility Requirements**

Families with dependent children (children under 18) who are experiencing homelessness in Maricopa County. Families are screened, prioritized and placed on the shelter waitlist through the Family Housing Hub, the single countywide coordinated entry point for families, rather than applying directly at the shelter. Families that include a veteran can also access support through the Family Housing Hub on the UMOM campus.

**Population Served**

Families with children experiencing homelessness in Maricopa County, including larger families (a 2022-2024 county-funded expansion targeted families averaging six members) and families with non-traditional heads of household. UMOM reports serving about 75% of families in Maricopa County shelters and sheltering roughly 155 families (about 800 people) on campus each night.

**How to Best Connect**

Start with the Family Housing Hub: call or text 2-1-1, or call 602-595-8700 (also 877-211-8661); online at fhhub.org. UMOM asks families to call 2-1-1 or visit 211arizona.org before going in person so they are matched with the best options, which may include help to avoid shelter. The Family Housing Hub is at 3307 E. Van Buren St. #108, Phoenix, AZ 85008, next to the UMOM campus at 3333 E. Van Buren St.; intake is handled by phone and English/Spanish are available there. UMOM's main line is 602-275-7852. Confirm current hours and any in-person arrangements before sending a family.

**Important Information to Know**

No fees are charged (211 lists no fees for the shelter or the Hub). Placement is not immediate: UMOM manages the single countywide family shelter waitlist and reported in mid-2026 that more than 200 families were experiencing homelessness and in need of shelter; capacity and waitlist status change daily. Length of stay varies - recent UMOM participant materials describe a short-term emergency shelter stay (30 days) focused on diversion and housing search, while older materials cite stays of up to 120 days - so confirm current terms at intake. Shelter is a step toward permanent housing; a 2024 county case study reported 802 individuals moved into permanent housing from the program that year.

phone: 602-275-7852

address: 3333 E. Van Buren St., Phoenix, AZ 85008

website: https://www.umom.org/shelter

hours: Family shelter intake is by phone through the Family Housing Hub: call 2-1-1 or 602-595-8700 (a current 211 listing shows Hub office hours Mon 1-6 p.m. and Tue-Fri 10 a.m.-3 p.m.; earlier listings differ, so confirm by phone). UMOM's main line is staffed Mon-Fri 8 a.m.-5 p.m.; a city resource listing also describes the UMOM campus as open daily 8 a.m.-5 p.m. for resource access, with intake/waitlist handled by phone.

resolutionReason: Usable: UMOM's identity, its family emergency shelter service in Maricopa County, and the Family Housing Hub intake route are supported by current UMOM, county and 211 sources. Waitlist status, capacity and length-of-stay details change and must be confirmed at intake; no place or eligibility is guaranteed.
